# 1773 Pochaiv Lavra General Menaion / Mineia Zahalna (Мінея Загальна, Почаїв: Друкарня Почаївської Лаври, 1773) <a id="1773-pochaiv-lavra-general-menaion-mineia-zahalna"></a>

*(Binding — Upper outer front cover: Worn brown calfskin binding over pasteboard with diagonal blind tooling and scuffed edges; no printed or manuscript text present).*

*FRONT MATTER, TITLE PAGE, AND BLESSING:*

*(Inside Front Cover — Flyleaf / Paste-down: Archival library notations, nineteenth-century shelfmarks, inventory classifications, and typographical stamp from Przemyśl)*[^1]

**GENERAL MENAION**  
that is:  
**General Services to single and multiple Saints,**  
**sung according to the Typikon.**  
**PRINTED**  
**By the blessing of His Excellency, the Most Reverend Kyr**  
**SYLVESTER LUBIENIECKI-RUDNICKI,**  
**Exarch of all Rus’, Bishop of Lutsk and Ostroh,**[^2]  
**In the Holy Miracle-Working Lavra of Pochaiv,**  
**of the Order of Saint Basil the Great.**[^3]  
**In the year from the creation of the world according to the Greek chronographers, 7281;**  
**and from the Nativity of Christ, 1773.**[^4]

*Table of Contents of the items found in this book:*[^5]  
Common Service to a Single Prophet: Folio 1.  
Common Service to a Single Apostle: Folio 7 verso.  
Common Service to Two and Many Apostles: Folio 13 verso.  
Common Service to a Single Hierarch: Folio 20.

Common Service to Two and Many Venerable Fathers: Folio 34.  
Common Service to a Single Martyr: Folio 44 verso.  
Common Service to Two and Many Martyrs: Folio 51.  
Common Service to a Single Hieromartyr: Folio 57.  
Common Service to Two and Many Hieromartyrs: Folio 63.  
Common Service to a Single Venerable-Martyr: Folio 64 verso.  
Common Service to Two and Many Venerable-Martyrs: Folio 75 verso.  
Common Service to a Single Woman-Martyr: Folio 81 verso.  
Common Service to Two and Many Women-Martyrs: Folio 87 verso.  
Common Service to a Single Venerable Woman: Folio 93 verso.  
Common Service to Two and Many Venerable Women: Folio 94 verso.  
Common Service to a Single Venerable-Woman-Martyr: Folio 105 verso.

Resurrectional and Common Theotokia of the Eight Tones: Folio 232 verso.

# PART I: COMMON SERVICES TO THE SAINTS

## Common Service to a Single Prophet

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^6]

*Sticheron 1:*  
Having received the ray of the most divine radiance through purity of mind, and having become a herald and signifier of the divine words and a divine prophet: thou didst appear as the God-moved mouth of the Spirit, proclaiming and making known unto all the nations the salvation that is given and the coming Kingdom of Christ, O [Name];[^7] Him do thou entreat, to save and enlighten our souls.

*Sticheron 2:*  
Radiant worthily with the vision of God and prophetic preeminence, and honored with grace, O God-inspired [Name], and accounted worthy of divine nearness: now in thy boldness toward the All-Good One and sustained by His mercy, pray unceasingly for those who in faith praise thee and honor thee as one who speaks of God, honorable and God-pleasing, that we may be delivered from tribulations and that our souls may be saved.

*Sticheron 3:*  
Thou hast shown Thy prophet, O [Name], as a living cloud, O Immortal One, pouring forth water unto life that is truly eternal, having sent him richly, and having granted the All-Holy Spirit, Who is consubstantial with Thee, the Father Almighty, and with Thy Son Who shone forth from Thy essence: through Whom he foretold the saving the saving advent of Christ our God, and to proclaim salvation unto all nations.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
For I have fallen into the depth of many transgressions from my laziness, wretched and despairing because of despondency and ignorance, and now I am held fast by despair. Be unto me help and cleansing and salvation, O most pure one, granting me timely consolation. I entreat thee, and unto thee, O beloved Virgin, do I fall down, crying unto thee with faith, lest I become entirely the prey and rejoice the deceiver.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^8]  
Weep not for Me, O Mother, beholding upon the Tree hanging Thy Son and God, Who without support hung the earth upon the waters and created all creation; for I will arise and be glorified, and will crush the kingdoms of Hades with power and destroy his might, and deliver those who are bound from his malice, as I am tender-hearted; and I will bring them unto My Father, as I am the Lover of mankind.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: On "Lord, I have cried," stichera on 6.*  
*Glory... Tone 6:*  
O prophet and herald of Christ, thou never departest from the Throne of majesty, and thou ever standest before each one who calls upon thee; serving in the highest, thou blessest the universe, being glorified everywhere: ask cleansing for our souls.

*Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
Who will not call thee blessed, O most holy Virgin?

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and the Readings:*  
*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wisdom 3:1–9):*[^9]  
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was taken for misery, and their going from us to be utter destruction; but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality. And having been a little chastened, they shall be greatly rewarded; for God proved them, and found them worthy for Himself. As gold in the furnace has He tried them, and received them as a burnt offering sacrifice. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and run to and fro like sparks among the stubble. They shall judge the nations, and have dominion over the people, and their Lord shall reign forever. They that put their trust in Him shall understand the truth; and such as be faithful in love shall abide with Him; for grace and mercy is to His saints, and He has care for His elect.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wisdom 5:15–6:3):*[^10]  
The righteous live unto ages, and their reward is in the Lord, and the care of them is with the Most High. Therefore shall they receive a kingdom of glory, and a crown of beauty from the hand of the Lord; for with His right hand shall He cover them, and with His arm shall He protect them. He shall take to Him His jealousy for complete armor, and make the creature His weapon for the revenge of His enemies. He shall put on righteousness as a breastplate, and true judgment instead of a helmet. He shall take holiness for an invincible shield. His severe wrath shall He sharpen for a sword, and the world shall fight with Him against the unwise. Then shall the right-aiming thunderbolts go abroad, and from the clouds, as from a well-drawn bow, shall they fly to the mark. And hailstones full of wrath shall be cast as out of a stone bow, and the water of the sea shall rage against them, and the floods shall cruelly drown them. Yea, a mighty wind shall stand up against them, and like a storm shall blow them away: thus iniquity shall lay waste the whole earth, and ill dealing shall overthrow the thrones of the mighty. Hear therefore, O ye kings, and understand; learn, ye that be judges of the ends of the earth. Give ear, ye that rule the people, and glory in the multitude of nations. For power is given you from the Lord, and sovereignty from the Highest.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wisdom 4:7–15):*[^11]  
Though the righteous man happen to die early, he shall be in rest. For honorable age is not that which stands in length of time, nor that is measured by number of years. But wisdom is the gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is old age. He pleased God, and was beloved of Him; so that living among sinners he was translated. Yea, speedily was he taken away, lest wickedness should alter his understanding, or deceit beguile his soul. For the bewitching of naughtiness obscures things that are honest, and the wandering of concupiscence undermines the simple mind. He, being made perfect in a short time, fulfilled a long time; for his soul pleased the Lord: therefore He hastened to take him away from among the wicked. This the people saw, and understood it not, neither laid they up this in their minds, that His grace and mercy is with His saints, and that He has respect unto His chosen.

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 2.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^12]

*Sticheron 1:*  
The spiritual ray found the purity of thy mind shining like a mirror, O prophet [Name], and manifestly illuminated the world with the radiance of divine knowledge, prefiguring the types of the divine mysteries, and the grace that is about to be granted unto all mankind, O most marvelous one.

*Verse: Thou art a priest forever after the order of Melchizedek (Ps. 109:4).*

*Sticheron 2:*  
Having become the mouth of God, thou didst openly rebuke the workers of impiety, condemning them by inevitable judgment, O most rich [Name], following righteous teaching and divine judgments: therefore attending to the elevation of thy most wise words, O blessed one, we worthily praise thee with hymns.

*Verse: Moses and Aaron among His priests, and Samuel among them that call upon His name (Ps. 98:6).*

*Sticheron 3:*  
Ever standing before the Throne and being filled with ineffable and divine Glory, beholding transcendent beauties and being filled with light, O prophet [Name], speaker of God: remember all who honor thee in faith, asking for them spiritual salvation and remission of sins for all, O God-pleasing one.

*Glory... Tone 4:*  
Those divine properties and essential attributes wherein God is prefigured, the glorious and honorable prophets secondarily partake of by gift, through communion and grace, as the Lord enlightens His favored ones by His own radiance.

*Now and ever... Resurrectional Theotokion:*  
Behold, the prophecy of Isaiah has been fulfilled...

*Or if thou celebratest not, say this Theotokion:*  
Joy of the heavenly hosts, mighty protection of men on earth, O most pure Virgin: save us who run to thee, for after God we have placed our hope in thee, O Theotokos.

*Cross-Theotokion:*  
A sword has pierced, O Son, the Virgin said when she saw Thee hung upon the Tree; my heart is torn asunder, O Master, even as Simeon foretold of old: but arise and glorify Thy Mother and handmaid, O Immortal One, I pray.

*Troparion in the Typikon.*  
*Or if there is no Typikon rubric, say this Troparion, Tone 2:*  
Celebrating the memory of Thy prophet, O [Name], O Lord, therefore we pray Thee: save our souls.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion, in the Tone of the troparion.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the same Troparion.*  
*After the First Stichologia, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3.*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "The beauty of virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*[^13]

*Sessional Hymn 1:*  
Thou didst prefigure the Incarnation of the Word, being illuminated by the divine Spirit, O great one among the divine prophets, O [Name], God-inspired: therefore as a prophet we sacredly call thee blessed, and celebrate thy memory today, crying unto thee with one accord, O wise one: Pray unto Christ to save our souls. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
Inconceivable and incomprehensible, O sovereign Lady full of divine grace, is the fearful mystery of God accomplished in thee: for having conceived the Uncontainable One, thou didst bear Him clothed in flesh from thy most pure blood; Him do thou ever entreat as thy Son, O pure one, that our souls may be saved.

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3.*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры):*[^14]

*Sessional Hymn 2:*  
A divine instrument of the Comforter, ever tuned clearly by His grace, O blessed one, thou wast a prophet; for thou proclaimest the manifestation of hidden things, and dost illuminate with faith those who run who run with faith unto thee, O glorious [Name]: pray unto Christ God to grant us great mercy. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
The prophets proclaimed, the apostles taught, the martyrs confessed, and we have believed that thou art truly the Theotokos: therefore we magnify thy ineffable childbearing.

*At the Polyeleos, Megalynarion:*[^15]  
We magnify thee, O prophet of God, [Name], and we honor thy holy memory: for thou prayest for us unto Christ our God.

*Psalm 111: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord:*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 6.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "The gates of compassion" (Милосе́рдїя две́ри):*[^16]  
Having cleansed thy mind of defilements, O all-wise one, thou didst make it a divine mirror, receptive to the ray of the divine Spirit: and now rejoicing, thou hast departed unto the fountain of radiances, O prophet [Name]. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
Him Who before the ages was begotten of the Father without a mother, the Son and Word of God, thou didst bear in the last times, incarnate of thy pure blood without a man, O Mother of God: Him do thou entreat, that remission of sins be granted us before the end.

*The Graduals: the First Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*  
Thou art a priest forever after the order of Melchizedek (Ps. 109:4).  
*Verse: The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand (Ps. 109:1).*  
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*  
*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 96 (Mt. 23:34–39).*

*After Psalm 50, Glory:*  
Through the intercessions of the prophet, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our transgressions.

*Now and ever:*  
Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, O Merciful One, cleanse the multitude of our transgressions.

*Then: Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy, and according to the multitude of Thy compassions blot out my transgression (Ps. 50:1).*

*Sticheron, Tone 6:*  
O prophet and herald of Christ: *(It is written at "Lord, I have cried," at Glory).*

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos:*  
*Canon of the Theotokos to 4; and of the Saint to 8.*  
*Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^17]  
When Israel traversed the deep on foot as on dry land, beholding Pharaoh their pursuer drowning, they cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparion 1:*  
We pray thee, O prophet, as thou standest before the Throne of God, to pray diligently: that illumination be granted unto us who sing thy honorable memory in faith.

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou didst appear as a divine instrument, receptive to the illuminations and gifts of the divine Spirit, O all-wise [Name]: therefore rejoicing, we the faithful call thee blessed.

*Troparion 3:*  
Standing prophetically before God the Savior, pray now with boldness: to enlighten with radiant rays those who in faith bless thee, O God-inspired one.

*Theotokion:*  
With most radiant beams the Sun that shone forth from thy holy womb illuminates the whole earth, O sovereign Lady; and enlightened thereby, we honor thee as the Mother of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^18]  
There is none holy like Thee, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparion 1:*  
He Who sees all things as God, the knowledge of things to come and of those about to be: He manifestly reveals that knowledge unto thee and fittingly displays it, O blessed and most marvelous one.

*Troparion 2:*  
Strengthening thy mind with pious boldness, O blessed one, thou didst bow thyself unto the divine Spirit: and thou wast and thou wast receptive of the divine inspirations that dwell there.

*Troparion 3:*  
A river of mystical waters, issuing from the abyss of the gifts of the Spirit, wast thou, O speaker of God.

*Theotokion:*  
All generations in faith bless thee, who in time didst supernaturally bear in the flesh the Timeless Word, and yet remained a Virgin.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 6.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "The gates of compassion" (Милосе́рдїя две́ри):*  
Having cleansed thy mind of defilements, O all-wise one, thou didst make it a divine mirror, receptive to the ray of the divine Spirit: and now rejoicing, thou hast departed unto the fountain of radiances, O prophet [Name].

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
The all-glorious Mother of God, holier than the holy angels, let us unceasingly praise with heart and mouth, confessing her as the Theotokos, who truly bore God incarnate, and who prays unceasingly for our souls.

*Cross-Theotokion:*  
Come, all ye people, let us sing of Him Who was crucified for our sake; for Mary saw Him upon the Tree and said: Even though Thou sufferest crucifixion, Thou art my Son and my God.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^19]  
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church divinely sings, crying out with a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparion 1:*  
The Lord of all appeared unto us, according to thy prophecy, O blessed one, calling all unto His knowledge and delivering them from bondage.

*Troparion 2:*  
Illuminated by grace, thou didst proclaim, saying: submitting to serve under one yoke, following faith, and serving the Lord.

*Troparion 3:*  
Being a prophet, ever illuminated by the rays of the Light-giver's radiance: enlighten us, O blessed one, who sing to thee with love.

*Theotokion:*  
O all-pure Mary, destroy the turmoil of passions in my thoughts, and the storm of tribulations, having borne the Fountain of dispassion, O Virgin Mother.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^20]  
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illuminate the souls of those who lovingly wake at dawn for Thee, I pray Thee, O Word of God, to know Thee, the true God, Who callest them out from the darkness of sin.

*Troparion 1:*  
From unrighteousness unto virtue, and from the captivity of passions turn me, O blessed one, by thy prayers, O glorious prophet, and direct me toward the light of pious works.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having enlightened thy soul with the fair graces of virtues, thou didst show it well-pleasing unto the radiances of the divine Spirit: by Whom also thou wast enriched with the grace of prophecy.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having become a divine prophet, leading a pure life, thou wast a receptacle of the Holy Spirit: therefore thou wast accounted worthy to behold Him Who is invisibly understood.

*Theotokion:*  
The prophetic utterances, O most pure one, proclaimed thy childbearing by many types: and beholding now their fulfillment, we declare thee to be truly the Theotokos.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^21]  
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, I run to Thy calm haven and cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly-Merciful One!

*Troparion 1:*  
Having received the ray of the Spirit, O glorious one, manifesting as a mirror of the divine radiance of prophecy, thou didst shine upon the world, prophesying things to come as though present.

*Troparion 2:*  
Thy King has come: rejoice, O Zion, and of fair appearance, and He enlightened the world with the splendors of His Godhead, and destroyed the demonic deception.

*Troparion 3:*  
By the power of the divine Spirit didst thou behold things to come, O prophet, receiving the types of divine manifestations as in a mirror by the brightness of thy soul with mortal flesh was united the Only-Begotten of the Father, and within thy womb, one of two nature passed through without corruption, preserving uninjured thy pure virginity, O all-immaculate one.

*Kontakion in the Typikon.*  
*Or if there is no Typikon rubric, say this Kontakion, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*[^22]  
Thy pure heart, enlightened by the Spirit, became the most radiant receptacle of prophecy; for thou seest things far off as though present: therefore we honor thee, O blessed prophet [Name], glorious one.

*Ikos:*  
Enriched with the grace of prophecy, Thou didst learn the understanding of things to come in a manner worthy of God through the Spirit, O [Name], God-inspired, and Thou foretellest unto all the advent of Christ, and unto the nations, O most rich one, their divine calling: therefore we celebrate thy honorable memory, and sing to thee with love, and faithfully call thee blessed, crying unto thee, O glorious one: Pray unceasingly unto Christ God for all of us.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^23]  
An angel made the furnace shed dew for the holy children, while the command of God consuming the Chaldeans convinced the tyrant to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:*  
Thy memory shone forth upon the world like the sun, O prophet, illuminating by thy grace of prophecy those who sing in concord and faith: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou didst appear as a most fertile cloud; thou hast drawn for us the rain of the knowledge of God from the saving fountains, O blessed one, enlightened wherewith we cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 3:*  
The prophets, illuminated by the Lord, foretell the knowledge of things to come; with divine wisdom they cry aloud the Word Who exists from the beginning, unto Whom we sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*  
The Word, Who of old gave existence unto all by His divine will, wishing to recall man, O pure one, dwelt within thy womb, O blessed one, who didst bear God in the flesh.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^24]  
From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew upon the holy ones, and didst consume with water the sacrifice of the righteous one: for Thou doest all things, O Christ, only by Thy willing; Thee do we highly exalt unto all ages!

*Troparion 1:*  
Dedicated wholly unto the Almighty, thou wast mystically taught the ways of providence, instructively teaching the nations: therefore we sing to thee, O Prophet, speaker of God.

*Troparion 2:*  
Offer supplication unto God for those who sing of thee, O divinely blessed one, and destroy the turmoil of temptations, that we may sing to thee, O Prophet, speaker of God.

*Troparion 3:*  
As a fire unto all sitting in darkness hast thou appeared, illuminating with divine light and godly wisdom those who faithfully sing of the Lord unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*  
Having given birth to God and the Lord, the Giver of life, thou hast stopped the unrestrained destruction of death, O most pure one, having put it to death: therefore we sing of thee unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^25]  
It is not possible for men to behold God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-immaculate one, the Word incarnate became visible unto men: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.

*Troparion 1:*  
In the land of the meek hast thou dwelt, where thou peacefully beholdest the angelic brightnesses, O [Name], all-wise Prophet of God, having become meek and shining with grace: therefore rejoicing, we the faithful call thee blessed.

*Troparion 2:*  
Brightly beholding the fulfillment that has taken place of thy prophecies, O most glorious one we marvel at the grace given unto thee, and we admire the purity of thy mind, and the God-beholding character of thy soul.

*Troparion 3:*  
Where the angelic ranks, and the choirs of the prophets and patriarchs, and the brightnesses of the saints dwell, there hast thou settled, O Prophet [Name]: and now rejoicing and making merry with them, pray unto the Lord for us, that we who praise thee in faith may be saved.

*Theotokion:*  
Like unto thee, no other woman was ever granted from generations of old, O all-immaculate Mother of God: for thou alone possessed incomparable holiness and purity above all; therefore thou didst receive God taking flesh within thee.

*Exapostilarion:*  
All the Prophets rejoice, being glad on thy joyful day, O divinely wise one, partaking of thy joy and of divine Glory; with them pray thou that those who sing of thee may be saved.

*Theotokion:*  
With the sprinkling of thy compassions, O pure Maiden, wash away the filth of my soul: and grant that I may unceasingly pour forth drops of tears, drying up the floods of my passions.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos:*  
*At the Praises, stichera, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^26]

*Sticheron 1:*  
The divine Spirit was poured forth, as the honorable [Name], moved by Him, proclaimed prophecies upon us believers, and reveals the manifestation of divine mysteries; and those who receive His working, being enlightened by the divine Dawn and by divine grace, prophesy. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 2:*  
Full of divine utterance, the marvelous [Name] goes forth like a fountain watering souls from Thy house, O Master, and drops sweetness upon us, making pleasant our thoughts: being wholly elevated in spirit, raised by virtue unto divine height.

*Sticheron 3:*  
By thy prophetic boldness and intelligent approach unto God, O [Name], whereby Thou beholdest heavenly visions, make Him merciful toward us who accomplish thy memory in faith, asking remission of transgressions, O glorious one, and communion in divine delights and great mercy.

*Glory... Tone 4:*  
Rejoicing in the Lord God Thy Savior, be glad, O glorious speaker of God: and having received the ray of the radiance that is there, and being intellectually illuminated by the divine light, deliver by Thy prayers from temptations and tribulations those who accomplish in faith Thy all-festive memory.

*Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
Deliver us from our necessities, O Mother of Christ God, who didst bear the Creator of all, that we all may cry unto thee: Rejoice, the only protection of our souls!

*At the Liturgy, Beatitudes from the Canon, Ode 3 and Ode 6.*  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*  
Thou art a priest forever after the order of Melchizedek (Ps. 109:4).  
*Verse: The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand, until I make Thine enemies the footstool of Thy feet (Ps. 109:1).*  
*Epistle to the Corinthians, pericope 156 (1 Cor. 12:7–11); or to the Hebrews, pericope 314 (Heb. 11:33–40); or of the General Epistle of James, pericope 57 (Jas. 5:10–20).*[^27]  
*Alleluia, Tone 5:*  
Moses and Aaron among His priests, and Samuel among them that call upon His name (Ps. 98:6).  
*Verse: Light is sprung up for the righteous, and gladness for the upright of heart (Ps. 96:11).*  
*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 96 (Mt. 23:34–39); or according to Luke, pericope 66 (Lk. 11:47–12:1).*  
*Communion Verse: The righteous shall be in everlasting remembrance (Ps. 111:6).*

## Common Service to a Single Apostle

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you" (Что́ вы нарече́мъ):*[^28]

*Sticheron 1:*  
What shall we call thee, O apostle? Heaven, for thou hast declared the glory of God; a river, for thou mystically waterest creation; a star, illuminating the Church; a chalice, pouring forth holy drink; a closest friend of Christ; an equal citizen with the bodiless hosts: pray for the salvation of our souls.

*Sticheron 2:*  
O glorious apostle and beholder of God, thy feet were made beautiful, walking well in the path of preaching, and confining all enemy paths by the breadth of divine knowledge, through the Word Who appeared in the grossness of flesh and chose thee as a most glorious disciple, O blessed one: Him do thou entreat, that our souls may be saved.

*Sticheron 3:*  
Truly, O apostle and speaker of God, thou wast sent forth as a radiant arrow from Christ, wounding enemies, but manifestly granting healing unto wounded souls: therefore as is due we call thee blessed, and accomplish thy holy celebration today: pray that our souls may be saved.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
Countless times, O most pure one, have I promised to make repentance for my sins, but the habitual love of my vices does not release me. Therefore I cry unto thee and fall down praying: do thou, O sovereign Lady, deliver me from such torment, guiding me unto better things that are close unto salvation.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^29]  
Beholding Thee upon the Tree, as a Lamb and Shepherd, the Lamb who bore Thee lamented, and maternally cried aloud unto Thee: O most longed-for Son, how art Thou hung upon the wood of the Cross, O Long-Suffering One? How have Thy hands and Thy feet, O Word, been nailed by the lawless, and how hast Thou poured forth Thy blood, O Master?

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: Glory... Tone 6:*  
Grace was poured forth in thy lips, O glorious apostle [Name], and thou didst become a lamp of the Church of Christ, teaching the noetic sheep to believe in the Consubstantial Trinity, in one Godhead.

*Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
Who will not call thee blessed, O most holy Virgin?

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the Readings:*  
*Reading from the General Epistle of John (1 John 3:21–4:6):*[^30]  
Beloved, if our heart condemn us not, then have we confidence toward God. And whatsoever we ask, we receive of Him, because we keep His commandments, and do those things that are pleasing in His sight. And this is His commandment, That we should believe on the name of His Son Jesus Christ, and love one another, as He gave us commandment. And he that keeps His commandments dwells in Him, and He in him. And hereby we know that He abides in us, by the Spirit which He has given us. Beloved, believe not every spirit, but try the spirits whether they are of God: because many false prophets are gone out into the world. Hereby know ye the Spirit of God: Every spirit that confesses that Jesus Christ is come in the flesh is of God: and every spirit that confesses not that Jesus Christ is come in the flesh is not of God: and this is that spirit of antichrist, whereof ye have heard that it should come; and even now already is it in the world. Ye are of God, little children, and have overcome them: because greater is He that is in you, than he that is in the world. They are of the world: therefore speak they of the world, and the world hears them. We are of God: he that knows God hears us; he that is not of God hears us not.

*Reading from the General Epistle of John (1 John 4:11–16):*[^31]  
Beloved, if God so loved us, we ought also to love one another. No man has seen God at any time. If we love one another, God dwells in us, and His love is perfected in us. Hereby know we that we dwell in Him, and He in us, because He has given us of His Spirit. And we have seen and do testify that the Father sent the Son to be the Savior of the world. Whosoever shall confess that Jesus is the Son of God, God dwells in him, and he in God. And we have known and believed the love that God has to us. God is love; and he that dwells in love dwells in God, and God in him.

*Reading from the General Epistle of John (1 John 4:20–5:5):*[^32]  
Beloved, if a man say: I love God, and hates his brother, he is a liar; for he that loves not his brother whom he has seen, how can he love God Whom he has not seen? And this commandment have we from Him: That he who loves God love his brother also. Whosoever believes that Jesus is the Christ is born of God; and everyone that loves Him that begot loves him also that is begotten of Him. By this we know that we love the children of God, when we love God and keep His commandments. For this is the love of God, that we keep His commandments; and His commandments are not grievous. For whatsoever is born of God overcomes the world; and this is the victory that overcomes the world, even our faith. Who is he that overcomes the world, but he that believes that Jesus is the Son of God?

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^33]

*Sticheron 1:*  
Thou didst receive, O apostle, unconquerable authority and power over demons in the name of Christ, to drive away these principalities of darkness. And thou didst traverse the earth, illuminating like the sun, and didst teach all lands, O glorious one, proclaiming the first, saving coming of Christ.

*Verse: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world (Ps. 18:4).*

*Sticheron 2:*  
Imitating the primal goodness, and the natural and most divine life, thou wast a good man, O [Name], by essence, and called a son of divine grace; by the goodness of thy ways and the purity of thy mind, thou didst show thyself to be a genuine disciple of Christ.

*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands (Ps. 18:1).*

*Sticheron 3:*  
An instrument well-fashioned by divine governance, O apostle [Name], thou art the confirmation of the calling of the nations, guiding them unto the knowledge of Christ by words and deeds; and thou didst enlighten all, to confess the true Godhead of the Trinity, the Savior of our souls.

*Glory... Tone 2:*  
Leaving earthly things behind, thou didst follow Christ, and being sealed by the inspiration of the Holy Spirit, thou wast sent by Him unto the lost nations, to turn men unto the light of the knowledge of God, O apostle [Name]; and having finished the struggles of thy divine sufferings and diverse torments, thou didst commit thy soul unto Christ: Him do thou entreat, O all-blessed one, to grant us great mercy.

*If thou celebratest, speak the Resurrectional Theotokion:*  
*Tone 2: "O new wonder, greater than all ancient wonders"* (see fol. 233v)[^34]

*If thou dost not celebrate, speak this Theotokion:*  
As a fruitful olive tree, the Virgin brought thee forth, the living Fruit, bearing fruit of great and rich mercy for the world.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^35]  
Beholding Thee nailed to the wood of the Cross, O Jesus, she that knew not wedlock weeping said: O sweetest Child, why hast Thou left me alone who bore Thee, O Inaccessible Light of the unoriginate Father? Make haste and be glorified, that they who glorify Thy divine sufferings may obtain divine glory.

*Troparion in the Typikon.*[^36]  
*If it is not in the Typikon, speak this Troparion, Tone 3:*  
Holy apostle [Name], entreat the merciful God, that He may grant remission of sins unto our souls.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," the same troparion.*  
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*  
Having caught the nations, the glorious apostle, and having taught the ends of the earth to worship Thee with the Father and the Spirit, O Christ God: therefore establish Thy Church, and send down Thy blessing upon the faithful, O only merciful Lover of mankind.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
We have become partakers of the divine nature through thee, O Birthgiver of God, Ever-Virgin; for thou didst bear God incarnate for us: therefore as is meet we all piously magnify thee.

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*  
Christ, as the Sun of Righteousness, sent thee forth as a ray to enlighten the whole earth, O glorious apostle [Name]: by thy divine prayers thou illuminest, and with the divine unwaning light thou enlightenest all who in faith celebrate thy holy memory.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
Unashamed hope of them that trust in thee, who alone beyond nature didst bear in the flesh Christ our God: Him with the holy apostles do Thou entreat, to grant cleansing of sins unto the world, and amendment of life unto us all beforehand.

*At the Polyeleos, Megalynarion:*[^37]  
We magnify thee, O holy apostle [Name], and we honor thy pains and labors, wherewith thou didst labor in the Gospel of Christ.

*Selected Psalm: The heavens declare the glory of God (Ps. 18:1).*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^38]  
Having caught rational fish with the net of divine words, thou didst offer these as first-fruits unto our God; and desiring to be clothed in the wounds of Christ, thou wast shown forth as an imitator of His passion. Therefore coming together, as is due we honor, O glorious apostle, thy all-festive memory, and with one accord cry aloud unto thee: Entreat Christ God, that He grant remission of sins unto them that with love honor thy holy memory.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
Let us hymn the heavenly door and ark, the all-holy mountain, the radiant cloud, the unburnt bush, the rational paradise, the deliverance of Eve, the great treasure of all the world: for salvation was wrought in her for the world, and remission of ancient transgressions. Therefore also we cry unto her: Entreat Christ God, to grant remission of sins unto them that piously worship thy most holy Offspring.

*Gradual Hymns, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*  
Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world (Ps. 18:4).  
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God (Ps. 18:1).*  
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*  
*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 34 (Mt. 9:36–10:8).*[^39]  
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*  
Having clearly received the grace of the Holy Spirit, being a fellow-partaker of the sacred apostolic choir, O [Name]: therefore having breathed with a fiery tongue the fiery breath that was once borne from heaven, thou didst burn up the thorny ungodliness of the gentiles; O preacher of Christ God, entreat that our souls may be saved.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos:*  
*Canon of the Birthgiver of God to 4; and of the Saint to 8.*[^40]  
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^41]  
Let us send up a song, O ye peoples, unto our marvelous God, Who freed Israel from bondage, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master!

*Troparion 1:*  
Standing on high before the Master, O marvelous apostle [Name], Who glorified thee and showed thee forth as a radiant disciple unto Himself: enlighten my soul, that I may hymn thy divine memory.

*Troparion 2:*  
Christ, granting thee every excellence of good things, showed thee forth, O apostle, as the summit of divine gifts; by righteous judgment, O God-revealed one, He Who alone is righteous.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having received the all-spiritual ray that came down upon thee from heaven, O apostle, thou wast made fiery-spirited, burning up all the delusion of polytheism.

*Theotokion:*  
Having shown forth my mortal and corruptible nature as immortal and incorruptible, O Savior, Thou didst dwell in the womb of the holy, all-pure Virgin without knowing wedlock, being formed into human nature.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^42]  
There is none holy like the Lord, and none righteous like our God, Whom all creation hymns: there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord.

*Troparion 1:*  
Truly didst thou know the divine mystery of the Incarnation, O apostle [Name], recipient of God, having received transcendent radiance from the Savior Himself.

*Troparion 2:*  
The Word unoriginate and co-eternal richly enlightened thee, His minister, O marvelous apostle, with the bright radiances of divine grace.

*Troparion 3:*  
Kindled with radiance and spiritual activity, thou wast sent forth from Christ as a God-like arrow, O most glorious apostle, and didst enlighten the world by thy teaching, O blessed one.

*Theotokion:*  
The prophet prefigured thee, O Birthgiver of God, as an all-golden lampstand bearing the unwaning Light, Christ our God, Who enlightens the world with the rays of the Godhead.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3.*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры):*[^43]  
By the illumination of the Holy Spirit, thou didst dispel the darkness of polytheism, and didst enlighten the hearts of the faithful, singing the saving commandments, O all-wise apostle [Name]: entreat Christ God, to grant us also great mercy.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
Wheresoever a man finds salvation, thither with good reason does he flee; and what other refuge is there such as thou, O Birthgiver of God, sheltering our souls?

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^44]  
Having acquired the Cross of thy Son as a rod of strength, O Birthgiver of God, thereby do we cast down the boastings of the enemy, incessantly magnifying thee with love.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^45]  
Foreseeing Thee, O Word, about to become incarnate from the mountain overshadowed, the only Birthgiver of God, the prophet beheld Thee with God-seeing eye, and with fear glorified Thy power.

*Troparion 1:*  
Being wholly a treasury of Gospel gifts, filled with grace, thou wast the light of the world and the salt of the universe, O all-blessed [Name].

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou didst turn away from shameful material manifestations, and wast accounted worthy to behold immaterial light: the form of the Godhead having received human matter, O wonder-worthy one.

*Troparion 3:*  
As a disciple of incorruptible life: put to death our indwelling sin by the life-bearing power of the Giver of life, whose operation thou didst receive.

*Theotokion:*  
Equal to Thy Father in essence, Thou wast made equal to men in nature, receiving our flesh, O Master, from the all-pure Virgin.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^46]  
Out of the night of ignorance, having enlightened the ends of the earth with the knowledge of God: illuminate me with the dawn of Thy love for mankind, O Lord.

*Troparion 1:*  
Thy tongue was mingled with spiritual fire, O beholder of God, which Thou didst receive with love, sitting in the upper room.

*Troparion 2:*  
Living as a son exalted in the mansions on high, O [Name], thou didst bring unto us exalted and great teachings.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having a pure mind toward God in brightness, Thou didst acquire a clean heart, and didst behold the incarnate God, incomprehensible to the intellect.

*Theotokion:*  
The first law ceased by thy childbearing, O Virgin; and grace flourished, and righteousness shone forth.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^47]  
Grant me a robe of light, O Thou Who deckest Thyself with light as with a garment, O most merciful Christ our God.

*Troparion 1:*  
As a disciple and friend of Christ, O glorious apostle, thou wast zealous with zeal for the Lord God Almighty.

*Troparion 2:*  
The Savior showed Thee forth as a performer of divine miracles, having given Thee authority by the working of His goodness.

*Troparion 3:*  
Adorned with divine grace, thou didst proclaim to the world the common salvation of God Who is over all, O apostle of Christ [Name], most praised.

*Theotokion:*  
Let the mouths of the impious be stopped who do not confess thee to be the Birthgiver of God, O all-immaculate one; and let their faces be clothed with shame.

*Kontakion in the Typikon.*[^48]  
*If it is not in the Typikon, speak this Kontakion, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^49]  
As a most radiant star the Church has ever acquired thee, O apostle [Name], being enlightened by the manifold granting of thy miracles; therefore we cry unto Christ: Save them that in faith honor the memory of Thine apostle, O Thou Who art plenteous in mercy.

*Ikos:*  
Grant me a stream of speech, O Lord Who didst make the watery nature, and strengthen my heart, O Compassionate One, Who didst establish the earth by Thy word; and enlighten my thought, O Thou Who deckest Thyself with light as with a garment: that I may speak and sing what is meet, worthily to honor Thine apostle, O Thou Who art plenteous in mercy.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^50]  
The Hebrew youths in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages.

*Troparion 1:*  
Having marvelous zeal, thou now standest before the throne of the Master, O [Name]: as a servant of Christ thou wast crowned, O preacher of God, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages.

*Troparion 2:*  
Together rejoicing in dance with the Word and sitting beside Him, thou didst share there in the kingdom on high, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages.

*Troparion 3:*  
Clothed with wisdom, irradiated with grace, adorned with peaceful beauty, O God-speaking holy apostle, thou criest aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages.

*Theotokion:*  
By the deceit of fair appearance the serpent once cast out the forebears; but unto the Son, O Mother of God, thou didst cry aloud: Blessed is the fruit of thy womb, O all-pure one.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^51]  
While the musical instruments sounded in harmony, and countless peoples fell down in worship to the image in Dura, the three youths, refusing to obey, praised the Lord and glorified Him unto all ages.

*Troparion 1:*  
How beautiful were shown forth thy feet, comely also is thy tongue speaking the glory of Christ, and it taught to cry: Hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 2:*  
Adorned with radiant virtues and emitting the flashes of miracles, thou wast known unto the peoples as a blessed seed, having assured them to cry aloud: Hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 3:*  
A holy disciple, taught truly in heavenly mysteries, thou didst traverse the universe, teaching with loud voice the word of faith in Christ, O apostle, and confessing ineffable grace, crying aloud: Hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*  
No mind can declare thy childbearing, O Parent of God, and speech is powerless to utter it: for having conceived God, thou didst bear Him, O Virgin, Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^52]  
Truly we confess thee to be the Birthgiver of God, who through thee are saved, O pure Virgin, with the bodiless choirs magnifying thee.

*Troparion 1:*  
Thou wast revealed unto the ends of the earth, O apostle, by the divine light of holiness; and having received spiritual fire, thou wast shown forth radiant, O [Name]: therefore do we magnify thee.

*Troparion 2:*  
Wholly dedicated unto God, thou wast fashioned with Him; Him do thou now entreat, O God-blessed one, for us who in faith and love praise thee.

*Troparion 3:*  
Celebrating thy all-blessed memory, we ever pray that by the intercession of thy boldness we may be delivered from surges of temptation, which boldness thou hast as an apostle of Christ, O all-honored beholder of God.

*Theotokion:*  
Her that appeared unto men as the mediatress of the salvation of all, and who enlightened the world with the radiances of God-beholding purity, in songs do we magnify.

*Exapostilarion.*  
*Podoben: "Having seen in spirit" (Дх҃омъ прозря̀):*[^53]  
Having directed the course of thy beautiful feet unto the heavenly pathway, O apostle [Name], thou didst ascend rejoicing; and standing before the Trinity, in the Father thou beholdest the Son and the divine Spirit: therefore in faith we celebrate thy most sacred and divine memory.

*Theotokion:*  
Remembering the hour of the fearful examination, the multitude of my evil deeds appalls and terrifies me: yet take pity, O all-pure one, by thy fervent prayer, and grant me salvation; for whatsoever thou desirest, thou canst do.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos:*  
*At the Praises, stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^54]

*Sticheron 1 (Twice):*  
With the rod of grace, from the depth of vanity thou didst draw forth men, O wonder-worthy one, obeying the beckoning of the Teacher, O [Name], Who enlightened thine understanding in all things, and manifestly showed thee forth as an apostle and honorable speaker of God of His incomprehensible Godhead.

*Sticheron 2:*  
The radiance of the Spirit came down upon thee in the form of fire, and made thee, O blessed one, a divine receptacle, swiftly driving away the mist of ungodliness and enlightening the world with the radiance of thy all-wise words, O speaker of mysteries, adornment of apostles, blessed eyewitness of Christ.

*Sticheron 3:*  
Illuminating with the lightnings of preaching them that sat in the darkness of ignorance, O glorious one, thou didst show them forth as sons of the Master and God through faith; Whose sufferings and death thou didst emulate, and of Whose glory thou wast made an heir, being wise and God-speaking, as a disciple of truth.

*Glory... Tone 2:*  
Leaving earthly things behind, thou didst follow Christ, and being sealed by the inspiration of the Holy Spirit, thou wast sent by Him unto the lost nations, to turn men unto the light of the knowledge of God, O apostle [Name]; and having finished the struggles of thy divine sufferings and diverse torments, thou didst commit thy soul unto Christ: Him do thou entreat, O all-blessed one, to grant us great mercy.

*Now and ever... Theotokion:*

All my hope I place in thee, O Mother of God: keep me under thy protection.

*At the Liturgy, Beatitudes from the Canon, Ode 3 and Ode 6.*  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*  
Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world (Ps. 18:4).  
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God (Ps. 18:1).*  
*Epistle to the Corinthians, pericope 131 (1 Cor. 4:9–16).*[^55]  
*Alleluia, Tone 1: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord (Ps. 88:5).*  
*Verse: God is glorified in the council of the saints (Ps. 88:7).*  
*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 34 (Mt. 9:36–10:8).*  
*Communion Verse: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world (Ps. 18:4).*

## Common Service to Two and Many Apostles

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^56]

*Sticheron 1:*  
As eyewitnesses and witnesses of the Incarnation of the Word, O most rich disciples, ye are called blessed: for ye appeared unto the world flashing like lightning, and like mountains dripping spiritual sweetness; like ever-flowing rivers of paradise having divided, ye water the churches of the nations with divine waters.

*Sticheron 2:*  
As rays flashing with spiritual splendors, ye were sent into all the world, unstintingly granting the working of miracles: having been ministers of the Mysteries of Christ, and God-inscribed tablets of the grace of God, written with the God-taught Law, O most rich sacred initiates.

*Sticheron 3:*  
The fishermen's rod crossed out the arrogance of philosophers and the streams of rhetoricians, setting forth the dogmas in God-wise teachings: clearly setting forth the Gospel of good mysteries, partaking of ever-existing nourishment, and angelic enjoyment, and enduring glory.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, radiant sun, chariot of the unsetting Sun, who didst cause the Inconceivable Sun to dawn forth! Rejoice, O mind gleaming with divine splendors, ray of brilliance illuminating the ends of the earth, who art truly golden-beaming, all-fair and all-immaculate, who didst shine forth the unwaning Light unto the faithful!

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^57]  
Beholding Christ, the Lover of mankind, crucified and pierced in the side with a spear, the all-pure one wept, crying aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee, in return for the good things Thou didst do for them? And Thou makest haste to leave me childless, O most beloved One: I marvel, O Compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: On "Lord, I have cried," stichera to 6.*  
*Glory... Tone 8:*  
The creation which ye traversed, ye illuminated, O disciples of the Savior, burning up idol delusion like brushwood with your teachings; having caught the nations from the depth of ignorance unto the knowledge of God, ye saved them: and now pray ye unto Christ, that He may be merciful unto us in the Day of Judgment.

*Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
*Tone 8: "The King of heaven, for His love of mankind"* (see fol. 235r)[^58]

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the Readings:*  
*Reading from the General Epistle of Peter (1 Peter 1:1–9):*[^59]  
Brethren, blessed be the God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, Who according to His abundant mercy has begotten us again unto a lively hope by the resurrection of Jesus Christ from the dead, to an inheritance incorruptible, and undefiled, and that fades not away, reserved in heaven for you, who are kept by the power of God through faith unto salvation ready to be revealed in the last time ready to be revealed in the last time. Wherein ye greatly rejoice, though now for a season, if need be, ye are in heaviness through manifold temptations: that the trial of your faith, being much more precious than of gold that perishes, though it be tried with fire, might be found unto praise and honor and glory at the appearing of Jesus Christ: Whom having not seen, ye love; in Whom, though now ye see Him not, yet believing, ye rejoice with joy unspeakable and full of glory: receiving the end of your faith, even the salvation of your souls.

*Reading from the General Epistle of Peter (1 Peter 1:13–19):*[^60]  
Beloved, girding up the loins of your mind, being sober, hope to the end for the grace that is brought unto you at the revelation of Jesus Christ; as obedient children, not conforming yourselves to the former lusts of your ignorance: but as He Who called you is holy, so be ye holy in all your manner of life; because it is written: Be ye holy, for I am holy. And if ye call on the Father, Who without respect of persons judges according to each man's work, conduct yourselves in fear during the time of your sojourning: knowing that ye were not redeemed with corruptible things, with silver or gold, from your vain way of life received by tradition from your fathers, but with the precious blood of Christ, as of a lamb without blemish and without spot.

*Reading from the General Epistle of Peter (1 Peter 2:11–24):*[^61]  
Beloved, I beseech you as strangers and pilgrims, abstain from fleshly lusts, which war against the soul; having your manner of life honorable among the gentiles: that, whereas they speak against you as evildoers, they may by your good works, which they shall behold, glorify God in the day of visitation. Submit yourselves to every ordinance of man for the Lord's sake: whether it be to the king, as supreme; or unto governors, as unto them that are sent by him for the punishment of evildoers, and for the praise of them that do well. For so is the will of God, that with well doing ye may put to silence the ignorance of foolish men: as free, and not using your liberty for a cloak of maliciousness, but as the servants of God. Honor all men. Love the brotherhood. Fear God. Honor the king. Servants, be subject to your masters with all fear; not only to the good and gentle, but also to the froward. For this is thankworthy, if a man for conscience toward God endure grief, suffering wrongfully. For what praise is it, if, when ye are beaten for your faults, ye shall take it patiently? but if, when ye do well, and suffer for it, ye take it patiently, this is acceptable with God. For even hereunto were ye called: because Christ also suffered for us, leaving us an example, that ye should follow His steps: Who did no sin, neither was guile found in His mouth: Who, when He was reviled, reviled not again; when He suffered, He threatened not; but committed Himself to Him that judges righteously: Who His own self bore our sins in His own body on the tree, that we, being dead to sins, should live unto righteousness.

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 6.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "On the third day" (Трїдне́внѡ):*[^62]

*Sticheron 1:*  
Ye became ministers of the great mysteries of God, O theologians and God-beholding disciples; and having received the grace of healings, ye heal the infirmities of all men.

*Verse: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world (Ps. 18:4).*

*Sticheron 2:*  
A great refuge and protection of our souls, driving away grievous spirits, are ye, O God-beholding apostles of the Lord: therefore do we ever honor you.

*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands (Ps. 18:1).*

*Sticheron 3:*  
From every distress, and demonic harm, and transgression, and evil captivity, deliver them that in faith praise you, O divine and blessed apostles.

*Glory... Tone 4:*  
Instruments well-fashioned by divine governance, O apostles, ye are the confirmation of the calling of the nations, guiding them unto the knowledge of Christ by words and deeds; and ye enlightened all, to confess the true Godhead of the Trinity, the Savior of our souls.

*If thou celebratest, speak the Resurrectional Theotokion:*  
*Tone 4: "Look down upon the entreaties of thy servants"* (see fol. 234r)[^63]

*If thou dost not celebrate, speak this Theotokion:*  
Having thee, O Birthgiver of God, as our hope and intercession, we shall not fear the snares of the enemy, for thou savest our souls.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^64]  
A sword, as Simeon said, pierced thy heart, O all-holy Lady, when thou didst behold the ineffable Word Who shone forth from thee, condemned by the lawless, lifted up upon the Cross, tasting vinegar and gall, His side pierced, His hands and feet nailed; and with weeping thou didst cry aloud, maternally exclaiming: What is this, O sweet Child, this new mystery?

*Troparion, Tone 3:*  
Holy apostles, entreat the merciful God, that He grant remission of sins unto our souls.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," the same troparion.*  
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*[^65]  
The spiritual choir of the apostles was mystically proclaimed unto the world from God on high, and they appeared as physicians of them that suffer great afflictions; uniquely invoking the Tri-hypostatic God, they wisely recorded the divine Incarnation of the Lord Emmanuel.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
The bush which Moses saw unconsumed, the mountain of God, the holy cloud, the undefiled tabernacle, the table that received God, the palace of the King on high, the all-radiant and impassable gate, O Virgin, do we hymn thee.

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*[^66]  
With light-beaming radiances, as stars ye enlighten the ends of the earth by the teachings of holy preaching, O heavenly initiates of the Lord's mysteries, apostles. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
The fervent defense of them that are in distress, our help and reconciliation unto God, through whom we were delivered from corruption: the Birthgiver of God do we, the faithful, call blessed.

*At the Polyeleos, Megalynarion:*[^67]  
We magnify you, O holy apostles, who enlightened the whole world with your teachings, and brought all the ends of the earth unto Christ.

*Selected Psalm: The heavens declare the glory of God (Ps. 18:1).*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3.*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры):*[^68]  
As divine trumpets of the Comforter, sounding forth the breath of the words of salvation, ye proclaimed it unto the world; and crying aloud, ye brought them that slept in the darkness of delusion unto the light of the knowledge of God, O apostles of the divine Light, praying unto Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
Not departing from the divine nature when He became flesh in thy womb, yet God remained incarnate, Who preserved thee as a Virgin Mother after childbearing even as before birth, all-immaculate, the only Lord: Him do thou fervently entreat to grant us great mercy.

*Gradual Hymns, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*  
Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world (Ps. 18:4).  
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God (Ps. 18:1).*  
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*  
*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 50 (Lk. 10:1–15).*[^69]  
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*  
The all-honored feast of the apostles has arrived for the Church of Christ, mediating salvation unto us all; mystically clapping our hands, let us say unto them: Rejoice, ye lamps of them that are in darkness, flashing the rays of the Noetic Sun! Rejoice, O apostles, unshakable foundations of divine teachings, friends of Christ, honorable vessels! Come into the midst in the midst of us invisibly, granting immaterial gifts unto them that praise your feast with songs.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos:*  
*Canon of the Birthgiver of God to 4; and of the Saints to 8.*[^70]  
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^71]  
Having traversed the deep of the Red Sea on foot with dryshod steps, ancient Israel of old, by Moses' hands stretched out in the form of a cross, vanquished the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparion 1:*  
As I desire to hymn the apostolic choir, O Christ, by their prayers, as God, grant me the radiance of the All-Holy Spirit and the light of Thy wisdom.

*Troparion 2:*  
Strengthened by Thy power and grace, O Christ, Thine honorable apostles destroyed the power of the adversary enemies, having become beholders of God by blameless beckoning toward Thee.

*Troparion 3:*  
Working healings in Thy name, O Master, the glorious apostles caught the gatherings of the gentiles into the knowledge of Thee, and enlightened them with Thy light.

*Troparion 4:*  
Taught heavenly wisdom, O most glorious apostles of Christ, truly wise, ye manifestly turned the wordiness of the impious into foolishness by the success of your preaching.

*Theotokion:*  
Thou didst bear, O all-pure one, One of the most divine Trinity, Who appeared in the flesh from thee according to our nature, by the good pleasure of Him Who begot Him, and by the operation of the All-Holy Spirit, O Virgin Mother.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^72]  
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and my foundation!

*Troparion 1:*  
Speaking heavenly and honorable teachings on earth, preaching God, confirmed with fiery tongues, ye delivered them, O preachers of Christ.

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou didst show forth Thy disciples, O Master, as rational heavens declaring Thy glory unto all the ends of the earth.

*Troparion 3:*  
Being written in the heavens, and having appeared as habitations of Christ: preserve now, O all-wise ones, them that honor you with love.

*Theotokion:*  
He that dwells in the heights took up His abode among us, O all-pure one: for having taken flesh from thee without seed, He appeared.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^73]  
Having caught rational fish with the net of divine words, ye brought these as first-fruits unto our God; and desiring to be clothed in the wounds of Christ, ye were shown forth as imitators of His passions: therefore coming together, as is due we honor, O glorious apostles, your all-festive memory, and with one accord cry aloud unto you: Entreat Christ God, that He grant remission of sins unto them that with love honor your holy memory.

*Glory... Now and ever... Theotokion:*  
As a Virgin and the only one among women who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all generations of men call thee blessed: for the Fire of the Godhead dwelt in thee, and thou sucklest the Creator and Lord as an infant: therefore the angelic and human race worthily glorify thy all-holy childbearing, and with one accord cry aloud unto thee: Entreat Christ God, that He grant remission of sins unto them that in faith worship thy most holy Offspring.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^74]  
The Ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, cried out weeping, and bitterly lamenting exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee; but my maternal inward parts burn, beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender compassions of mercy, O Long-Suffering Lord, Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Fountain: have compassion, and grant remission of sins unto them that in faith hymn Thy divine sufferings.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^75]  
Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of Righteousness, the Church stood in her place, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion 1:*  
The sound of the divine words of the apostles went forth into all the universe like fire, burning up the material of delusion, and enlightening the congregations of the pious with grace.

*Troparion 2:*  
The world, blackened with the gloom of ungodliness, the disciples of the Lord enlightened as God-shining luminaries with the rays of grace, having appeared with the radiance of preaching.

*Troparion 3:*  
Enlightened with the sacred rays of the Noetic Sun, ye shine like the sun in the world, O all-praised ones, driving away the mist of delusion with divine light.

*Troparion 4:*  
Having Thy Cross as a rod of strength, Thine eyewitnesses of glorious life, O Word, cut through the sea, like horses troubling the waters of polytheism.

*Theotokion:*  
Adorned with diverse effusions of light like an animate heaven, bearing Thee, O Christ, King of kings, the all-pure Maiden is now glorified as the Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^76]  
Thou, O my Lord, didst come into the world as a light, a holy light turning from the dark gloom of ignorance them that in faith hymn Thee.

*Troparion 1:*  
Thy divine and all-wise ministers Thou didst show forth as light in the world, proclaiming Thee unto all, the Unsetting Light.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having manifestly traversed every virtue, O apostles, destroy the tangled webs of diverse demonic malice.

*Troparion 3:*  
The apostles revealed unto us the Trinitarian brilliance in the unity of the Godhead, speaking with fiery tongues.

*Theotokion:*  
Thee do we set forth as an unconquerable weapon against our enemies; thee have we acquired as our foundation and the hope of our salvation, O Bride of God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^77]  
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.

*Troparion 1:*  
Having established Thy disciples with wisdom and miracles, O Savior, Thou didst make them mightier than Hellenic babbling, and they destroyed its deceitful dictates.

*Troparion 2:*  
Divine rivers of wisdom filled all the church valleys with saving waters, enriching them with streams from the saving springs.

*Troparion 3:*  
Ye were shown forth as living stars, O all-praised ones; by radiant flashings scatter all dark delusion, having made the light of the knowledge of God to dawn.

*Theotokion:*  
He saw thee as a dove perfect and all-comely, finding a radiant lily and flower of the valleys, O Mother of God: the Noetic Bridegroom dwelt in thee.

*Kontakion in the Typikon.*[^78]  
*If it is not in the Typikon, speak this Kontakion, Tone 4:*[^79]  
Ye appeared as branches of the vine of Christ, O wise ones, bearing clusters of virtues, pouring out for us the wine of salvation; which receiving, we are filled with gladness and celebrate your honorable memory, in which pray ye, O apostles of the Lord, that great mercy and remission of sins may be granted unto us.

*Ikos:*  
As disciples of the Master of all, O beholders of God, with the net of your prayers draw forth from the depth of transgressions my humble soul, snared by the nets of demonic wiles: that having passed well the remaining time of my life, I may sing I may sing of you with love, and glorify your blameless life which ye accomplished on earth, having enlightened those grievously darkened, teaching them divinely to honor the Trinity, O apostles of the Lord.

*Ode 7, Tone 4. Heirmos:*[^80]  
The Abrahamite youths in the Persian furnace, burning more with the love of piety than with the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Troparion 1:*  
Having made Thy disciples sons, O Thou Who beforehand art God by nature, Thou didst show them forth in love as heirs of the Father's inheritance, O most good One, and didst deign to walk with them as God and Master.

*Troparion 2:*  
Granting an outpouring of wisdom, breadth of heart, and an eloquent tongue unto Thy divine disciples, O Word, Thou didst send them forth to preach the Gospel of the Kingdom unto all the ends of the earth.

*Troparion 3:*  
The apostles appeared as clouds of the divine light, raining down living water upon all, crying: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Theotokion:*  
Adorned beforehand with the glory of God thou didst appear, O all-pure one, who alone from all ages didst conceive God the Word, O Virgin Mother: blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady.

*Ode 8, Tone 4. Heirmos:*[^81]  
Daniel, stretching forth his hands, shut the lions' mouths in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion 1:*  
By the fire of the Spirit, the divine and most wise choir of Christ's apostles burned up the diverse schools of the demons like tares, and enlightened the hearts of the faithful, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion 2:*  
With one accord let us honor the divine disciples, the apostles of Christ, who thundered forth unto us the divine commands, the havens of the faithful, the common benefactors of mankind, the servants of the Savior.

*Troparion 3:*  
Let us honor the all-honorable and receptive vessels of virtues: the first-fruits of humanity, the trumpets of preaching, the streams of incorruptible life, the God-bearing lightnings, the fountains of healings, the beautiful feet of the Gospel.

*Theotokion:*  
The full Son humbled Himself for our sake, that we might partake of His fullness; for having entered into thy all-pure womb, the Uncontainable One, not departing from the Father's bosom, took flesh: therefore we all bless thee, O Mary, Bride of God.

*Ode 9, Tone 4. Heirmos:*[^82]  
A cornerstone was cut out without hands from thee, O unquarried mountain, O Virgin, even Christ, Who joined together the disparate natures: therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God.

*Troparion 1:*  
Having received authority from the Master to loose the bonds of transgressions, O beholders of God, mercifully cleanse of sin those who sing your praise, and count them worthy of salvation.

*Troparion 2:*  
Ye all received the all-spiritual radiance substantially appeared unto you, O glorious apostles, being instructed in the upper chamber in lofty preaching and teaching; and now ye are worthily blessed.

*Troparion 3:*  
Unto you, His friends who have now reposed, Christ gives unfading crowns, and fills you with divine vision: pray ye now unto Him, to save all churches.

*Theotokion:*  
He that adorned all things by a word, willing to come in the flesh, took up His abode in thee, having found thee alone the rampart of all, and showed thee forth as the true Mother of God, O Virgin Mother.

*Exapostilarion:*[^83]  
Come, ye people, let us hymn the apostles of Christ with divine songs, the preachers of the faith, as they pray unto Christ for our souls.

...for our souls.

*Theotokion:*  
All-holy, pure Sovereign Lady, who alone art unwedded, salvation and hope: do thou entreat God our Savior born of thee, to save the world from delusion, and from perils and afflictions.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O Lord, though Thou didst stand before the tribunal" (Гдⷭ҇и, а҆́ще и҆ на сꙋди́щи):*[^84]

*Sticheron 1:*  
O Lord, by the rays of the Comforter Thou didst enlighten Thine apostles, and having set them as luminaries for the confirmation of the faith, by the noetic enlightenment of Thy knowledge, O Master: therefore do we worship Thine ineffable love for mankind. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 2:*  
O Lord, by the prayers of the apostles Thou hast surrounded this Thy flock, keeping it unharmed from the temptation of enemies; for Thou didst redeem it with Thy precious blood from the bondage of the stranger, as Thou art compassionate and the Lover of mankind.

*Sticheron 3:*  
Ye appeared together in the foundation of the Church as precious stones, flashing forth radiant dawn unto the inhabited earth with the sure knowledge of God, O divine apostles, intercessors before the Trinity and beseechers for our souls.

*Glory... Tone 4:*  
As winged eagles ye traversed all the earth, scattering honorable teachings and plucking out the tares of deceit by grace, and producing fruit of many ears, O all-praised ones, which the noetic granaries hold unto ages, richly preserved for the immortal Husbandman.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Having thee, O Mother of God, as hope and intercession, we shall not fear the assaults of enemies, for thou savest our souls.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Ode 3 and Ode 6.*  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 8: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world (Ps. 18:4).*  
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands (Ps. 18:1).*  
*Epistle to the Corinthians, Pericope 131 (1 Cor. 4:9–16).*[^85]  
*Alleluia, Tone 1: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord (Ps. 88:6).*  
*Verse: God is glorified in the council of the saints (Ps. 88:8).*  
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 50 (Luke 10:1–15) or Pericope 51 (Luke 10:16–21).*[^86]  
*Communion Hymn: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world (Ps. 18:4).*

## Common Service to a Single Hierarch[^87]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried": Stichera, Tone 6.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*[^88]

*Sticheron 1:*  
All-consecrated, thou didst show thyself a God-bearer, arrayed by the Holy Spirit with the holy anointing of God; and radiant, continually entering into the Holy of Holies, illumined by the divinely originating brightnesses, partaking of the grace of the Holy Mysteries as a true hierarch most glorious, praying with boldness for our souls.

*Sticheron 2:*  
Thy life shone forth with the brightness of virtues; thou didst enlighten the faithful and drive away the darkness of delusion; for thou didst appear as a true radiant sun, O all-blessed hierarch [Name]; and now thou hast taken up thine abode where the unwaning light shines, having become a son of day by the grace of the Holy Spirit: therefore, celebrating thy divine and light-bearing memory with honor, O ever-memorable one, we revere thee with love.

*Sticheron 3:*  
Thy mind adorned by faith through its inclination unto God, O divinely wise one, thou wast shown forth as a hierarch all-glorious; in a mortal and corruptible body, having wisely meditated upon incorruption, thou didst acquire the brightness of the bodiless hosts; and having come to be outside passions, adorned with dispassion, [Name], O father, hierarch most wise, radiant luminary and intercessor for those who honor thy memory with love.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, fulfillment of the law! Rejoice, temple of the Holy Trinity, incorruptible bride! Rejoice, divine chariot of the King of all! Rejoice, carrying the immaterial Coal in thy hands like the tongs of the fire! New paradise, enclosed garden, all-radiant divine table, undefiled dove! Throne of the Most High, divine noetic couch, whom the Holy Spirit overshadowed, O maiden!

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^89]  
When of old the undefiled Ewe-lamb and blameless Sovereign Lady beheld her Lamb lifted up upon the Cross, she cried aloud maternally, and wondering wept: What is this sight, O sweetest Child, new and all-glorious? How do the ungrateful people deliver Thee unto Pilate's judgment-seat, and condemn the Life of all unto death? Yet I hymn Thine ineffable condescension, O Word!

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: At "Lord, I have cried": Stichera on 6.*  
*Glory... Tone 8:*  
The fruit of thy virtues, O venerable father, has enlightened the hearts of the faithful; for who, hearing of thy boundless humility, did not marvel at thy patience, thy gentleness toward the poor, thy consolation unto the afflicted? Thou didst teach all things in a manner worthy of God, O hierarch [Name]; and now thou art crowned with an unfading crown, praying for our souls.

*Both now... Theotokion (Tone 8): "The King of heaven, through His love for mankind..." (see fol. 235r).*[^90]  
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the readings:*

*Reading from the Proverbs (Prov. 10:7, 3:13–16, composite):*[^91]  
The memory of the righteous is with praises, and the blessing of the Lord is upon his head. Blessed is the man that finds wisdom, and the mortal that knows prudence. For it is better to buy her than treasures of gold and silver. And she is more precious than costly stones; and everything precious is not worthy of her. For length of days and years of life are in her right hand, and in her left hand are riches and glory. Out of her mouth proceeds righteousness, and law and mercy she carries upon her tongue. Give ear therefore unto me, O children, for I will speak honorable things; and blessed is the man that will keep my ways: for my outgoings are the outgoings of life, and desire is prepared from the Lord. For this cause I entreat you, and put forth my voice unto the sons of men: for I, wisdom, have built; counsel, knowledge, and understanding have I called upon. Mine is counsel and confirmation; mine is understanding, and mine is strength. I love them that love me, and they that seek me shall find grace. Understand craftiness therefore, ye simple ones, and apply your hearts, ye uninstructed. Give ear unto me again, for I will speak honorable things; and I will open right things from my lips, for my throat shall meditate on truth, and lying lips are an abomination before me. All the words of my mouth are with righteousness; there is nothing crooked nor perverse in them. All things are right unto them that understand, and plain to them that find knowledge. For I will teach you truth, that your hope may be in the Lord, and ye may be filled with the Spirit.[^92]

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Prov. 10:31–32, 11:7, Wis. 6:12–15, 7:30, 8:2–8, composite):*[^93]  
The mouth of the righteous drops wisdom; and the lips of wise men know grace. The mouth of the wise meditates on wisdom; and righteousness delivers from death. When a righteous man dies, his hope does not perish; for a righteous son is born unto life, and in his good things he shall embrace the fruit of righteousness. Light is always upon the righteous, and from the Lord they shall find grace and glory. The tongue of the wise knows good things, and in their heart wisdom rests. The Lord loves pious hearts, and acceptable unto Him are all who are blameless in the way. The wisdom of the Lord enlightens the face of the understanding man; for she anticipates them that desire her, before they know her; and she is easily seen of them that love her. Whoso rises early unto her shall have no great travail; and he that watches for her shall quickly be without care. For she goes about herself seeking such as are worthy of her, and shows herself favorably unto them in the ways. Malice shall never prevail against wisdom: for this cause I was a lover of her beauty, and I loved her, and sought her out from my youth. And I desired to make her my spouse, for the Master of all loved her; for she is privy to the mystery of the knowledge of God, and a chooser of His works. Her labors are virtues; for she teaches sobriety and understanding, righteousness and courage, than which men can have nothing more profitable in their life.
