And if a man desire much experience, she knows things of old, and considers things to come; she knows the subtleties of speeches, and the solutions of riddles; she foresees signs and wonders, and the issues of seasons and years; and she is a good counselor in all things, for immortality is in her, and good report in the communion of her words. For this cause I conversed with the Lord, and prayed unto Him, and said from my whole heart: O God of our fathers and Lord of mercy, Who hast made all things with Thy word, and ordained man through Thy wisdom, that he should have dominion over the creatures which were made by Thee, and order the world in holiness and righteousness: give me wisdom, that sits by Thy throne; and reject me not from among Thy children, for I am Thy servant and son of Thine handmaid. Send her out of Thy holy heavens, and from the throne of Thy glory, that being present she may labor with me, that I may know what is pleasing before Thee: and she shall lead me into understanding, and preserve me in her glory. For the thoughts of mortal men are all fearful, and their devices fallible.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wis. 4:7–10, composite with Wis. 6:18, 7:15–26, 10:9–12, 2:1–15):*[^94]  
When the righteous man is praised, the people rejoice; for the memory of him is immortal, because it is known with God and with men; and his soul was pleasing unto the Lord. Love wisdom therefore, O men, and ye shall live; desire her, and ye shall be instructed: for the beginning of her is love, and the keeping of laws. Honor wisdom, that ye may reign unto ages. I will tell you, and will not hide from you the mysteries of God: for He is the guide of wisdom, and the director of the wise; and in all understanding and knowledge He is an artificer. And wisdom teaches all understanding: for in her is a spirit of understanding and holy, the brightness of the everlasting light, and the image of the goodness of God: she makes men friends of God and prophets. She is more beautiful than the sun, and above all the order of stars; being compared with the light, she is found before it. She delivered from afflictions those that served her, and guided them in straight paths; she gave them holy understanding, and preserved them from hunters, and gave them a mighty struggle, that they all might know that piety is stronger than all. And malice shall never prevail against wisdom, nor shall judgment, when it reproves, pass by the wicked. For they said within themselves, reasoning unrighteously: Let us oppress the righteous man, and let us not spare his holiness, neither let us reverence the ancient gray hairs of the aged. Let our strength be the law; and let us lie in wait for the righteous, because he is not for our turn, and he is contrary to our doings, and upbraids us with our offending the law, and objects to our infamy the transgressions of our education. He professes to have the knowledge of God, and calls himself the child of the Lord. He was made to reprove our thoughts; he is grievous unto us even to behold, for his life is unlike other men's, and his ways are changed. We were esteemed of him as counterfeits; and he abstains from our ways as from filthiness, and pronounces the end of the righteous to be blessed. Let us see then if his words be true, and let us prove what shall happen in the end of him. Let us examine him with insult and torture, that we may know his meekness, and prove his patience. Let us condemn him with a shameful death, for according to his words he shall be respected. Such things did they imagine, and were deceived; for their own malice blinded them. As for the mysteries of God, they knew them not, neither did they judge that Thou art God alone, having the power of life and death, saving in time of tribulation, and delivering from all evil: compassionate and merciful, granting grace unto Thy venerable ones, and with Thy right arm resisting the proud.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 8.*

*Sticheron 1:*  
O hierarch [Name], ray of the noetic light, luminary of the Church, beauty of hierarchs, true rule of the monastic fasting life: thou hast appeared as a protector unto us, delivering our souls from the destroying enemy.

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints (Ps. 115:6).*

*Sticheron 2:*  
O hierarch [Name], thou didst receive great power from God; by thy prayer thou didst drive away a multitude of crafty demons and their malice from all who flee unto thee with faith.

*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me? (Ps. 115:3).*

*Sticheron 3:*  
How shall we worthily hymn the great hierarch [Name], of honorable mind, the God-radiant light illuminating us and imparting divine knowledge, the confessor of profound mysteries? With one accord let us all say: Rejoice, O father of fathers, [Name]!

*Glory... Tone 6:*  
Thou art a good shepherd and a fervent teacher, O hierarch [Name]; praising thee we ever cry aloud: Through thee God adorned His Church, and showed forth unto His people thy body, hidden for many years in the earth, incorruptible: therefore cease not to pray for those who praise thee, and who honor thy memory; ask remission of sins and salvation for our souls.

*Both now... Theotokion (Tone 6): "O unwedded Virgin..." (see fol. 234v).*[^95]  
*If thou dost not celebrate, speak this Theotokion:*  
Sovereign Lady, accept the prayer of thy servants, and deliver us from every constraint and sorrow.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^96]  
Beholding Thee nailed to the Cross, O Jesus, and voluntarily accepting sufferings, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, wept aloud: Woe is me, O sweetest Child! How dost Thou endure wounds unjustly, O Physician Who hast healed human weakness and delivered all from corruption by Thy tender mercy?

*Troparion in the Typikon.*[^97]  
*If it is not in the Typikon, speak this Troparion, Tone 4:*[^98]  
The truth of things revealed thee to thy flock as a rule of faith, an icon of meekness, and a teacher of temperance; therefore thou hast acquired by humility that which is exalted, and by poverty that which is rich: O holy father hierarch [Name], entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion.*  
*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": the same Troparion.*  
*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*[^99]  
Having been a servant unto Christ, the Master of all, thou didst instruct the people and enlighten them by divine Baptism, guiding them unto the knowledge of Him, O hierarch: therefore we all name thee a preacher of truth, a hierarch and well-pleasing servant of Christ. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Unwedded, pure Mother of God, O Virgin, who alone art the protectress and covering of the faithful: from perils and afflictions, and cruel tribulations deliver all who have hope in thee, O maiden, and save our souls by thy divine supplications.

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*  
Taking up thy Cross from youth, thou didst piously follow Christ, having withered the carnal mind by temperance: therefore, seated upon the throne of hierarchy, O hierarch, thou didst magnify the Master and His most pure Mother, Who have adorned thy shrine with diverse gifts, [Name], O divinely blessed one. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
As all-immaculate one who didst give birth unto transcendent God, entreat Him unceasingly together with the hierarch, that remission of transgressions and amendment of life before the end be granted unto us, who sing thy praise with faith and love as is meet, O only all-hymned one.

*At the Polyeleos, Megalynarion:*  
We magnify thee, O holy father hierarch [Name], and we honor thy holy memory: for thou dost pray for us unto Christ our God.  
*Selected Psalm: Hear this, all ye nations (Ps. 48:1).*[^100]

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^101]  
Having reigned over carnal passions, thou didst appear a God-like hierarch; and thou didst most gloriously shepherd thy flock, O venerable one, and didst enlighten them with Baptism, and instruct them to glorify one God in three Hypostases: therefore thou pourest forth healings even after thy decease unto those who come with faith to the holy church of God and to the shrine of thy relics, O hierarch [Name]: entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love. *(Twice) Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Let us hymn the heavenly gate and ark, the all-holy mountain, the radiant cloud, the unburnt bush, the rational paradise, the deliverance of Eve, the great treasure of all the universe: for salvation was wrought in it for the world, and remission of ancient transgressions; therefore we cry aloud unto her: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who piously worship thy all-holy birthgiving.

*Anabathmoi, the first Antiphon of Tone 4.*[^102]  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: My mouth shall speak of wisdom, and the meditation of my heart shall be of understanding (Ps. 48:4).*  
*Verse: The mouth of the righteous meditates on wisdom (Ps. 36:30).*  
*Let every breath praise the Lord (Ps. 150:6).*  
*Gospel according to John, Pericope 36 (John 10:9–16).*[^103]  
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*  
Heir of God, companion of Christ, servant of the Lord, holy [Name]! Even as was thy name, so was thy life: for understanding was yoked with thy gray hairs; the brightness of thy countenance bore witness to the simplicity of thy soul; the modesty of thy speech made manifest thy meekness. Thy life was glorious, and thy dormition with the saints: pray thou for our souls.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos:*  
*Canon of the Theotokos on 4, and of the saint on 8.*[^104]  
*Ode 1, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^105]  
Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, Israel, beholding Pharaoh the pursuer drowned, cried aloud: Unto God let us sing a triumphal song!

*Troparion 1:*  
Standing with the choirs on high as a hierarch and servant of God, pray fervently for us, that through thy prayers we may obtain eternal good things.

*Troparion 2:*  
Chosen to serve the honorable Gospel unto the Lord, O blessed [Name]: by thy most wise teachings, O father, thou didst nurture the people in the knowledge of God.

*Troparion 3:*  
Enriched with the knowledge of God, thou didst pour forth the word of life from thy heart, watering souls famished by passions, O most wise hierarch of God, [Name], divinely blessed one.

*Theotokion:*  
The sacred prophetic choir from afar made manifest that thou shouldest be the true Mother of God, O pure one, higher than the Cherubim and all creation.

*Ode 3, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^106]  
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparion 1:*  
Divine sweetness dropped from thy lips, O father, and thou didst dry up the drops of bitter godlessness, having watered the pious with the drink of divine knowledge, O blessed one.

*Troparion 2:*  
Foreseen by the judgment of God, thou wast shown forth as a hierarch, offering bloodless sacrifices unto God Who was sacrificed for our sake, O holy father hierarch [Name].

*Troparion 3:*  
The grave wherein thy honorable body lies pours forth fragrance like a paradise of God, making the faithful fragrant, O hierarch most glorious and all-honorable, [Name].

*Theotokion:*  
The human mind cannot comprehend the ineffable depth of thy childbearing, O pure one; for God, humbling Himself for the sake of tender mercies, renewed me wholly in thy womb.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*[^107]  
Rightly believing, O venerable one, thou didst shepherd the Church of Christ, and didst drive away the bitter tares of heretics; thou dwellest in the highest, O blessed one.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
We have known the Word of the Father, Christ our God, incarnate of thee, O Virgin Mother of God, who alone art pure, who alone art blessed: therefore unceasingly singing thy praise, we magnify thee.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^108]  
Beholding Thy Son lifted up upon the Tree, O all-pure one, torn with maternal bowels, thou didst piteously cry aloud: Woe is me! How hast Thou set, O my Timeless Light?

*Ode 4, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^109]  
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the honorable Church sings in a manner worthy of God, crying aloud with a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparion 1:*  
Thou, being filled with the Holy Spirit, O holy father [Name], didst drive away crafty spirits from men, and didst make the faithful spiritual by thy teachings.

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou didst theologize the uncreated Unity, distinguished in three Persons, undivided and unconfused, enlightening the pious by thy sacred theologies.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having first put to death carnal mindsets by temperance and labors, thou wast shown forth as a divine hierarch and an all-holy intercessor before the Trinity.

*Theotokion:*  
The impending curse was destroyed through thee, O Mother of God: for thou, O all-pure one, didst bring forth for us the Fountain of sanctity of ever-flowing life.

*Ode 5, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^110]  
With Thy divine light, O good One, illumine, I pray, the souls of those who yearn early unto Thee with love, to know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, calling them from the darkness of sin.

*Troparion 1:*  
Divinely performing the things of God, and touching holy things with a most pure mind, thou didst appear blameless, serving God as a most sacred hierarch of God.

*Troparion 2:*  
Servants of idols became servants of God through thy honorable teachings; having taken them forth from the unworthy, thou didst show thyself a worthy servant unto God the Almighty.

*Troparion 3:*  
Called by grace, thou didst demolish the idolatrous godlessness; by the raining down of thy words, O venerable one, thou didst make famished hearts fruitful by the grace of God.

*Theotokion:*  
After thy marvelous childbearing, thou didst remain a Virgin as before: for He that was born was God, Who alters all things by His will, O unwedded Mary, Bride of God.

*Ode 6, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^111]  
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O most Merciful One!

*Troparion 1:*  
Thy tongue, sharpened with understanding by the Spirit like the pen of a ready scribe, wrote the graceful word in the hearts of the pious by the divine beckoning, O all-sacred father.

*Troparion 2:*  
Meditating on divine things, thus entering into the noetic Holy of Holies, thou dost perfect the faithful in the spirit with the radiance of the Trinity, [Name], O most consummate sacred father.

*Troparion 3:*  
By the streams of thy commands having dammed up the muddy dictates of the impious, thou wast shown forth as a river of peace, watering the companies of the faithful with piety, O all-honorable hierarch.

*Theotokion:*  
The Word dwelt in thy womb without seed, and appeared as a perfect man, renewing nature in a manner worthy of God, even as He alone knows, O divinely receptive maiden all-immaculate.

*Kontakion in the Typikon.*[^112]  
*If it is not in the Typikon, speak this Kontakion, Tone 2.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^113]  
Divine thunder, spiritual trumpet, planter of faith and cutter-off of heresies, well-pleasing servant of the Trinity, great hierarch [Name]: standing ever with the angels, pray unceasingly for us all.

*Ikos:*  
Wretched by slothfulness, I have fallen and slept unto death; but as a good shepherd raise me up, O father, and lull my passions which cruelly torment me, that having arisen I may purely hymn thy light-bearing solemnity, which the Master of the universe worthily glorified as His most faithful servant, as a most wise teacher, as a friend and initiate of mysteries, and as a steward of His traditions which thou didst keep well, an undefiled anointing, [Name], O most wise one: pray ever unceasingly for us all.

*Ode 7, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^114]  
The angel made the furnace drop dew for the venerable youths, while the decree of God, consuming the Chaldeans, persuaded the tormentor to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:*  
Illumined with spiritual grace through participation in the divine beckoning, thou wast a light-giving star, enlightening those who wisely sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:*  
Having become a most holy church, radiant with virtues of deeds, thou didst have the uncreated Trinity dwelling within thee, O sacred one, crying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion 3:*  
Having cast off slumber from thine eyes by divine vigilance, thou didst receive the splendors of God from an angel of light, showing thee forth as a pillar and support unto the faithful, a true hierarch.

*Theotokion:*  
He that sits uncircumscribed in the bosom of the Father now sits circumscribed in thy bosom, O all-pure one, arrayed in thy form: having been circumscribed for Adam's salvation.

*Ode 8, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^115]  
From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and the sacrifice of the righteous one Thou didst consume with water; for Thou doest all things, O Christ, simply by willing: Thee do we exalt unto all ages!

*Troparion 1:*  
By thy humility thou didst bring low the proud serpent, O blessed one, being elevated unto God by thy pure thoughts: therefore we honor thee, exalting Christ unto the ages.

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou didst theologize the incarnate Word, and didst save the people from the delusion of bondage, driving away idolatrous impieties, [Name], O father, hierarch most wise and God-bearer.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having thy life conforming unto thy Master, and having speech befitting thy life: being filled with spiritual days, O father, thou didst pass over unto the resting-places on high.

*Theotokion:*  
Delivered from the first curse by thy childbearing, O all-blessed Maiden rejoiced of God, we cry aloud unto thee with the voice of Gabriel: Rejoice, cause of the salvation of all!

*Ode 9, Tone 6. Heirmos:*[^116]  
It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate was shown forth unto men: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.

*Troparion 1:*  
Thou hast settled in the land of the meek, O hierarch, having been meek and gentle, joined with the heavenly hosts: wholly adorned with the brightness of virtues and robed with the light of good deeds.

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou beholdest the splendors of God and of the angels, the beauty of patriarchs, martyrs, and apostles: pray with them unto the Lover of mankind, to grant remission of sins and amendment of life unto us who praise thee, O venerable one.

*Troparion 3:*  
Thou didst radiantly adorn the throne that holds the primacy among all cities, wherein having lived as an angel and adorned it with the hierarchy, having anointed it thou didst sanctify it, perfecting a divinely wise people.

*Theotokion:*  
As a heavenly drop the rain descended into thy womb, O Virgin, and dried up the streams of delusion; rain down incorruption upon all men, and through thee deliverance was granted, O Maiden rejoiced of God.

*Exapostilarion.*  
*Podoben: "Our Savior has visited us" (Посѣти́лъ ны̀):*[^117]  
Gloriously adorned today is this radiant solemnity, as thou standest in the light of the glory of the face of God, O hierarch [Name]: remember us who praise thy memory.

*Theotokion:*  
Next to God, we place our hope upon thee, O all-pure one, being co-crucified with Christ Who came forth from thee: through thy supplications unto Him, preserve us unharmed even unto the end.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^118]

*Sticheron 1:*  
O venerable father [Name], having ascended the ladder of divine knowledge and drawn near unto God, receiving a gift from on high, thou healest incurable diseases and drivest away unclean spirits: therefore we celebrate thy memory, magnifying Christ with gladness of heart, Who has magnified His well-pleasing servant. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 2:*  
O marvelous father [Name], purely illumined by the light-beholding of the mind, thou didst lull to sleep the tempestuous sea of passions; and flying with the pure wings of dispassion, thou hast attained where beauty is ineffable and incomprehensible: ever praying for us who praise thee.

*Sticheron 3:*  
O father of fathers, [Name], rule of priesthood, icon of sobriety, strength of monastics, confirmation of the Church, lamp of love, throne of sensibility, fountain of wonders, and fire-inspired tongue, sweet-speaking mouth: thou wast a vessel of the divine Spirit and a noetic paradise, O divinely blessed one.

*Glory... Tone 6:*  
O venerable, thrice-blessed, most holy father, good shepherd, and disciple of Christ the Chief Shepherd, who didst lay down thy life for the sheep: do thou even now, all-praised [Name], O hierarch, ask through thy prayers that great mercy be granted unto us.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
We have known God Who took flesh of thee, O Virgin Mother of God: entreat Him for the salvation of our souls.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Ode 3 and Ode 6.*  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 1: My mouth shall speak of wisdom, and the meditation of my heart shall be of understanding (Ps. 48:4).*  
*Verse: Hear this, all ye nations; give ear, all ye inhabitants of the earth (Ps. 48:2).*  
*Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 318 (Heb. 7:26–8:2).*[^119]  
*Alleluia, Tone 2: The mouth of the righteous meditates on wisdom, and his tongue will speak of judgment (Ps. 36:30).*  
*Verse: The law of His God is in His heart, and His steps shall not trip (Ps. 36:31).*  
*Gospel according to John, Pericope 36 (John 10:9–16).*[^120]  
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be; he shall not be afraid of evil tidings (Ps. 111:6).*

## Common Service to Two and Many Hierarchs[^121]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried": Stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^122]

*Sticheron 1:*  
Firmly established as stars of many lights, O all-praised ones, ye adorned the world, O all-honorable ones, and illuminate it with rays, the dogmas of the true faith; and ye drive away the darkness of heresy, O hierarchs: pray ye, that those who celebrate your all-honorable memory with faith may be delivered from corruption and perils.

*Sticheron 2:*  
Having enlightened the inhabited earth with spiritual splendors, ye appeared as light-giving suns; as ye extend rays of divine commands unto all ends, ye enlighten the hearts of the faithful, O most blessed God-bearers, driving away the darkness of heresies by the power of Him Who dawned from the Virgin.

*Sticheron 3:*  
From the Church of Christ, O most blessed ones, ye drove away noetic wolves with the staff of your commands: and ye encompassed her round about with rational firmness, and presented her unto Christ whole and unshaken. Pray ye unto Him, that those who celebrate your all-honorable memory with faith may be delivered from corruption and perils.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Deliver my humble soul from condemnation, O all-holy Bride of God, and from cruel transgressions; and snatch me from death through thy prayers, and grant that I may obtain justification in the day of trial, which the assemblies of the saints have attained; show me forth cleansed before the end by repentance and by the outpourings of tears.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^123]  
Beholding Christ the Lover of mankind crucified and pierced in the ribs with a spear, the all-pure one wept, crying aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee in return for the good things Thou didst for them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O Compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: At "Lord, I have cried": Stichera on 6.*  
*Glory... Tone 6:*  
Men of God and faithful servants, ministers of the Lord, men of desires, chosen vessels, pillars and confirmation of the Church, heirs of the Kingdom: keep not silent to cry aloud unto the Lord for us!

*Both now... Theotokion (Tone 6): "Who will not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin?..." (see fol. 234v).*[^124]  
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the readings:*

*Reading from the Proverbs (Prov. 10:7, 3:13–16, composite):*[^125]  
The memory of the righteous is with praises, and the blessing of the Lord is upon his head. Blessed is the man that finds wisdom, and the mortal that knows prudence. For it is better to buy her than treasures of gold and silver. And she is more precious than costly stones; and everything precious is not worthy of her. For length of days and years of life are in her right hand, and in her left hand are riches and glory. Out of her mouth proceeds righteousness, and law and mercy she carries upon her tongue. Give ear therefore unto me, O children, for I will speak honorable things; and blessed is the man that will keep my ways: for my outgoings are the outgoings of life, and desire is prepared from the Lord. For this cause I entreat you, and put forth my voice unto the sons of men: for I, wisdom, have built; counsel, knowledge, and understanding have I called upon. Mine is counsel and confirmation; mine is understanding, and mine is strength. I love them that love me, and they that seek me shall find grace. Understand craftiness therefore, ye simple ones, and apply your hearts, ye uninstructed. Give ear unto me again, for I will speak honorable things; and I will open right things from my lips, for my throat shall meditate on truth, and lying lips are an abomination before me. All the words of my mouth are with righteousness; there is nothing crooked nor perverse in them. All things are right unto them that understand, and plain to them that find knowledge. For I will teach you truth, that your hope may be in the Lord, and ye may be filled with the Spirit.

*Reading from the Proverbs (Prov. 10:31–32, 11:1–12):*[^126]  
The mouth of the righteous drops wisdom, but the tongue of the unrighteous shall perish. The lips of righteous men drop graces, but the mouth of the impious is perverse. False balances are an abomination before the Lord, but a just weight is acceptable unto Him. Wheresoever insolence enters, there also is dishonor; but the mouth of the humble meditates on wisdom. The perfection of the upright shall guide them, but the overthrow of the rebellious shall despoil them. Possessions shall not profit in the day of wrath, but righteousness shall deliver from death. When the righteous man dies, he leaves regret; but easy and laughed to scorn is the destruction of the impious. The righteousness of the blameless directs paths, but unrighteousness falls into impiety. The righteousness of upright men shall deliver them, but the lawless are taken in their own foolish counsel. When a righteous man dies, his hope does not perish; but the boast of the impious perishes. The righteous man escapes from the snare, and in his stead the impious is delivered up. In the mouth of the impious is a snare to citizens, but the perception of the righteous is prosperous. In the good prosperity of the righteous a city is set right, and at the destruction of the impious there is rejoicing. By the blessing of the upright a city is exalted, but by the mouth of the impious it is overthrown. A man void of understanding scoffs at citizens, but a wise man keeps silence.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wis. 4:7–15):*[^127]  
Though the righteous happen to die, he shall be in rest. For honorable age is not that which stands in length of time, nor is it measured by number of years; but wisdom is the gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is the age of old age. Having been well-pleasing unto God, he was beloved, and living among sinners he was translated; he was snatched away, lest wickedness should alter his understanding, or deceit beguile his soul. For the bewitching of wickedness obscures good things, and the roaming of desire alters the innocent mind. Being made perfect in a short time, he fulfilled long years; for his soul was pleasing unto the Lord: therefore hastened He to take him away from the midst of wickedness. And the people saw it, and understood not, neither laid they up this in their minds: that grace and mercy are upon His venerable ones, and visitation upon His elect.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "O praised martyrs" (Похва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*[^128]

*Sticheron 1:*  
Together let us worthily praise today the spiritual initiates of mysteries and noetic trumpets of God, divine mirrors, the divine and fire-inspired hierarchs, who pour forth for us golden streams of teachings: Entreat Christ, to grant unto our souls peace and great mercy.

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6).*

*Sticheron 2:*  
Worthily with hymns let us praise the degree of faith by understanding, divine and vigilant, golden-flowing rivers, all-radiant lamps, champions of the Trinity, receptacles of the grace of the Holy Spirit, unshakable pillars and confirmations of the Church.

*Verse: Thy priests shall clothe themselves with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice (Ps. 131:9).*

*Sticheron 3:*  
Spiritual instruments of the thunder of divine beckoning, lightnings of proclamation, all-golden candlesticks, radiant and light-bearing in God, O most blessed hierarchs: entreat Christ for us who honor you, to grant unto our souls peace and great mercy.

*Glory... Tone 6:*  
Today let us praise the spiritual trumpets of the Spirit, the God-bearing fathers, who sang in the midst of the Church a harmonious song of theology, the one unchanging Trinity, Essence and Godhead: destroyers of heresies and champions of the orthodox, who ever pray unto the Lord that our souls may be shown mercy.

*Both now... Theotokion (Tone 6): "My Creator and Redeemer..." (see fol. 234v).*[^129]  
*If thou dost not celebrate, speak this Theotokion:*  
O Mother of God, thou art the true vine that didst bud forth for us the Fruit of life: we pray thee, O Sovereign Lady, intercede together with the hierarchs, that our souls may be shown mercy.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^130]  
Standing by the Tree at the time of the Crucifixion, the Virgin, with the virgin disciple, weeping, cried aloud: Woe is me! How dost Thou suffer, O Christ, Who art the dispassion of all?

*Troparion in the Typikon.*[^131]  
*If it is not in the Typikon, speak this Troparion, Tone 4:*[^132]  
O God of our fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy meekness: take not Thy mercy away from us, but through their prayers guide our life in peace.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion.*  
*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": the same Troparion.*  
*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5.*  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-unoriginate Word" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*[^133]  
As the adornment of the Church, let us all praise the hierarchs of the Lord, an unfailing treasury of dogmas; for by them He Himself instructed us to honor the Holy Trinity: united in Essence, yet divided in Hypostases.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
O all-holy Virgin, have mercy upon us who flee with faith unto thee, merciful one, and who ask thy fervent protection; for as good one thou canst save all, as being Mother of the most high God, by thy maternal prayers ever sheltering us, O Maiden rejoiced of God.

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3.*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры):*[^134]  
Ye appeared as pillars of the Church and an unstealable wealth of piety, O divinely blessed hierarchs; in dispassion ye lived your life, and clarified the dogmas of the Trinity, O venerable fathers: entreat Christ God, that our souls may be saved. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
He was not separated from the divine nature when He became flesh in thy womb; but God became man and remained what He was: Who preserved thee after childbirth as a Virgin even as prior to childbirth, O all-immaculate one, the only Lord: Him do thou entreat fervently, to grant unto us great mercy.

*At the Polyeleos, Megalynarion:*  
We magnify you, O great hierarchs, and we honor your holy memory: for ye pray for us unto Christ our God.  
*Selected Psalm: Hear this, all ye nations (Ps. 48:1).*[^135]

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*  
Let the most wise teachers of the universe, who have glorified God by deeds and words upon earth, be magnified today: as they are intercessors of salvation for us. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
O invincible mediatress of the afflicted, and fervent protection of those who hope in thee: deliver me from perils, for thou art the helper of all.

*Anabathmoi, the first Antiphon of Tone 4.*[^136]  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6).*  
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me? (Ps. 115:3).*  
*Let every breath praise the Lord (Ps. 150:6).*  
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 38 (Matt. 10:32–33, 10:37–38, 19:27–30).*[^137]  
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*  
O venerable fathers, into all the earth has gone out the proclamation of your right deeds; therefore in the heavens ye found the reward of your labors: ye destroyed the legions of demons and attained the ranks of angels, whose life ye blamelessly emulated: having boldness before the Lord, ask peace for our souls.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos:*  
*Canon of the Theotokos on 4, and of the saint on 8.*[^138]  
*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^139]  
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea; for He alone has been gloriously glorified.

*Troparion 1:*  
Walking in the world, O most wise hierarchs, shining with the divine commandments of the King of all Who reigns: therefore let us sing unto Christ, for He is glorified.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having been enlightened in the world with the light of piety, and having driven away the darkness of impiety: let us honor them, O faithful, as great and all-honorable hierarchs.

*Troparion 3:*  
As ye stand with the angels before the inaccessible King, pray for those who celebrate your memory, to grant unto us cleansing of sins, as ye are God-bearing sacred preachers.

*Theotokion:*  
Having conceived the timeless and unoriginate Word of the Father, and having brought Him forth in the flesh beyond word, O all-pure one: pray fervently that we may be delivered from perils.

*Ode 3, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^140]  
Thou Who didst establish the heavens in the beginning with understanding, and didst found the earth upon the waters: establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments; for there is none holy save Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion 1:*  
By true temperance and steadfast prayer, ye ascended into the city on high of virtues, O venerable and God-bearing ones, where ye ever enjoy the torrent of delight.

*Troparion 2:*  
As ye have boldness before God, O venerable hierarchs: pray for us, that we may be saved, who sing of your memory with faith.

*Troparion 3:*  
As teachers of pious commands, of most wise divine words, narrators of truth: ye received victory over heresies, O venerable ones.

*Theotokion:*  
As heaven and the throne of God we all hymn thee, O pure Virgin Mother of God: for from thee Jesus Christ the truth was shown forth, for our salvation.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*  
Pure vessels of temperance and unstealable wealth of piety were ye shown forth truly, O blessed ones; having enlightened your life with dispassion, and enriching those in need with mercy, O venerable fathers: entreat Christ God, to grant unto us great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Whithersoever one is saved, thither he rightly flees: and what other such refuge is there like thee, O Mother of God, who coverest our souls?

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^141]  
Having acquired the Cross of thy Son as a rod of power, O Mother of God: thereby we cast down the insolence of enemies, ceaselessly magnifying thee with love.

*Ode 4, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^142]  
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy divinity.

*Troparion 1:*  
Let us all praise the most wise hierarchs in songs, saying: O God-bearing fathers, fervently pray that we may be saved.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having you truly as declarers and planters of pious commands, and as most wise teachers and hierarchs, O divinely blessed ones: we praise you with hymns and songs.

*Troparion 3:*  
Your sacred memory, O faithful, as guides and nurturers, venerable hierarchs of the Lord: let us celebrate with hymns and songs.

*Theotokion:*  
Unwedded bride, and all-pure Mother of Christ God who knewest not wedlock: unceasingly entreat Him to save the souls of those who hymn thee.

*Ode 5, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^143]  
Rising early, we cry unto Thee: O Lord, save us; for Thou art our God, besides Thee we know none other.

*Troparion 1:*  
Pastorally anointed with the myrrh of piety, O divinely wise ones: therefore let us all honor you as hierarchs, O God-bearers.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having been imitators of the apostles, O glorious divinely wise ones, by labors and vigils, protectors of the people of the Church.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having emulated Elijah the zealot, and Moses the beholder of God, O God-beholding fathers, ye put to shame the leaders of heresies.

*Theotokion:*  
As the Queen that gave birth unto the King of all, come, all ye faithful, with one accord let us honor the blessed one with the angel.

*Ode 6, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^144]  
Grant me a robe of light, O Thou Who deckest Thyself with light as with a garment, O most merciful Christ our God.

*Troparion 1:*  
Ye adorned the glory of the Church, O blessed fathers, by your divine commands, and cut down heresies from the root.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having shone forth into all creation as lamps of piety by the beauty of commands, glorious ones, through teachings, having darkened heresies by the Word of God.

*Troparion 3:*  
Taking tongue and word instead of a weapon, O venerable ones, ye cut off all alien teachings, and made clear the Trinity plainly unto all.

*Theotokion:*  
Born of the Virgin, Thou hast enlightened the world, O Christ God: and deliver me from my transgressions, as Lover of mankind, and direct my life, I pray Thee.

*Kontakion in the Typikon.*[^145]  
*If it is not in the Typikon, speak this Kontakion, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "As first-fruits" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*[^146]  
As teachers of virtues and adornment of hierarchs, the Church singing your praise glorifies you: through your prayers grant unto those who honor you with love the amendment of virtues and deliverance from scandals, as ye are unconquerable.

*Ikos:*  
Rivers of piety were ye shown forth, watering all the earth with the streams of your teachings, as divine hierarchs, and washing away defilements with torrents of wonders, and having worthily inherited the stream of delight. Therefore, having come together piously, we honor you with songs today, and faithfully cry unto you: Entreat Christ God unceasingly for us all, as ye are unconquerable.

*Ode 7, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^147]  
Thou Who didst establish the earth in the beginning, and didst found the heavens by the Word: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:*  
Thou Who didst show forth true divine hierarchs as shepherds unto Thy flock: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:*  
Ye who quenched the flame of passions, and divinely refreshed the souls of the pious, who cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion 3:*  
Having labored by temperance and vigilance and true faith, O hierarchs: remember us all.

*Theotokion:*  
He that was born for our sake of the Virgin, and delivered the world from the enemy: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Ode 8, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^148]  
Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and by fire in the bush revealed unto Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin: praise the Lord, and exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion 1:*  
Emulating Moses, O fathers, ye ascended the mountain of dispassion into the noetic and graceful darkness, and received the law, O hierarchs: therefore we sing of Christ unto the ages.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having shepherded the people of Christ, O venerable ones, fleeing the Egyptian deceit, ye escaped the passionate Babylon, having found the dwelling of Sion on high unto all ages.

*Troparion 3:*  
As ye were anointed with myrrh, O hierarchs of God, which is piety in word and deed: ye minister sacredly divine things, O consecrated ones. Therefore ye sing unto the Trinity in one Essence unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*  
Him Who dwelt in the womb of the holy Virgin, and therein terribly beyond word renewed the ancient Adam: praise the Lord, and exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^149]  
The childbearing of the Ever-Virgin revealed on the mountain to the lawgiver in fire and the bush, for the salvation of us the faithful, with unceasing hymns we magnify.

*Troparion 1:*  
Ye who by dispassion and faith, hope and love, showed forth unto us the words of eternal life: O most wise hierarchs, pray that our souls may be saved.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having shepherded the people with the rod of the Holy Spirit, and having driven away from the Church of God the heresies of God-fighting men, O all-honorable hierarchs: therefore we praise you with songs.

*Troparion 3:*  
As sacred church lamps, we all ceaselessly honor in songs and hymns the shrine of your relics, O blessed fathers, worthily magnifying you.

*Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, ever-living fountain of incorruption! Rejoice, all-radiant cloud of the Sun! Rejoice, chariot of all the Godhead! Rejoice, ark of sanctification!

*Exapostilarion.*  
Let us praise the God-bearing fathers, light-bearing lamps, exceeding the rays of the sun, from the radiant Trinity and three-sun Unity, supernaturally blended.

*Theotokion:*  
Thee with unceasing hymns we call blessed, O Virgin, who didst receive in the womb One of the Trinity, O Mother of God, and in thy divine arms didst bear the transcendent Word, without change and without alteration.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 6.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ):*[^150]

*Sticheron 1:*  
Grace has prevailed, faith has been strengthened, and all things are filled with the knowledge of God; for the sake of the apostles and teachers, we have been enriched with salvation. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 2:*  
A good concourse of heavenly mysteries and human teachings, with right actions overcoming every heretical word: Thou hast made Thy hierarchs marvelous, O Lord.

*Sticheron 3:*  
Let the most wise teachers of the universe, who glorify God by words and deeds upon earth, be magnified today: as the bestowers of salvation unto us all.

*Glory... Tone 6:*  
Good, faithful, and worthy servants, good laborers in the vineyard of Christ, who also bore the burden of the day and increased the talent given unto you, and envied not those who came after you: therefore also the heavenly gates were opened unto you; having entered into the joy of Christ the Master, pray for us, O holy teachers!

*Both now... Theotokion (Tone 6): "O Mother of God, thou art the true vine..." (see fol. 29r).*[^151]  
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Ode 3 and Ode 6.*  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6).*  
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me? (Ps. 115:3).*  
*Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 335 (Heb. 13:17–21).*[^152]  
*Alleluia, Tone 2: Thy priests shall clothe themselves with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice (Ps. 131:9).*  
*Gospel according to John, Pericope 35 from the midpoint (John 10:1–9).*[^153]  
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be; he shall not be afraid of evil tidings (Ps. 111:6).*

## Common Service to a Single Venerable[^154]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried": Stichera on 8.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Martyrs of the Lord" (Мꙋ́ченицы Гдⷭ҇ни):*[^155]

*Sticheron 1:*  
A righteous and honorable lamp, divine and light-bearing, is never hidden beneath the bushel of worldly thickness: but upon the heights of exalted wonders the Lover of mankind places him: Through his prayers, O Christ, grant unto Thy people great mercy.

*Sticheron 2:*  
Upon the plow thou didst place the labor of thy hands, O all-honorable one, ever working divine things, and thou didst not turn back looking behind: but thou wast directed into the Kingdom of Christ the incarnate God, for the salvation of our souls.

*Sticheron 3:*  
Carried in the bodily vessel, by the gentle breathings of the gentle Spirit, thou didst easily traverse the gulf of life, O wise one: and having sold thy possessions for the pearl of great price, and having found it, thou didst retain it within thyself: having become blessed by His divine beauties.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Impure are my thoughts, deceitful my lips, and all my deeds defiled: and what shall I do? How shall I meet the Judge? O Sovereign Lady Virgin, entreat thy Son, Creator, and Lord, that in repentance He may receive my spirit, as He alone is compassionate.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^156]  
The undefiled Heifer, beholding the Bullock voluntarily nailed upon the Tree, weeping piteously, cried aloud: Woe is me, O most beloved Child! What has the ungrateful Jewish assembly rendered unto Thee, wishing to make me childless of Thee, O all-beloved One?

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: At "Lord, I have cried": Stichera on 6.*  
*Glory... Tone 6:*  
Having preserved that which is according to the image unharmed, and having ascetically established the mind as master over destructive passions, thou didst ascend as far as possible unto that which is according to the likeness: for having manfully constrained nature, thou didst hasten to subordinate the worse to the better, and to enslave the flesh to the spirit. Therefore thou wast shown forth as a guide of monastics, a desert-dweller, a trainer of those who run well, a most accurate rule of virtue; and now in the heavens, the mirrors being dissolved, O our father [Name]: thou beholdest purely the Holy Trinity, praying unceasingly for those who honor thee with faith and love.

*Both now... Theotokion (Tone 6): "Who will not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin?..." (see fol. 234v).*[^157]  
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the three readings:*  
*(See in the Service of a Prophet, fol. 1v).*[^158]  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^159]

*Sticheron 1:*  
Brighter than the sun, thy feast, O God-bearer, illumines those who come unto thee with faith, makes fragrant with incorruption, and pours forth healings unto souls, O venerable [Name], intercessor for our souls.

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6).*

*Sticheron 2:*  
Having conquered sensory bodily passions with the clusters of temperance, O wonderworker [Name], and having shown the zeal of the bodiless upon earth, thou didst subordinate all carnal desires to the spirit: therefore now dwelling in the heavenly mansions, pray for our souls.

*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord; in His commandments he will desire greatly (Ps. 111:1).*

*Sticheron 3:*  
O blessed [Name], having laid the foundation of virtue, thou didst put off the old man with his lusts, and didst put on Christ in truth: therefore thou didst also refute many enemy battle-arrays, O venerable one, having been a guide of monastics: pray that our souls may be saved.

*Glory... Tone 8:*  
We honor thee as the guide of multitudes of monastics, O our father [Name]: for by thy path we have learned truly to walk uprightly. Blessed art thou who didst labor for Christ, and didst refute the power of the enemy, companion of angels, partaker with the venerable and the righteous: with them pray thou unto the Lord, that our souls may be shown mercy.

*Both now... Theotokion (Tone 8): "O unwedded Virgin, who God..." (see fol. 234v).*[^160]  
*If thou dost not celebrate, speak this Theotokion:*  
Pure Virgin, gate of the Word, Mother of our God: pray that we may be saved.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^161]  
I cannot bear, O Child, beholding Thee fallen asleep upon the Tree, Who bestowest wakefulness unto all: that Thou mayest grant divine and saving wakefulness unto those who of old fell asleep in the sleep of perdition through the fruit of transgression: the Virgin, weeping, said, whom we magnify.

*Troparion in the Typikon.*[^162]  
*If it is not in the Typikon, speak this Troparion, Tone 8:*[^163]  
In thee, O father, was preserved accurately that which is according to the image: for taking up the Cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by deed didst teach to despise indeed the flesh, for it passes away; but to care for the soul, a thing immortal: therefore also with the angels thy spirit rejoices, O venerable [Name].

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": the same Troparion.*  
*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^164]  
Thou didst follow the calling of thy Lord, having cast away the world and all the fair things in the world, O all-blessed [Name]; thou didst endure wilderness affliction fervently, and didst manfully repel the legions of demons: therefore we faithfully praise thy memory ever with hymns. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Thou didst renew by thy divine childbearing, O pure one, the mortal substance of earthborn men that was corrupted by passions; and thou didst raise all from death unto the life of incorruption: therefore as is meet we all call thee blessed, O all-glorified Virgin, even as thou didst foretell.

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5.*  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-unoriginate Word" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*[^165]  
Let us honor the faster of the Lord with hymns, as one who truly put to death all the assaults of passions by temperance and steadfast endurance, and greatly put to shame the adversary enemy and all his pride: and who now prays unto the Lord, that our souls may be shown mercy. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
The awesome wonder of conception and the ineffable manner of childbearing were made known in thee, O pure Ever-Virgin; it awes my mind and amazes my comprehension: thy glory, O Mother of God, is spread out unto all, for the salvation of our souls.

*At the Polyeleos, Megalynarion:*  
We call thee blessed, O venerable father [Name], and we honor thy holy memory, instructor of monastics and companion of angels.  
*Selected Psalm: With endurance I waited for the Lord (Ps. 39:1).*[^166]

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й, Спа́се):*[^167]  
Thou didst bury all the insurrection of passions by the mortification of the flesh, O sacred father; thou didst receive unending life after death: therefore today the Church of Christ celebrates thy most marvelous memory, O adornment of the venerable. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Guide us into the path of repentance, who ever turn aside into evil trackless wastes and provoke the all-good Lord, O unwedded, blessed Mary, refuge of desperate men, dwelling-place of God.

*Anabathmoi, the first Antiphon of Tone 4.*[^168]  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6).*  
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me? (Ps. 115:3).*  
*Let every breath praise the Lord (Ps. 150:6).*  
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 43 (Matt. 11:27–30).*[^169]  
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*  
O venerable father, into all the earth has gone out the proclamation of thy right deeds; therefore in the heavens thou hast found the reward of thy labors: thou didst destroy the legions of demons and attain the ranks of angels, whose life thou didst blamelessly emulate: having boldness before Christ God, ask peace for our souls.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos:*  
*Canon of the Theotokos on 4, and of the saint on 8.*[^170]  
*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^171]  
Traversing the water as on dry land, and fleeing the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Unto our Deliverer and God let us sing!

*Troparion 1:*  
Adorned with good manners, thou didst cleave unto Christ from youth from youth, having mortified carnal passions by temperance, and unto life thou wast translated, O venerable one.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having been a fulfiller of the words and laws of God, thou wast filled, O most wise father, with divine gifts and wonders, and didst richly send forth rays unto all.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having prevailed by the strength of Christ, thou didst cast down the dominion and power of the enemy: and thou didst receive the prize of victory, O venerable one, through marvelous splendors.

*Troparion 4:*  
Thou hadst a good conscience, and the eye of the heart looking unto God: therefore He gave heed unto Thy prayer, O most wise one, and joined thee with the venerable.

*Theotokion:*  
Assuage the passions of my flesh and the improper motions of my mind, I pray thee, O all-pure Virgin, and set me right who have strayed in mind.

*Ode 3, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^172]  
Thou art the confirmation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.

*Troparion 1:*  
Wholly cleaving unto the Almighty, O venerable and most wise father, thou didst escape all demonic malice.

*Troparion 2:*  
Adorned with the height of humility, thou didst cast down to the earth the boastful one by thine excellent deeds.

*Troparion 3:*  
Encompassed with flesh, O most wise father, thou didst bring low the exalted neck of the impious one by the humility of thy words.

*Troparion 4:*  
Having the power of God assisting Thee, O most wise one, as marvelous thou pourest forth wonders and drivest away diseases.

*Theotokion:*  
In thy womb was the Creator of the universe incarnate, O all-immaculate one, for the blessing of those who faithfully hymn thee.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^173]  
Thou didst subdue the passionate carnal mindset, and having enslaved the worse to the better, O our father [Name], all-glorious one, thou didst shatter demonic wiles by fasting, and didst shine forth in the world like a sunbeam by the splendors of thy virtues: therefore we hymn thee.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
An invincible wall art thou unto us Christians, O Virgin Mother of God: for fleeing unto thee we abide unharmed, and sinning again we have thee as an intercessor: therefore with thanksgiving we cry unto thee: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^174]  
O all-immaculate Virgin Mother of Christ God, a sword pierced through thy most holy soul when thou didst behold Thy Son and God voluntarily crucified: Whom, O all-blessed one, cease not to entreat to grant us remission of transgressions.

*Ode 4, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^175]  
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy divinity.

*Troparion 1:*  
Having made thy soul a temple of the Holy Spirit, O all-glorious one, thou wast an heir of the Kingdom on high, with the hosts on high.

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou ever healest multi-painful passions of those who draw near unto thee: for having received grace from the Lord, O venerable one, to work signs and wonders.

*Troparion 3:*  
With most fair blossoming thou didst blossom, O father, in the house of God, adorned with virtues and made fragrant with marvelous fruits.

*Theotokion:*  
O spiritual field, as an ear of corn from the furrow thou didst bring forth Him Who nourishes all creation, the God of all, O Ever-Virgin.

*Ode 5, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^176]  
Rising early, we cry unto Thee: O Lord, save us; for Thou art our God, besides Thee we know none other.

*Troparion 1:*  
With a cleansed mind, O all-glorious one, thou beholdest the ineffable beauty of Christ, the God of all.

*Troparion 2:*  
Even as Elijah on the chariot, O father, by thy virtues thou didst ascend unto heaven, being lightened by the Spirit.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having braced thy body by temperance and purity, thou didst attain, O father, unto the mansions on high in breadth.

*Theotokion:*  
Heal the darkening of my diseased mind, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady, who didst give birth unto Christ the Physician.

*Ode 6, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^177]  
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my griefs; for my soul is full of evils, and my life has drawn near unto hades, and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion 1:*  
The crafty serpent was put to death by thy sacred prayers, O divinely blessed one, and from him the malice of those who ask signs from thee is destroyed: for thou art a most radiant well-pleasing servant of God, having been known as faithful.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having renewed thy soul with a diligent plow, O sacred father, thou didst most wisely cast into it fruitful seeds of virtue, and didst reap rich ears of diverse healings.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having prevailed by the strength of the Spirit, O father, thou didst cast down the dominion and power of the enemy: and thou didst receive the prize of victory, O venerable one, through marvelous splendors.

*Theotokion:*  
The Lord is with thee, O all-pure Maiden: even as He willed, being born, He delivered all of us from the domain of the deceitful one through thy mediation: therefore now as is meet generations of generations call thee blessed.

*Kontakion in the Typikon.*[^178]  
*If it is not in the Typikon, speak this Kontakion, Tone 2.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^179]  
Having armed thyself divinely with spiritual purity, and having firmly placed unceasing prayers into thy hand as a spear, thou didst pierce through demonic battle-arrays, O our father [Name]: pray unceasingly for us all.

*Ikos:*  
Having loved the divine commandments of Christ, O venerable one, and having hated the enjoyment of this world, thou didst come fervently unto Him, and wast a lamp enlightening the ends of the earth with spiritual radiance. Therefore falling down I pray thee: Illumine my spiritual eyes, to hymn thy struggles, fasting and vigilance, tears, afflictions, and exhaustion of body, for the sake of the blessed future life, which thou now enjoyest: pray unceasingly for us all.

*Ode 7, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^180]  
The youths coming from Judea in Babylon of old trampled the flame of the furnace by faith in the Trinity, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:*  
Having traversed ascetic struggles with a steadfast mind, O father, thou didst bring low the proud mind by divine humility, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:*  
As a most fair blossom, O father, thou didst blossom in the house of God, adorned with virtues and made fragrant with fruits of wonders.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having enlightened thy heart, thou wast the leader of a sacred assembly, O father, instructing and teaching, and bringing all unto the will of God, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*  
Having given birth unto a young Child, the Word Who is before all ages, thou didst renew us who were grown old by sin, O Virgin, and didst strengthen us to sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^181]  
The God-inspired youths in the furnace, trampling the flame with fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion 1:*  
O heir of divine mansions, thou didst live like an angel, O father: therefore thy spirit rejoices with the angels.

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou didst journey without turning back, O most wise and most marvelous father, by the divine paths that lead thither, having turned away completely from those that lead evilly.

*Troparion 3:*  
When grace took up its abode in thy soul, O father, the unclean spirits that craftily dwell in men were driven away.

*Theotokion:*  
Thou art an inexhaustible water, O Virgin, from which we all drink and are filled with grace, being sanctified in soul and body.

*Ode 9, Tone 8. Heirmos:*[^182]  
Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God: that the Most High voluntarily descended even unto the flesh, becoming man from a virgin womb: therefore, O faithful, we magnify the all-pure Mother of God.

*Troparion 1:*  
Thy honorable shrine richly pours forth healing by the Spirit of God, and heals long-standing passions, O father, of those who draw near unto thee, and drives away cruel evil spirits: and it raises up the faithful to praise thy radiant struggles.

*Troparion 2:*  
As a great sun thou hast shone forth unto us by the greatness of thy struggles, and hast illuminated all ends of the earth, O divinely wise one. Having died, thou hast passed from light unto the Light that has dawned: therefore we cry unto thee: Illumine our thoughts, O venerable father [Name]!

*Troparion 3:*  
Thy patient body, O blessed one, having been declared with chains, heals incurable passions by a touch; God and the Savior having greatly glorified thee, O most wise and most marvelous father [Name], as one who glorified Him by thy good deeds, O venerable one.

*Troparion 4:*  
Thou didst blossom in the ascetic valleys, O our all-praised father, like a fragrant rose, and like a lily thou didst perfume the consciences of the faithful with virtues and wonders: therefore, O venerable one, drive away from us foul-smelling passions.

*Theotokion:*  
Illumine, O pure Virgin, my heart that ever grieves through transgressions and many worldly clamors; leave me not to be a joy unto enemies, that I may glorify thee and hymn thee with love, O all-hymned one.

*Exapostilarion.*  
*Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны ᲂу҆слы́шите):*[^183]  
Thou didst blossom like a palm tree according to David, O father, and didst appear as a dwelling of the all-holy Spirit, and He showed thee forth glorious unto the universe: pray unceasingly for us who honor thy all-honorable memory with faith, O venerable [Name].

*Theotokion:*  
Thee with unceasing hymns we call blessed, O Virgin, for thou didst give birth unto One of the Trinity, O Mother of God, and dost bear in thy divine arms the all-rich Word, without change and without alteration.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 6.*  

*Sticheron 1:*  
O all-blessed God-bearer [Name], thou didst subordinate all carnal wisdom to the spirit, having been established by the sufferings of fasting; like gold tried in the furnace shining thou wast shown forth, thou wast a receptacle of the all-holy Spirit: having gathered multitudes of monastics, and by thy teachings, as by a certain ladder ascending unto heaven, thou didst lead them up unto the height of virtues: remember us who ever honor thy sacred memory, and pray that our souls may be saved. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 2:*  
Today thy all-radiant and all-festal memory has shone forth, O all-glorious [Name], summoning a multitude multitudinous assembly of fasters, and choirs of venerable ones; and angels together with men, truly unto the praise of Christ our God Who is worshipped in the Trinity. Therefore, fleeing unto the sacred shrine of thy relics, we richly receive gifts of healing, and we glorify Christ Who has crowned thee, the Savior of our souls.

*Sticheron 3:*  
O all-blessed God-bearer, our father [Name], thou didst prophetically wash the earth with tears, and gavest no sleep to thine eyes, nor slumber to thine eyelids, displaying heartfelt desire unto Christ Whom thou lovedst. Therefore thou wast an example for monastics, and didst fulfill every form of virtue. Therefore we call thee blessed, magnifying Him Who has glorified thee.

*Glory... Tone 2:*  
O venerable father, having diligently trained thyself in virtue from infancy, thou wast an instrument of the Holy Spirit; and having received from Him the working of wonders, thou didst persuade men to disdain sweet things. And now, most purely illumined by divine Light, enlighten also our thoughts, [Name], our father.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
All my hope I place upon thee, O Mother of God: preserve me under thy shelter.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Ode 3 and Ode 6. Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*[^184]  
Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6).  
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me? (Ps. 115:3)*  
*Epistle to the Galatians, Pericope 213 (Gal. 5:22–6:2). Alleluia, Tone 6:*  
Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, in His commandments he will greatly delight (Ps. 111:1).  
*Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon the earth (Ps. 111:2).*  
*Gospel from Matthew, Pericope 43 (Matt. 11:27–30); or Luke, Pericope 24 (Luke 6:17–23).*  
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be (Ps. 111:6).*[^185]

## Common Service to Two and Many Monastics[^186]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^187]

*Sticheron 1:*  
O venerable and divinely wise fathers, ye give light unto the blind, healing unto the sick, and health unto the lame, prayerfully inclining unto those who draw near with faith unto your temple and ask your help; for we, all the faithful, have found you as protectors and intercessors, praising you.

*Sticheron 2:*  
O all-honorable venerable fathers, having ascended upon the chariot of virtues, ye ran swiftly unto God, receiving the prizes of victory; and as a mercy unto us ye have left your bodies laid in graves, O fathers, to pour forth healings and drive away deceitful spirits: therefore we call you blessed, O all-praised ones.

*Sticheron 3:*  
Having joined the choirs of fasters, adorned, O blessed ones, by an ascetic life, ye now dwell rejoicing in the heavenly mansions, where the angels keep festival; and being truly deified by divine light, remember in love those who call you blessed upon the earth and celebrate your holy festival.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
My thoughts are unclean, and deceitful my lips, and all my deeds are defiled; and what shall I do? How shall I meet the Judge? O Sovereign Lady Virgin, pray thy Son and Creator and Lord, that He may receive my spirit in repentance, as He alone is compassionate.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^188]  
The immaculate Heifer, beholding the Bullock voluntarily nailed upon the Tree, weeping piteously, cried aloud: Woe is me! cried aloud: O most beloved Child, what has this ungrateful Jewish assembly rendered unto Thee, wishing to make me childless of Thee, O all-beloved?

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: On "Lord, I have cried", Stichera on 6.*  
*Glory... Tone 6:*  
O venerable fathers, into all the earth has gone out the proclamation of your righteous deeds: therefore in the heavens ye found the reward of your labors, ye destroyed ranks of demons, ye attained the orders of angels, whose life ye blamelessly emulated; having boldness toward the Lord, ask peace for our souls.

*Both now... Theotokion (Tone 6): "Who would not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin..." (see fol. 234v).*[^189]  
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And Readings, 3.*  
*(See in the Service to a Prophet, fol. 2).*[^190]  

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бля):*[^191]

*Sticheron 1:*  
Having put on Christ, having divinely put off the corruptible man: therefore by spiritual grace, ever illuminated like bright stars, ye shone forth unto the world, praying that those who ever celebrate your honorable memory with faith may be delivered from corruption and perils.

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6).*

*Sticheron 2:*  
Now receiving delight, and encircled with light, and inheriting eternal life: deliver from perils and various temptations and every affliction those who flee unto you, having divinely acquired boldness toward God, and displaying love that is truly Christ-like.

*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, in His commandments he will greatly delight (Ps. 111:1).*

*Sticheron 3:*  
O all-marvelous God-bearers, champions of the Trinity, having deployed yourselves with manly wisdom for battle against the world-ruler, ye cast him down by firm wrestlings, and were bound with victorious wreaths: therefore we celebrate your divine and light-bearing memory, illumined by grace.

*Glory... Tone 8:*  
O venerable fathers, having utterly hated the beauty of the world and fleeting food, and having loved the monastic life above all, ye were companions of the angels, and all-radiant lamps unto the universe; like a second sun ye shine by wonders: but remember all of us who celebrate your sacred memory. For we are your children, and the sheep of your rational teachings; we call upon you for help, asking through you to receive peace and great mercy.

*Both now... Theotokion (Tone 8): "O unwedded Virgin..." (see fol. 234v).*[^192]  
*If thou dost not celebrate, speak this Theotokion:*  
I, O holy Virgin Mother of God, flee unto thy protection; I know that through thee I shall find salvation, for thou art able, O pure one, to help me.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^193]  
Beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and voluntarily accepting sufferings, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, cried aloud: Woe is me, sweet Child! How dost Thou suffer unrighteous wounds, O Physician Who didst heal human infirmity, and by Thy tender compassion didst deliver all from corruption?

*Troparion in the Typikon.*[^194]  
*If there is no Typikon, speak this Troparion, Tone 4:*[^195]  
O God of our fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy meekness: take not Thy mercy away from us, but through their prayers guide our life in peace.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion.*  
*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": the same Troparion.*  
*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^196]  
All-radiant lamps of the truth of Christ, enlighten the world enlighten by your teaching, O God-inspired fathers, having destroyed the heresies of ill-believing blasphemers; ye quenched the flaming confusions of those who blaspheme: therefore as servants of Christ ye enlighten all. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
O all-immaculate Virgin, who didst give birth unto the most radiant God: with His saints unceasingly entreat Him, to grant remission of transgressions and amendment of life unto us before the end, who sing to thee as is meet with faith and love, O only all-hymned one.

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*  
Ye abstained from the flesh for the sake of Christ, and having appeared as spiritual leaders unto men, hear the words of secret teachings; and ye appeared unto monastics as a rule of uprightness, having trampled down all passions by strength: therefore having utterly burned up the wiles of demons, pray ever pre-eminently unto Him, O God-bearing fathers, to grant remission of sins unto those who honor your holy memory with love. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Generations of generations glorify thee, even as thou didst foretell, O Maiden; for thou wast the palace of the Creator of all, and a divine temple, wherein the Most High dwelt and was clothed with flesh, that He might save us.

*At the Polyeleos, Refrain (Megaplynarion):*[^197]  
We call you blessed, O venerable fathers, and we honor your holy memory: instructors of monastics, and companions of angels.  
*Selected Psalm: With patience I waited for the Lord (Ps. 39:1).*[^198]

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^199]  
When, O blessed ones, ye winged your mind toward divine desire, then ye abandoned all mindsets dragging downward, and settling in the wilderness, like a lily ye blossomed forth, most fair in virtues; having uprooted the thistles of passions, therein ye planted the fruits of your labors: therefore having also reaped sheaves of unshakeable wealth in the heavens, O venerable fathers, entreat Christ God, to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor your holy memory with faith. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion: "All creation rejoices in thee, O full of grace..."*[^200]  
All creation rejoices in thee, O full of grace: the angelic assembly, and the human race; hallowed temple, and rational paradise, glory of virginity, of whom God was incarnate and became a young child, He Who is our God before all ages: for He made thy womb a throne, and made thy body more spacious than the heavens. In thee all creation rejoices, O full of grace: glory to thee!

*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], the first Antiphon of Tone 4.*[^201]  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*  
Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6).  
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me? (Ps. 115:3)*  
*Let every breath praise the Lord (Ps. 150:6).*  
*Verse: Praise God in His saints (Ps. 150:1).*  
*Gospel from Matthew, Pericope 43 (Matt. 11:27–30).*  

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*  
Angels upon earth and men of God in heaven, good adornment of the world, and decoration of good nurturers; praise of monastics and venerable ones: let us honor the great fathers. For being planted in the house of the Lord, they blossomed righteously, and like cedars in the desert ye multiplied the flock of Christ, of rational sheep, in holiness and righteousness.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: The Canon of the Theotokos on 4, and of the Venerables on 8.*[^202]  
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^203]  
Come, ye people, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea, and guided the people whom He brought out of Egyptian bondage, for He has been glorified.

...and guided the people whom He brought out of Egyptian bondage, for He has been glorified.

*Troparion 1:*  
Illumined with the radiance of the tri-radiant Godhead, O all-wise ones, ye appeared as luminaries, truly enlightening those who honor your sacred and light-bearing memory.

*Troparion 2:*  
Ye all commended yourselves unto the Creator, and directed all spiritual desire unto Him: therefore ye received spiritual grace.

*Troparion 3:*  
Illumined with the light of grace, O venerable ones, enlighten those who celebrate your memory with faith, and by your prayers deliver them from the darkness of sin.

*Theotokion:*  
Having given birth in the flesh unto the Incorporeal One, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, wash away our carnal and spiritual defilements by the sprinkling of thy divine prayer.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^204]  
Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death, and plant the fear of Thee in the hearts of us who hymn Thee.

*Troparion 1:*  
Having watered your souls with streams of tears, O venerable ones, ye made them fruitful, bringing forth every virtue, O all-glorious displays of wonders.

*Troparion 2:*  
Your divine temple works healing, and drives away human diseases, and strengthens their understanding to praise your all-honorable labors.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having of old fought against the enemy by temperance, and now by strength having set up victory over him, O venerable ones, ye manfully endured and cast down the raging of demons.

*Theotokion:*  
From thy womb, O Virgin, the great Sun shone forth all-gloriously, and having enlightened earthly things, received the choir of venerable ones.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*[^205]  
Having crossed the sea of this life without toil by temperance, ye reached the noetic harbor of dispassion, O venerable and divinely wise fathers.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Virginity and childbearing beyond nature and word came together in thee, O Mother of God; for thou didst give birth unto God incarnate, the Savior of our souls.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^206]  
Beholding thy Son lifted up upon the Tree, O all-pure one, torn with motherly anguish, thou didst cry aloud: Woe is me! Whither hast Thou set, O my ageless Light?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^207]  
I have heard, O Lord, Thy glorious dispensation, and I glorified Thy unsearchable power, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion 1:*  
Having loved the humility of Christ, ye were exalted by dispassion, and brought low all demonic pride.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having shone forth like a sun from the east, O venerable ones, by the rays of wonders ye enlighten the whole universe under the sun.

*Troparion 3:*  
A censer of sweet fragrance unto the All-seeing One, O venerable ones, ye brought your prayers, which, as He accepted them, He disposes in your ascetic festival.

*Theotokion:*  
The choir of venerable ones and every soul of the faithful call thee blessed, O all-pure one; for beyond mind and word thou didst give birth unto the Word of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^208]  
O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments: for besides Thee we know none other God.

*Troparion 1:*  
With your souls ye cleaved unto the one love of Christ, and having hastened to be doers of the commandments of God, ye received the grace of many wonders, O all-glorious ones.

*Troparion 2:*  
Guileless and meek, ye destroyed all the malice of the enemy, and appeared merciful unto the end and from the one Merciful One ye received true and saving grace.

*Troparion 3:*  
By the law and prayer and fasting ye ever endured, and were clothed in dispassion, having subdued the carnal mindset by the strength of the Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*  
Sing a new song unto the Lord, and sing unto the Name that has divinely shone forth from the womb of the unwedded one, and has shown unto the faithful salvation, a firm hope and praise.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^209]  
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unsearchable abyss of Thy loving-kindness: lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion 1:*  
By the sprinkling of your great struggles, O fathers, ye overcame the faces of demons, well-pleasing unto the Lord Who was crucified for our sake, O venerable ones.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having acquired goodly dispositions, O fathers, ye richly received a gift from God the Benefactor, and this ye grant unto those who are in need, O venerable ones.

*Troparion 3:*  
Filled with spiritual gifts, O fathers, ye subjected the hostile spirits; as noetic and understanding ones, ye cleaved utterly unto God.

*Theotokion:*  
Heal the passions of my soul, who wast the wellspring of dispassion, having enlightened the choirs of venerable fathers by thy childbearing.

*Kontakion in the Typikon.*[^210]  
*If there is no Typikon, speak this Kontakion, Tone 2.*
