*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^211]  
Passing dry-shod through the multitude of waves, by the streams of your tears ye strongly drowned the incorporeal foes, O divinely wise and venerable ones; and having received the gift of wonders, pray unceasingly for us all.

*Ikos:*  
By the great multitude of your virtues, O venerable ones, being like a fair and full garden, and having blossomed as from paradise by fasting and prayers, ye perfume all with the fragrance of your many labors, ascetic struggles, and monastic sweats, in which ye passed piously unto the sorrowless life, and were crowned with victorious wreaths: pray unceasingly for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^212]  
Unto the golden image worshipped in the plain of Dura, Thy three youths paid no heed to the godless decree; but cast into the midst of the fire, bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion 1:*  
By the armor of your prayers, O venerable ones, having overcome deceitful demons, ye received grace from heaven to heal diseases and drive away deceitful spirits from those who cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:*  
Having subjected your flesh unto the spirit, O wise fathers, ye lived in good temperance, all-praised in doing good, having a pure conscience and an innocent life, crying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion 3:*  
Entering by the true path into divine rest, ye avoided demonic tribulations, O blessed ones, covered by divine grace, crying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*  
The flaming sword that of old guarded the gates of Eden, O pure Virgin, turns its back to all who have ascetically cast down the fighting enemy, ever crying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^213]  
Unto Him Who of old prefigured the wonder of the Virgin unto Moses in the bush on Mount Sinai: praise, bless, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

The drops of your tears quenched the fire of sin, and showed forth unto all of us a river of wonders that drowns every passion of those who flee unto you, O all-glorious fathers.

*Troparion 2:*  
Indestructible faith and hope and true love ye possessed, O fathers, and merciful diligence, good patience, and meditation upon spiritual words, humility of mind, and perfect guilelessness.

*Troparion 3:*  
Ye struggled well, having conquered all workings of the enemy, and at the end of life ye were crowned, O wise ones, and were numbered with all the venerable: with whom we honor you and exalt Christ unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*  
Save me, O Mother of God, who didst appear unto the world as the beginning of salvation, delivering me from the tumult of unclean passions and every adverse affliction, that I may glorify thee unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^214]  
God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had fallen grievously through food into corruption, and Who for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin: with one mind, O faithful, with hymns we magnify Him.

*Troparion 1:*  
Behold, the Kingdom of heaven has opened unto you, O fathers, who have finished the good course; ye see what the angels see, and the reward of labors grants you honors, God honoring you: therefore, O holy fathers, we call you blessed.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having pleased God with a contrite heart, ye shattered demonic wiles, and ye set right those who were broken by them, O God-bearers: therefore we call you blessed with faith, bowing down before the shrine of your relics, O divinely blessed ones.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having your mind directed upward toward God, O God-bearing venerable fathers, ye abandoned earthly things and received heavenly things, for the sake of your labors and unflagging temperance: therefore we honor you.

*Theotokion:*  
Spare me, O Lord, spare me when Thou art about to judge me, and condemn me not unto the fire, nor rebuke me in Thy fury: the Virgin who gave birth unto Thee, and the angelic hosts, and the assemblies of the venerable entreat Thee, O Christ.

*Exapostilarion.*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "He has visited us" (Посѣти́лъ ны є҆́сть):*[^215]  
Ye blossomed like a palm tree according to David, O fathers, and appeared as dwellings of the Holy Spirit, and were shown forth glorious unto the universe: pray unceasingly for us who honor your all-honorable memory with faith, O venerable ones.

*Theotokion:*  
Thee with unceasing hymns we call blessed, O Virgin, for thou didst give birth unto One of the Trinity, O Mother of God, and dost bear in thy divine arms the all-rich Word, without change and without alteration.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^216]

*Sticheron 1:*  
Having shone forth more brightly than the sun, O venerable ones, your all-festal memory flashes with the rays of your virtuous deeds, O blessed ones, illuminating the senses of the faithful with the light of your wonders: celebrating this, we joyfully praise you and faithfully call your all-festal memory blessed. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 2:*  
Like other earthly angels ye lived, having worn down your body by temperance, O all-glorious ones; with vigilance and remembrance of death diligently meditating, the divine fathers grew in great ascents toward the supreme desire: therefore ye truly drew near unto the very cornerstone, Christ.

*Sticheron 3:*  
Carnal passions ye stilled by temperance and diligent entreaty; by streams of tears ye choked the deceitful serpent choked the deceitful serpent, O venerable fathers; and ye were well-pleasing unto God above others, O worthy of wonder: therefore Jesus, the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls, adorned you with heavenly gifts.

*Glory... Tone 8:*  
O venerable fathers, having meditated day and night in the law of the Lord, ye were accounted worthy to enjoy the Tree of life; and the fruit of your suffering blossomed into wreaths: having boldness toward God the Judge of contests, ask also for us cleansing and great mercy.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
O Sovereign Lady, receive the prayer of thy servants, and deliver us from every distress and sorrow.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Ode 3 and Ode 6. Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*[^217]  
Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6).  
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me? (Ps. 115:3)*  
*Epistle to the Galatians, Pericope 213 (Gal. 5:22–6:2). Alleluia, Tone 6:*  
Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, in His commandments he will greatly delight (Ps. 111:1).  
*Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon the earth (Ps. 111:2).*  
*Gospel from Matthew, Pericope 43 (Matt. 11:27–30); or Luke, Pericope 24 (Luke 6:17–23).*  
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be (Ps. 111:6).*[^218]

## Common Service to a Single Martyr[^219]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мч҃нцы):*[^220]

*Sticheron 1:*  
With uprightness of heart having understood the unchanging God, O all-honorable one, in Him thou didst believe without doubting, O blessed one; having kept His ineffable saving commandments, and having suffered eminently and patiently, O all-wise one, thou didst receive an unshakable kingdom.

*Sticheron 2:*  
With thine honorable blood thou didst sanctify the earth, and didst abolish unclean sacrifices unlawfully offered unto demons, and didst receive an incorruptible wreath upon thy head, O all-honorable one: therefore pray that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls.

*Sticheron 3:*  
We are sanctified who praise thy sacred contests and struggles, O all-honorable martyr [Name], by which thou wast a fellow citizen with the angels, and a partaker with all the passion-bearers: with whom pray that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Woe is me! What shall become of me, having defiled mind and soul and body with transgressions? What shall I do? And how shall I escape the unendurable flame, the unloosed and eternal bonds? But O all-immaculate one, before the end pray thy Son to grant me remission.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^221]  
Beholding her own Lamb upon the Cross having neither form nor beauty, the immaculate Ewe and Sovereign Lady, weeping, cried aloud: Woe is me! Whither has Thy beauty set, O Sweetest One? Where is the comeliness? Where is the sparkling grace of Thy countenance, O my most beloved Son?

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: On "Lord, I have cried", Stichera on 6.*  
*Glory... Tone 6:*  
Today the whole universe is enlightened with the rays of the passion-bearer, and the Church of God, adorned with flowers, cries aloud unto thee, O martyr [Name]: O servant of Christ and most fervent protector, cease not to pray for thy servants.

*Both now... Theotokion (Tone 6): "Who would not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin..." (see fol. 234v).*[^222]  
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And Readings* 3.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Isa. 43:9–14):*[^223]  
Thus says the Lord: All the nations were gathered together, and princes shall be gathered out of them: who will declare these things? or who will declare unto you things from the beginning? Let them bring forth their witnesses, and let them be justified: and let them give ear, and speak the truth. Be ye My witnesses, and I also am a witness, says the Lord God, and My servant whom I have chosen: that ye may know, and believe Me, and understand that I am He: before Me there was no other God, neither shall there be after Me. I am God, and there is none that saves besides Me. I have declared, and have saved; I have reproached, and there was no strange god among you: ye are My witnesses, and I am a witness, says the Lord God, even from the beginning, and there is none that can deliver out of My hands: I will work, and who shall turn it back? Thus says the Lord God Who redeems you, the Holy One of Israel.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wis. 3:1–9):*[^224]  
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was taken for misery, and their going from us to be utter destruction: but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality. And having been a little chastised, they shall be greatly rewarded: for God proved them, and found them worthy for Himself. As gold in the furnace has He tried them, and received them as a whole burnt offering. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and run to and fro like sparks among the stubble. They shall judge the nations, and have dominion over the people, and their Lord shall reign forever. They that put their trust in Him shall understand the truth, and such as be faithful in love shall abide with Him: for grace and mercy are upon His venerable ones, and visitation upon His elect.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wis. 4:7–15):*[^225]  
Though the righteous happen to die, he shall be in rest. For honorable age is not that which stands in length of time, nor is it measured by number of years; but wisdom is the gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is the age of old age. Having been well-pleasing unto God, he was beloved, and living among sinners he was translated; he was snatched away, lest wickedness should alter his understanding, or deceit beguile his soul. For the bewitching of wickedness obscures good things, and the roaming of desire alters the innocent mind. Being made perfect in a short time, he fulfilled long years; for his soul was pleasing unto the Lord: therefore hastened He to take him away from the midst of wickedness. And the people saw it, and understood not, neither laid they up this in their minds: that grace and mercy are upon His venerable ones, and visitation upon His elect.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бля):*[^226]

*Sticheron 1:*  
When the cruel tormentor delivered thy much-suffering and patient body unto evil torments to be punished with manifold tortures, O divinely minded martyr [Name]: thou didst not deny Christ, nor didst thou sacrifice unto idols; but thou didst endure as if another were suffering, awaiting future recompense and immortal theology.

*Verse: The righteous shall flourish like a palm tree, and shall be multiplied like a cedar in Lebanon (Ps. 91:12).*

*Sticheron 2:*  
Suspended and raked, pelted with stones, wounded with iron, and dispatched by the sword, thou didst remain unbending, having fixed thy soul upon the noetic rock. And therefore thou wast joined unto the angelic choirs, being accounted worthy of the unwaning Light.

*Verse: Planted in the house of the Lord, they shall flourish in the courts of our God (Ps. 91:13).*

*Sticheron 3:*  
Gifts of healing thy relics pour forth unto those who desire them, O glorious [Name], drowning passions and diseases, and destroying the pillars of deceitful demons; and they water the hearts of the faithful, budding forth divine fruits of virtues and the understanding of piety.

*Glory... Tone 6:*  
Come, all ye lovers of the martyrs, let us piously praise the passion-bearer of Christ who has finished his course, [Name] the glorious; for he crushed the serpent's head, and sanctified the earth with blood, and was translated from things here unto eternal dwellings; and having received the prize of struggles from the Almighty Hand, he asks cleansing for our souls, and great mercy and great mercy.

*Both now... Theotokion (Tone 6): "O Mother of God, thou art the true vine..." (see fol. 234v).*[^227]

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^228]  
When the all-pure one beheld Thee suspended upon the Cross, weeping with a mother's grief she cried aloud: O my God, my sweetest Child, how dost Thou endure this shameful suffering?

*Troparion in the Typikon.*[^229]  
*If there is no Typikon, speak this Troparion, Tone 4:*[^230]  
Thy martyr, O Lord, [Name], in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down tormentors and shattered the powerless audacity of demons: through his prayers save our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion.*  
*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": the same Troparion.*  
*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́ггелскїй):*[^231]  
Having hated the earthly army, O martyr, thou didst desire the glory that is in heaven, and didst endure afflictions and a violent death: therefore today we celebrate thy all-holy memory, offering praise unto Christ, O all-blessed [Name]. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Truly we all entreat thee as Mother of God and Virgin; we cry unto thy compassions, fleeing with love unto thy goodness: for we sinners have thee as an intercessor, and in temptations have acquired thee as salvation, O only all-pure one.

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*[^232]  
Having finished the true course, thou didst conquer all the strength of the tormentors, and didst receive a crown from the hand of the Ruler of all, O all-honorable [Name], and wast shown to be a partaker with the angels. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
We have known the Word of the Father, Christ our God, Who was incarnate of thee, O Virgin Mother of God, only pure, only blessed one: therefore unceasingly singing to thee we magnify thee.

*At the Polyeleos, Refrain (Megaplynarion):*[^233]  
We magnify thee, O holy passion-bearer [Name], and we honor thy precious sufferings, which thou didst endure for Christ.  
*Selected Psalm: God is our refuge and strength (Ps. 45:1).*[^234]

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*  
The invincible warrior of Christ, the valiant victor over enemies, who has shone forth in great wonders, let us all ever praise with faith, [Name] the Passion-bearer; for he pours forth healings unto those who draw near with faith, and assuages cruel passions, and protects men who are in tribulations. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
An invincible rampart art thou unto us Christians, O Virgin Mother of God; for fleeing unto thee we abide unharmed, and sinning again we have thee as an intercessor: therefore with thanksgiving we cry unto thee: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], the first Antiphon of Tone 4.*[^235]  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*  
The righteous shall flourish like a palm tree, and shall be multiplied like a cedar in Lebanon (Ps. 91:12).  
*Verse: Planted in the house of the Lord, in the courts of our God (Ps. 91:13).*  
*Let every breath praise the Lord (Ps. 150:6).*  
*Gospel from Luke, Pericope 63 (Luke 12:2–12).*  

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*  
Today the whole universe is enlightened with the rays of the passion-bearer, and the Church of God, adorned with flowers, cries aloud unto thee, O martyr [Name]: O servant of Christ and most fervent protector, cease not to pray for thy servants.

...O servant of Christ and most fervent protector, cease not to pray for thy servants.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: The Canon of the Theotokos on 4, and of the Saint on 8.*[^236]  
*Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^237]  
Christ, Who shatters battles with a high arm, shook horse and rider into the Red Sea, and saved Israel as they sang a song of victory.

*Troparion 1:*  
Standing before the throne of God with boldness, O divinely blessed one, as an invincible sufferer, by thy prayers preserve those who celebrate thy holy suffering with love.

*Troparion 2:*  
Having living water abiding in thy soul, O holy one, thou didst dry up muddy streams of malice, O all-glorious one, strengthened by the grace of the Savior, O divinely blessed one.

*Troparion 3:*  
Strengthened by the power of God, O divinely blessed one, thou wast able to shatter the haughtiness of the enemy; and thou wast an eminent victor, and a citizen of the city on high.

*Theotokion:*  
Show thy tender mercy, O all-pure Lady, upon me, the wretched one, fervently praying unto thy Son, O all-holy one, that by thy prayers we may escape eternal fire.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^238]  
Thou Who by Thy command didst fix the earth upon nothing, and didst suspend its unsupportable weight, establish Thy Church upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments, O Christ, Who alone art good and the Lover of mankind.

*Troparion 1:*  
Departing from the mire of passions with wisdom, and drawing near unto God by divine purities, thou didst receive divine illumination, showing thee forth, O worthy of glory, as a son of light, working the work of light.

*Troparion 2:*  
Earthly warfare and great glory thou didst count as nothing, O divinely blessed one: therefore thou didst enlist under Christ, and wast numbered among the ranks of martyrs, rejoicing, O blessed God-bearing martyr [Name].

*Troparion 3:*  
The paths of thy journey, O worthy of wonder, were directed unto a peaceful way, entering into the Kingdom of God, into the breadth of paradise, and unto eternal rest, O glorious one.

*Theotokion:*  
Having given birth unto the Master and Creator of all creation, thou didst appear, O pure Sovereign Lady, more honorable than the cherubim and seraphim: therefore all generations call thee blessed as is meet.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5.*  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́лное):*[^239]  
Illumined with splendors of virtues and adorned with the light of martyrdom, more than the sun thou shinest steadfastly, O most wise one, and truly enlightenest those who faithfully celebrate thy light-bearing memory, O all-glorious passion-bearer [Name]: save those who venerate thy relics.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
The terrible wonder of thy conception and the ineffable manner of thy childbearing were known in thee, O pure Ever-Virgin; it terrifies my mind and astounds my thought. Thy glory, O Mother of God, has spread unto all, for the salvation of our souls.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^240]  
By the Cross of thy Son, O woman full of the grace of God, all idolatrous deceit was abolished, and the power of demons was trampled down: therefore we, the faithful, as is meet ever hymn and bless thee, and truly confessing thee as the Mother of God, we magnify thee.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^241]  
Understanding Thy divine emptying, Habakkuk cried aloud unto Thee with trembling, O Christ: Thou hast come for the salvation of Thy people, to save Thine anointed.

*Troparion 1:*  
Adorned with love and hope toward the Lord, O most wise one, thou wast martyrologically enlightened, O all-blessed one, and wast a fellow citizen with the angels, O all-glorious one.

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou wast immovable and unyielding, living in exile and deprived of thine own kin for the sake of the Master of all, God, O divinely blessed and most wise one.

*Troparion 3:*  
Having fought the good fight, O glorious one, adorned with the beauties of martyrdom, thou hast passed over unto the invisible beauties, reaping the honors of thy labors.

*Theotokion:*  
He Who on high is understood with the Father, below is incarnate of thee for our sake, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady, through the abundance of His compassion, the only Good One, the Lover of mankind.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, early in the morning I rise unto Thee, and unto Thee I cry: Enlighten my darkened soul, O Christ, as Thou alone art tenderhearted.

*Troparion 1:*  
Strengthened by the laws of the Almighty, thou didst abolish the counsels of the lawless; and having lawfully suffered, O martyr, thou didst receive the crown of incorruption.

*Troparion 2:*  
The coals of deceit thou didst quench, O most wise one, with streams of thy blood; and now, pouring forth waters of healing, thou drivest away the flame of sickness by the grace of God.

*Troparion 3:*  
Thou wast able, O martyr, in bodily infirmity to destroy the multi-scheming serpent, and by wisdom didst cast down the curse of his manifold deceit.

*Theotokion:*  
Put to death, O all-immaculate Virgin, I pray thee, the soul-corrupting passions of my flesh, thou who didst give birth in the flesh unto the Source of passionlessness, Christ God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Calm, O Master Christ, the sea of passions raging with a soul-corrupting tempest, and lead me up from corruption, as Thou art tenderhearted.

*Troparion 1:*  
Thou didst stand at the tribunal of the lawless, O martyr of Christ, [Name], and didst uproot all deceit; thou wast judged for the sake of Christ, and didst courageously refute them.

*Troparion 2:*  
Adorned, O most wise martyr of Christ, with divine beauties in thy suffering: therefore Christ enlightened thee, and thou hast remained incorrupt.

*Troparion 3:*  
With blood thou didst sanctify the earth, and with thy sacred soul thou didst illumine heaven, O all-blessed one, and didst fill the martyric choirs with joy.

*Theotokion:*  
Taught by the Spirit, the Prophet foresaw thee as the gate through which God passed when He became incarnate, leaving it again sealed, O all-immaculate one.

*Kontakion in the Typikon.*[^242]  
*If there is no Typikon, speak this Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^243]  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^244]  
A radiant star hast thou appeared, unfailing unto the world, proclaiming the Sun, Christ, with thy rays, O passion-bearer [Name]; and all deceit hast thou extinguished: and unto us thou impartest light, praying unceasingly for us all.

*Ikos:*  
Grant me song, O my God, to hymn and speak and honor the struggles of Thy passion-bearer and martyr, that I may piously praise him who was valiant in suffering, [Name]: who was ever a victor over passions, great in piety, and shone forth in the assembly of martyrs; among whom the most wise one unceasingly hymns Christ with the multitude of angels, receiving divine radiance from on high, and praying unceasingly for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The all-exalted Lord of the fathers quenched the flame and bedewed the children as they sang in unison: Blessed art Thou, O God.

*Troparion 1:*  
Carrying thy suffering body, O pious peoples, much-suffering and most wise one, they cried aloud with thanksgiving: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou didst destroy Hellenic oblations by the grace of God; and being loosed, O blessed one, from the bond of bodily things, thou didst end thy life for Christ through martyrdom, having lawfully suffered.

*Troparion 3:*  
As upon a chariot thou didst mount upon thy blood, O praiseworthy passion-bearer, and rejoicing thou didst ascend unto celestial resting-places.

*Theotokion:*  
Thou art the glory and confirmation unto all, O pure one, who confess thee, O all-immaculate one, as Mother unto God Who came forth from thy womb beyond nature.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Unto Thee, the Creator of all, the children in the furnace, having woven a universal choir, sang: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion 1:*  
Thou wast an heir of the Jerusalem on high, having died in exile, O most wise one, singing unceasingly: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto ages.

*Troparion 2:*  
When many sorrows came upon thee, O blessed one, now sorrowless life has received thee, where thou criest: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto ages.

*Troparion 3:*  
Thou wast able by the all-effective power of God, O martyr, to destroy Hellenic deceits and all-crafty wisdom, and to receive an incorruptible crown from the Master of all.

*Theotokion:*  
The all-bountiful Word received flesh from thee, O pure one, and was united unto mankind; pray Him, O all-holy one, to put to death the stirrings of my flesh, and to save my soul.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Rejoice, O Isaiah: a Virgin has conceived in the womb, and born a Son, Emmanuel, both God and Man, Orient is His name; Whom magnifying, we call the Virgin blessed.

*Troparion 1:*  
Come, assembled together, ye faithful: let us celebrate today the sacred festival of the all-blessed and sacred martyr of Christ, [Name]; for as a sufferer he conquered all the multitudes of the enemy by divine power.

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou wast adorned, O glorious one, with the divine beauties of martyrdom, and didst ascend as upon a fiery chariot, rejoicing in thy blood; thou hast attained unto the heavens, to look upon the ineffable beauty of our Savior.

*Troparion 3:*  
Thou wast shown to be an eminent warrior of Christ God, and a valiant sufferer: therefore now thou dwellest in the kingdom on high, gloriously bound with a crown of victory, O all-praised passion-bearer.

*Theotokion:*  
Having given birth unto Christ, the Light unto those who are in darkness, enlighten my blinded soul, and grant me to walk along the paths that lead unto life, O all-holy Lady, guide unto all who hymn thee.

*Exapostilarion (Photogogikon):*[^245]  
As the sun, radiantly with miraculous beams dost thou guide all creation, O all-glorious [Name], passion-bearer: therefore celebrating thy memory, we ask Him Who rose from the grave to deliver all from tribulations.

*Theotokion:*  
By thy sovereign protection, O pure one, preserve us thy servants ever unharmed from the assault of the enemy; for thee alone have we acquired as a refuge in tribulations.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^246]

*Sticheron 1:*  
The peoples praise thy glorious memory with psalms and hymns together, O [Name]; for it has shone forth comely and light-bearing, adorned with glory and grace: therefore now the ranks of angels dance, and martyrs together with apostles praise the martyric sufferings of thy struggles. And they hymn and they hymn Him Who has glorified thee, the Savior Christ our God; pray Him to save and enlighten our souls.

*Sticheron 2:*  
Clothed in the full armor of Christ, thou wast found, O [Name], by those who sought thee not; for being kindled with fire for Christ, and mocking then the godless fury of vain gods, thou didst soldier for my King Christ, crying unto the lawless: Neither wild beasts, nor wheels, nor any other torment shall ever be able to separate me from the love of my Christ! Pray Him to save and enlighten our souls.

*Sticheron 3:*  
Heeding not the manifold instruments, the various torments, and fearful weapons, O crown-bearer [Name], thou didst finish the martyric course of piety: therefore we crown thy most radiant memory with flowers of songs, and with faith kiss thy precious relics. But as thou standest before the throne of the Master, Christ our God, cease not to pray that He may save and enlighten our souls.

*Glory... Tone 4:*  
O boast of martyrs, having raised radiant victories over bodily deceit, thou bearest thy glory meekly everywhere through thy desire, O all-blessed one, as a victor, [Name]; and now raise up from falls into sin those who in faith honor thy memory.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, O Virgin Mother of God, for thou didst give birth unto the heavenly King, the Savior and Enlightener of our souls.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Ode 3 and Ode 6. Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*[^247]  
The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him (Ps. 63:10).  
*Verse: Hear, O God, my voice, when I make my prayer unto Thee (Ps. 63:1).*  
*Epistle to Timothy, Pericope 292 (2 Tim. 2:1–10). Alleluia, Tone 4:*[^248]  
The righteous shall flourish like a palm tree (Ps. 91:12).  
*Gospel from Luke, Pericope 63 (Luke 12:2–12); or John, Pericope 55 (John 15:17–16:2).*[^249]  
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be (Ps. 111:6).*[^250]

## Common Service to Two and Many Martyrs[^251]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бла):*[^252]

*Sticheron 1:*  
Illumined by the Holy Spirit, O all-praised ones, ye set yourselves in battle array against the world-ruler, and courageously conquered his wiles, being moved against him by the command of God. Therefore, celebrating today your most radiant memory, we honor your suffering with praises as is meet.

*Sticheron 2:*  
Surrendering yourselves to wounds and torments, O holy ones, ye remained immovable in wisdom; kindled like lamps with spiritual warmth, ye enlightened the hearts of the faithful by grace. Therefore every race and age celebrates your holy memory, glorifying the Lord with songs.

*Sticheron 3:*  
By the brilliance of your wonders, O martyrs, ye have enlightened all creation, driving away the mists of passions and tribulations from those who flee unto you and ask your protection in faith, O valiant sufferers of the Savior: therefore in faith we celebrate your holy and light-bearing festival.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Bent down by demonic assaults and cast into the pit of destruction, have compassion on me, O Sovereign Lady, and establish me upon the rock of virtues; and having destroyed the assaults of the enemy, make me worthy to do the commandments of thy Son and our God, that I may obtain remission in the day of judgment.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^253]  
Beholding Christ the Lover of mankind crucified and pierced in the side with a spear, the all-pure one wept, crying aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have ungrateful people rendered unto Thee instead of the good things Thou didst for them? And Thou strivest to make me childless, O most beloved One! I marvel, O tenderhearted One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: On "Lord, I have cried", Stichera on 6.*  
*Glory... Tone 3:*  
Behold how good and how pleasant is the fraternal love of Thy martyrs, O Christ God! For bodily birth did not make them brothers, but brother-loving faith taught them to be of one mind even unto blood: through their prayers, O Christ God, save our souls.  
*Both now... Theotokion:*  
*How can we not marvel at thy theandric childbearing:*[^254]

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the Readings.*[^255]  
*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Isa. 43:9–14):*[^256]  
Thus says the Lord: All the nations are gathered together, and princes shall be gathered out of them: who will declare these things? Or who will declare to you things from the beginning? Let them bring their witnesses, and let them be justified; and let them hear, and let them speak the truth. Be ye My witnesses, and I too am a witness, says the Lord God, and My servant whom I have chosen: that ye may know and believe Me, and understand that I am He; before Me there was no other God, and after Me there shall be none. I am God, and beside Me there is no savior. I have declared and have saved, I have reproached, and there was no strange god among you: ye are My witnesses, and I am a witness, says the Lord God, even from the beginning; and there is none that can deliver out of My hands: I will work, and who shall turn it back? Thus says the Lord God Who redeems you, the Holy One of Israel.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wis. 3:1–9):*[^257]  
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was taken for misery, and their going from us to be utter destruction: but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality. And having been a little chastened, they shall be greatly rewarded: for God proved them, and found them worthy for Himself. As gold in the furnace hath He tried them, and received them as a whole burnt offering. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and run to and fro like sparks among the stubble. They shall judge the nations, and have dominion over the people, and the Lord shall reign in them unto ages. They that put their trust in Him shall understand the truth, and such as be faithful in love shall abide with Him: for grace and mercy are upon His venerable ones, and visitation among His elect.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wis. 5:15–6:3):*[^258]  
The righteous live unto ages; their reward also is with the Lord, and the care of them is with the Most High. Therefore shall they receive a kingdom of glory, and a crown of beauty from the hand of the Lord: for with His right hand shall He cover them, and with His arm shall He shield them. He shall take to Him His jealousy for complete armor, and make the creature His weapon for the vengeance of His enemies. He shall put on righteousness as a breastplate, and shall take true judgment instead of a helmet. He shall take holiness for an invincible shield. His severe wrath shall He sharpen for a sword, and the world shall fight with Him against the unwise. Then shall the right-aiming thunderbolts go abroad, and from the clouds, as from a well-drawn bow, shall they fly to the mark. And hailstones full of wrath shall be cast as out of a stone bow, and the water of the sea shall rage against them, and the floods shall cruelly drown them. Yea, a mighty wind shall stand up against them, and like a storm shall blow them away: thus iniquity shall lay waste the whole earth, and ill dealing shall overthrow the thrones of the mighty. Hear therefore, O ye kings, and understand; learn, ye that be judges of the ends of the earth. Give ear, ye that rule the people, and glory in the multitude of nations. For power is given you of the Lord, and sovereignty from the Most High.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бла):*[^259]

*Sticheron 1:*  
As stars of many lights, O all-praised passion-bearers, with divine splendors ye illumine the ends of the universe, delivering them from demonic darkness and soul-destroying passions and tribulations. Therefore today, coming together, let us praise your radiant, light-bearing, and holy festival, O glorious ones.

*Verse: The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them (Ps. 33:17).*  
*Sticheron 2:*  
Let the marvelous, divine, and most wise passion-bearers be honored with sacred hymns: for sacredly proclaiming the uncreated Trinity before enemies, they quenched the deceit of polytheism with the shedding of their blood, and received unfading glory.

*Verse: Many are the tribulations of the righteous, and out of them all the Lord will deliver them (Ps. 33:19).*  
*Sticheron 3:*  
The lawless king who lawlessly commanded to bow down and offer honor unto senseless, lifeless gods, O blessed ones, ye wisely put to shame, O martyrs of great name; and having suffered patiently and lawfully, ye wove crowns of victory, praying for the world.

*Glory... Tone 6:*  
A troop divinely crowned are the martyrs who have now appeared, an unblemished slaughter sacrificed zealously for Christ, a holy chosen army; come, lovers of the martyrs, having celebrated in spirit their all-sacred memory, let us cry unto them: Shatter the fury of the godless Hagarenes, and deliver from every distress people of pious mind, through your prayers.  
*Both now... Theotokion:*  
*My Creator and Redeemer:*[^260]  
*If thou celebratest not, speak this Theotokion:*[^261]  
We have known God Who was incarnate of thee, O Virgin Mother of God: pray Him for the salvation of our souls.  
*Cross-Theotokion:*[^262]  
Beholding Him Who was born of her, the unblemished one and Bride of God, as one condemned and nailed to the Cross, cried aloud: Glory to Thy long-suffering and great tender mercy, O my Son!

*Troparion in the Typikon.*[^263]  
*If there is no Typikon, speak this Troparion, Tone 4:*[^264]  
By the sufferings of the saints, which they endured for Thee, be Thou entreated, O Lord, and heal all our infirmities, we pray Thee, O Lover of mankind.  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": the same Troparion.*  
*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^265]  
As greatest stars ye ever enlighten the precious firmament of the Church, and illumine the faithful, O divine martyrs, warriors of Christ. *(Twice)*  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Unto those who flee unto thy shelter, O all-pure Virgin, thou receivest entreaty from us; cease not to pray the Lover of mankind to save thy servants.

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йся на крⷭ҇тъ):*[^266]  
Having endured martyrdom with a most courageous mind, O marvelous passion-bearers, ye passed through fire and water, and crossed over into the breadth of salvation, having received as an inheritance the heavenly kingdom; wherein make divine supplications for us, O wise great-martyrs. *(Twice)*  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Make us worthy gratefully to sing from the heart and to ask fervently for thy mercies, O Sovereign Lady Mother of God, unto thy servants who call and say: O all-holy Virgin, anticipate and deliver from enemies visible and invisible, and from every threat: for thou art our protection.

*At the Polyeleos, Refrain (Megaplynarion):*[^267]  
We magnify you, O holy passion-bearers, and we honor your precious sufferings, which ye endured for Christ.  
*Selected Psalm: God is our refuge and strength (Ps. 45:1).*[^268]

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*  
Ye were led bound by enemies and shut up in prison for many seasons; preserved by faith, ye remained unharmed; and being cut down in your bodies by the sword, O holy ones, by divine by divine desire ye were manifestly bound together: therefore as luminaries ye blossomed in the world, enlightening all by the grace of the Spirit, O blessed passion-bearers; pray Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who celebrate your holy memory with love. *(Twice)*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
As the Virgin and only one among women, who didst bear God in the flesh without seed, all generations of mankind call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead dwelt in thee, and as an infant thou didst nurse the Creator and Lord: therefore, angelical and human race, we worthily glorify thy all-holy childbearing, and with one accord cry unto thee: Pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who venerate thy all-holy childbearing in faith.

*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], the first Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon:*[^269]  
The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them (Ps. 33:17).  
*Verse: Many are the tribulations of the righteous, and out of them all the Lord will deliver them (Ps. 33:19).*  
*Let every breath praise the Lord (Ps. 150:6).*  
*Gospel from Matthew, Pericope 36 (Matt. 10:16–22).*[^270]  
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*  
Come, let us rejoice in the Lord on the memory of the passion-bearers, O ye who love the feasts; come, let us mystically sing, praising the sufferers of Christ, who betrayed idol deceit unto perdition, and clearly proclaimed Christ in the midst of the tribunal. Therefore, O all-blessed and much-suffering ones, cease not to pray for our souls.

*If there are Forty Martyrs, speak the Prokeimenon:*[^271]  
We went through fire and water, and Thou broughtest us out into a place of refreshment (Ps. 65:12).  
*Verse: Thou hast tried us, O God, as silver is tried (Ps. 65:10).*  
*Sticheron, Tone 2:*  
Prophetically David cried aloud in the Psalms: We went through fire and water, and Thou broughtest us out into a place of refreshment; and ye, O martyrs of Christ, fulfilling the word by your very deeds, passed through fire and water, and entered into the heavenly kingdom: therefore pray, being forty sufferers, to grant us great mercy.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos:*[^272]  
*The Canon of the Theotokos on 4, and of the Martyrs on 8.*  
*Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^273]  
Unto God the Savior, Who guided the people in the sea with dry feet, and drowned Pharaoh with all his army, unto Him alone let us sing: For He is glorified.  
*Troparion 1:*  
In divine wisdom, ye faithful, let us hymn the warriors and passion-bearers of Christ, as destroyers of deceit and radiant victors, singing unto God a song of victory: For He is glorified.  
*Troparion 2:*  
Having struggled radiantly upon earth, O sufferers, ye endured torments, and received crowns in the heavens, with one accord singing a song of victory: For He is glorified.  
*Troparion 3:*  
Bound faithfully in unity of soul, ye turned away from deceit, and were shown forth as crowned victors, with one accord singing a song of victory: For He is glorified.  
*Theotokion:*  
O all-pure Mother of God, unceasingly pray God Who was incarnate of thee and was not separated from the paternal bosom, to save from every distress those whom He created.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^274]  
By the power of Thy Cross, O Christ, establish my mind, that I may sing and glorify Thy saving ascension.  
*Troparion 1:*  
Adorned as soldiers by the mind of Christ, ye drowned the ungodly enemy in the streams of your blood.  
*Troparion 2:*  
Surrendering your body unto bitter and cruel torments, O praiseworthy martyrs, ye received the divine inheritance through faith.

*Troparion 3:*  
Crushed by the hurling of stones at the command of the tormentor, O passion-bearers, ye firmly kept the dominion of right faith.  
*Theotokion:*  
Unceasingly pray, O pure one, together with the martyrs, Him Who came forth from thy loins, to deliver from the deceit of the devil those who hymn thee, O all-immaculate one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йся на Крⷭ҇тъ):*[^275]  
The valiant warriors of Christ, having fought the good fight, utterly drowned the crafty enemy in the streams of their blood; crushed with stones, cut down with swords, burned with fire, and cast into water, they were manifestly shown to be crown-bearers: therefore they are honored and glorified in faith.  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
We shall never cease, O Virgin Mother of God, gratefully to hymn thy mercies from the heart, O Sovereign Lady, we thy servants, crying aloud and saying: O all-holy Virgin, anticipate and deliver us from enemies visible and invisible, and from necessity and every threat, for thou art our protection.  
*Cross-Theotokion:*[^276]  
The Virgin and Ewe, beholding upon the Cross the Lamb Who was born of her without seed, and pierced with a spear, wounded by arrows of grief, wept bitterly, crying aloud: What is this new mystery? How dost Thou die, Who alone art the Lord of life? Therefore arise, raising up our fallen forefather!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^277]  
I heard the report of the power of the Cross, how paradise was opened thereby, and I cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!  
*Troparion 1:*  
Holding fast to God-pleasing customs, the choir of sufferers conquered the God-opposing enemy, strengthened by the grace of the Savior.  
*Troparion 2:*  
O divinely called troop of Christ's passion-bearers, ye conquered the multitude of evil-doing enemies who opposed Him, singing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!  
*Troparion 3:*  
Beholding unapproachable light as they sat in prison, the passion-bearers destroyed the darkness of polytheism and idol deceit, strengthened by the power of God.  
*Theotokion:*  
The power of the Most High overshadowed thee, O Virgin, and made thee a paradise holding the Tree of life in its midst, the Mediator and Lord.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^278]  
Rising early in the morning we cry unto Thee, O Lord, save us: for Thou art our God, beside Thee we know no other.  
*Troparion 1:*  
Nurtured by the words of the Holy Spirit, the martyrs brought idol foolishness to nought.  
*Troparion 2:*  
The passion-bearers became light-bearing stars and flowers of faith, pouring forth sweet fragrance.  
*Troparion 3:*  
Ye appeared as a field of the Most High, O all-praised holy ones, reaped by the sickles of martyrdom.  
*Theotokion:*  
Cease not to pray Him Whom thou didst bear, O Mother of God, to save our souls, who fervently sing praise unto thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^279]  
The abyss surrounded me, the sea-monster became my grave; but I cried unto Thee, the Lover of mankind, and Thy right hand saved me, O Lord.  
*Troparion 1:*  
As they were cast in, the martyrs cried aloud: Into Thy hands, O Master, Lover of mankind, receive our spirits and give them rest; for we love Thee alone, the Multi-merciful One.  
*Troparion 2:*  
The choir of Thy martyrs, O Lover of mankind, became fellow-dwellers with the angels; for having finished the course, they now pray that our souls may be saved.  
*Troparion 3:*  
O glory of martyrs, beauty of martyrs, divinely chosen choir of martyrs, fervently pray that all who flee unto you may be saved.

*Theotokion:*  
What word can express the wonder of thy seedless conception, O all-immaculate one? For thou didst conceive God, Who came unto us through compassion.

*Kontakion in the Typikon.*[^280]  
*If there is no Typikon, speak this Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^281]  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^282]  
Radiant luminaries have ye appeared, O divine martyrs; with the brilliance of wonders ye enlighten all creation, healing infirmities and ever driving away deep darkness, praying unceasingly unto Christ God for us all.  
*Ikos:*  
O fair choir, radiant and divine luminaries who ever stand before the Great Light, O divinely blessed martyrs, and who are ever enlightened and deified by the splendors of the never-setting Godhead sent forth thence: enlighten those who in faith celebrate your divine memory, and deliver them from darkness, passions, tribulations, and malice, praying unceasingly for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^283]  
O Thou Who didst save the children who sang praise in the fiery furnace: Blessed is the God of our fathers!  
*Troparion 1:*  
In the furnace Thy passion-bearers cried aloud, O Christ: Blessed is the God of our fathers!  
*Troparion 2:*  
Enlightened by Trinitarian light, the sufferers surrendered their souls in joy, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!  
*Troparion 3:*  
Standing before God in joy, O crowned sufferers, pray Him for us!  
*Theotokion:*  
As our salvation, we pray thee, O Mother of God: pray Him Who was incarnate of thee for us.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^284]  
Him Who was born of the Father before the ages, Son and God, and in these latter times was incarnate of the Virgin Mother, ye priests, sing; ye peoples, exalt Him above all unto all ages.  
*Troparion 1:*  
Together with the martyrs let our petition be, we the faithful; and may we become partakers of their inheritance, singing unto Christ and exalting Him above all unto ages.  
*Troparion 2:*  
With gladness of heart, the choir of patient sufferers divinely receives crowns from Christ, and radiantly sings and exalts Him above all unto ages.  
*Troparion 3:*  
Dyed purple with the streams of your blood, O praiseworthy ones, ye reign unto ages with Christ in the heavens, piously singing and exalting Him above all unto ages.  
*Theotokion:*  
Higher than the cherubim hast thou appeared, O pure Mother of God, having carried in thy womb Him Who sits upon them; Whom with the bodiless hosts we earthborn glorify and exalt above all unto ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^285]  
Thee Who beyond mind and word art the Mother of God, who in time didst ineffably give birth unto the Timeless One, we the faithful magnify with one mind.  
*Troparion 1:*  
Pray for the common salvation, O all-glorious martyrs, standing before Christ, for Whose sake ye endured torments.  
*Troparion 2:*  
By mighty power ye cast down the fortress of deceit, O unconquerable ones; and now ye have been vouchsafed to dwell in heavenly mansions with the angels.  
*Troparion 3:*  
Having conquered the proud tormentor by lawful martyric customs, ye received crowns of righteousness, O all-honorable ones.  
*Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, O Mother of God, Mother of Christ God; pray Him Whom thou didst bear, together with the martyrs, to grant remission of sins unto those who hymn thee in faith.

*Exapostilarion (Photogogikon):*[^286]  
Ye were taken up, O passion-bearers, into the light-resembling paradise mansions, clothed in a radiant garment which ye wove with manifold torments; and ye stand before the throne of the Creator, fervently praying for all.  
*Theotokion:*  
By thy sovereign protection, O pure one, preserve us thy servants ever unharmed from the assaults of the enemy: for thee alone have we acquired as a refuge in tribulations.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^287]

*Sticheron 1:*  
O all-praised martyrs of Christ, counting as nothing the assaults of persecutors and a violent death, and courageously setting yourselves in array unto struggles with manly wisdom, ye were clothed with victorious glory, and were numbered with all the righteous; with whom, ever praising you, we call you blessed. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 2:*  
O all-blessed martyrs of Christ, ye surrendered yourselves unto voluntary slaughter, and sanctified the earth with your blood, and enlightened the air by your translation; and now ye dwell in the heavens, in the never-setting Light, ever praying for us, O eyewitnesses of God.

*Sticheron 3:*  
O unconquerable martyrs of Christ, the burning heat of torments ye passed through unharmed by the dew of divine grace, and were vouchsafed to dwell beside the waters of refreshment, having received crowns of victory: therefore, rejoicing today, we celebrate your holy memory, glorifying Christ.

*Glory... Tone 8:*  
Having soldiered for Christ, ye left behind the fair things that are upon earth, and having taken the Cross upon your shoulders, for the sake of manifold torments ye followed Him; and before many kings and tormentors ye denied Him not; angels bound your heads with crowns of victory, and courageous in soul, ye entered radiantly into the great bridal chamber: therefore, having boldness toward the Savior of all, pray for our souls.  
*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Receiving the voice of the Archangel Gabriel, let us say: Rejoice, O Mother of God, who didst give birth unto Christ the Giver of life for the world!

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Ode 3 and Ode 6. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*[^288]  
Unto the saints that are in His earth, the Lord hath been marvelous; He hath wrought all His desires in them (Ps. 15:3).  
*Verse: I beheld the Lord ever before me, for He is at my right hand, that I might not be moved (Ps. 15:8).*  
*Epistle to the Romans, Pericope 94 (Rom. 8:3–9); or to the Hebrews, Pericope 330 (Heb. 11:33–40).*[^289]  
*Alleluia, Tone 4:*  
The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them, and delivered them out of all their tribulations (Ps. 33:17).  
*Verse: Many are the tribulations of the righteous, and out of them all the Lord will deliver them (Ps. 33:19).*  
*Gospel from Matthew, Pericope 36 (Matt. 10:16–22); or Luke, Pericope 106 (Luke 21:12–19).*[^290]  
*Communion Hymn: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous (Ps. 32:1).*[^291]

## Common Service to a Single Hieromartyr[^292]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 1.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льніи мꙋ́нцы):*[^293]

*Sticheron 1:*  
O divinely wise and blessed [Name], with the crimson of thy blood thou didst adorn thy sacred and divine vesture; for piously thou didst pass from strength to strength, and from glory to glory: and now pray that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls.

*Sticheron 2:*  
First offering unto God an unbloody sacrifice, as a most lawful priest, as a whole burnt offering and acceptable sacrifice and afterwards, as a most true martyr, thou didst offer thyself in blood unto Christ, O divinely speaking and all-honorable one; pray Him for those who sing praise unto thee.

*Sticheron 3:*  
Thou didst bring armies of martyrs unto Christ, O father, through thy teachings and instructions, teaching and instructing them, and manifestly offering thyself as an example, O divinely wise one; with them pray that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Storm-tossed by a sea of transgressions, fleeing unto the calm haven of thy most pure prayer, O Mother of God, I cry unto thee: Save me, stretching out thy sovereign right hand unto thy servant, O all-immaculate one.

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^294]  
Standing by the Cross of thy Son and God, and beholding His long-suffering, the pure Mother wept, saying: Woe is me, sweetest Child! Why dost Thou suffer these things unjustly, O Word of God, that Thou mayest save mankind?

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: On "Lord, I have cried", Stichera on 6.*  
*Glory... Tone 6:*  
A most lawful priest wast thou even unto thine end, O blessed [Name]; for officiating at the divine and ineffable mysteries, thou didst pour forth thy blood for Christ God, and offeredst thyself as an acceptable sacrifice. Therefore, having boldness toward Him, fervently pray for those who celebrate thy most honorable memory with faith and love, and for those who honor it, that they may be delivered from temptations, and from all tribulations and distresses.  
*Both now... Theotokion:*  
*Who will not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin:*[^295]

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the 3 Readings.*[^296]  
*Look in the Service to a Martyr (see fol. 45).*[^297]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera Idiomela, Tone 6.*[^298]

*Sticheron 1:*  
Having dyed thy sacred vesture with blood, thou didst enter into the Holy of Holies, having become holy, O glorious [Name]; and there, ever enjoying deification, adorned also with holy martyrdom, thou wast shown to be more honorable than the angels by thy communions: therefore we honor thee, and celebrate in love thy most sacred festival, O divinely blessed one.

*Verse: The righteous shall flourish like a palm tree, and shall be multiplied like a cedar in Lebanon (Ps. 91:12).*  
*Sticheron 2:*  
Bound with iron, unimpeded didst thou tread the path leading unto the heavenly city, O venerable [Name], most wise one, clothed with honorable wounds as with an adornment; entering therein as a most true victor, an unconquerable martyr, and a most sacred celebrant, thou singest with the angels a divine and pure hymn: Holy, Holy, Holy, consubstantial Trinity!

*Verse: Planted in the house of the Lord, in the courts of our God they shall flourish (Ps. 91:13).*  
*Sticheron 3:*  
The Church acquired thy sacred struggles as an ascending step, O hieromartyr [Name], which thou also keepest unshaken and unassailed by sovereign wolves, as it proclaims thy valiance and magnifies thy sufferings, which thou didst lawfully endure for Christ, O blessed one.

*Glory... Tone 4:*  
Sacredly didst thou enter into the heavenly Church, sacredly drenched with blood, O blessed one; and thou standest before the Trinity, richly enriched from thence with flowing sacred splendors: therefore today, O [Name], celebrating thy light-bearing memory, we enlighten the senses of the soul.

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
*Look upon the entreaties of thy servants:*  
*If thou celebratest not, speak this Theotokion:*[^299]  
With the showers of the Most Holy Spirit, O all-pure one, bedew my mind, who didst bear Christ as a drop, Who by His mercies washed away the countless, boundless iniquities of men: dry up the spring of my passions, and vouchsafe me ever to drink of the torrent of living pleasure through thy prayers.  
*Cross-Theotokion:*[^300]  
When she beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, Thy Ewe and Mother marveled, and cried aloud: What is this sight, O beloved Son? Hath the lawless assembly rendered these things unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many wonders? But glory to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*Troparion in the Typikon.*[^301]  
*If there is no Typikon, speak this Troparion, Tone 4:*[^302]  
As a sharer of customs and successor to the thrones of the Apostles, thou didst find action as an ascent unto contemplation, O divinely inspired one: therefore, rightly dividing the word of truth, thou didst also suffer for the faith even unto blood, O hieromartyr [Name]; pray Christ God that our souls may be saved.  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": the same Troparion.*  
*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3.*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*[^303]  
By the sea of the confession of the faith thou didst quench the evil belief of deceit, having refuted idol godlessness; and having become a divine whole-burnt offering, by wonderworking thou dost illumine the ends of the earth, O venerable father; pray Christ God to grant us great mercy. *(Twice)*  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Thou wast the divine tabernacle of the Word, O only all-pure Virgin Mother, surpassing the angels in purity; cleanse me above all, who have become dust, defiled by fleshly transgressions, with the divine waters of thy prayers, granting great mercy, O pure one.

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^304]  
Having shone forth like the ray of the sun, thou dost shine brightly upon the whole world, O venerable one, through thy teachings, O divinely speaking [Name], light-bearer, praise of martyrs. *(Twice)*  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Stretching forth thy most pure hands, O Virgin Mother, shelter those who hope in thee and cry unto thy Son: Grant Thy mercies unto all, O Christ.

*At the Polyeleos, Refrain (Megaplynarion):*[^305]  
We magnify thee, O hieromartyr [Name], and we honor thy holy memory: for thou prayest for us unto Christ our God.  
*Selected Psalm: Hear these things, all ye nations (Ps. 48:1).*[^306]

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^307]  
Governed by all-effecting power and strengthened by the sail of the Cross, lightly didst thou sail across the storm of life, and in truth reached the divine harbors, having acquired much merchandise of virtues, which thou broughtest unto the Master of all; and thou heardest: "Well done, good servant", and what follows thereupon: therefore, O blessed [Name], pray Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love. *(Twice) Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Falling into manifold temptations from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of my countless transgressions: and as unto my fervent protection and shelter, O pure one, I flee unto the haven of thy goodness. Therefore, O all-pure one, fervently pray Him Who was incarnate of thee without seed for all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O all-pure Mother of God, ever entreating Him to grant remission of sins unto those who worthily hymn thy glory.

*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], the first Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*[^308]  
Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones (Ps. 115:6).  
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me? (Ps. 115:3).*  
*Let every breath praise the Lord (Ps. 150:6).*  
*Gospel from Luke, Pericope 67 (Luke 12:32–40).*[^309]  
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*  
Thou wast a sacred vessel of the Holy Spirit, having cleansed thy soul from passions, O sacred one: therefore thou didst receive the holy divine anointing, having become a hierarch and guide of God-wise people, and an unconquerable martyr of Him Who endured suffering for our sake and poured forth dispassion, O sufferer [Name].

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos:*[^310]  
*The Canon of the Mother of God on 4, and of the Saint on 8.*  
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^311]  
Come, ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of the bondage of Egypt: for He is glorified.  
*Troparion 1:*  
Enlightened by the illumination of Christ, O glorious hieromartyr [Name], by thy prayers, O all-blessed one, enlighten my darkened soul, that I may hymn thee.  
*Troparion 2:*  
With the enlightenment of saving preaching, having shone forth as an undefiled mirror unto thy pure heart, O father, thou didst send forth rays unto all.  
*Troparion 3:*  
By thy fasting thou didst transform all passionate play, and by abstinence thy desire, and by various struggles didst rise unto the height of dispassion.  
*Theotokion:*  
We know God Who was incarnate of thee without seedful desire, Who is before all creation and before all ages: therefore we hymn thee as the true Mother of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^312]  
Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who sing praise unto Thee.  
*Troparion 1:*  
An all-perfect living sacrifice thou didst offer thyself unto Christ through martyrdom, by martyric counsel, having previously suffered through abstinence.  
*Troparion 2:*  
Sacred was thy life, and divine thy word, and divine thy deeds, receiving ascents in thy heart of true divine vision, which thou hast attained.  
*Troparion 3:*  
Having directed thy mind in godly manner by the commandments of the Savior, O venerable one, Thou wast shown to be worthy and an unconquerable champion of the faith.  
*Theotokion:*  
Thou didst shake off numbness from our mind, having given birth unto Him Who is immortality; and thou hast woven a garment of incorruption for us by thy childbearing, O Virgin.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*  
Enlightened by the Divine Spirit, with pastoral boldness thou didst nobly put to shame the ferocity of tormentors, O hieromartyr; and having crossed the abyss of deceit, thou didst reach the divine haven, O venerable father; pray Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Whithersoever one is saved, thither does he rightly flee; and what other such refuge is there, as thou art, O Mother of God, sheltering our souls?  
*Cross-Theotokion:*[^313]  
Having acquired a rod of power, the Cross of thy Son, O Mother of God, thereby do we cast down the insolence of enemies, unceasingly magnifying thee in love.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^314]  
I heard, O Lord, Thy glorious dispensation, and I glorified Thine incomprehensible power, O Lover of mankind.  
*Troparion 1:*  
A divine whole-burnt offering and pure sacrifice thou didst offer thyself unto the Savior of all, O most wise hieromartyr.  
*Troparion 2:*  
Guiding the flock of Christ unto the light of divine knowledge, O venerable one, thou didst drive away the darkness of godlessness.  
*Troparion 3:*  
The flame of godlessness thou didst dry up with the river of thy sweats, O father, as a hieromartyr.  
*Theotokion:*  
Enlighten my darkened soul with thy light, O all-pure one, who didst bear the Light, the one Hypostasis.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^315]  
O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments: for beside Thee we know no other God.  
*Troparion 1:*  
Riches and radiant glory, and length of days, and incorruptible life, Christ the true Wisdom granted unto thee, O blessed one, as thou didst eminently love Him.  
*Troparion 2:*  
With the staff of faith thou drivest away the snares of heresies, and having united thy flock with the bond of love, thou keepest it unharmed in hope and faith.  
*Troparion 3:*  
Having seasoned food by abstinence, thou didst nourish thy soul, having as food unceasing prayers and instruction, and humility that elevates unto God.  
*Theotokion:*  
Thee as protectress, O pure one, and firm hope and might and shelter, and wall have we all acquired, and bridge leading into the kingdom.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^316]  
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy tender mercy: Bring me up from corruption, O God!  
*Troparion 1:*  
Having become both sacrifice and celebrant, thou didst sacrifice thyself unto God for the sake of faith, and unblemished thou keepest the completion of thy ministry unto Him.  
*Troparion 2:*  
Thou wast put to death unto the whole world, O divinely speaking one, but remaining alive unto God alone, thou didst pass unto Christ, enriched with incorruptible life.  
*Troparion 3:*  
Now having passed from earth into unending glory, heal my soul wretched with passions, O venerable one, by the fervent cleansing of thy prayer.  
*Theotokion:*  
Beholding Christ Who takes away the sins of the whole world, O all-pure, all-immaculate one, cease not to pray for the forgiveness of sins for thy servants.

*Kontakion in the Typikon.*[^317]  
*If there is no Typikon, speak this Kontakion, Tone 4:*[^318]  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ):*[^319]  
Having lived piously among hierarchs, and passed through the path of martyrdom, thou didst quench idol sacrifices and becamest a champion for thy flock, O God-wise one. Therefore honoring thee, we mystically cry aloud unto thee: From perils ever deliver us by thy supplications, O our father, [Name].  
*Ikos:*  
Assembling faithfully in love on the memory of the sacred sufferer, let us hymn him with songs today, that we may enjoy grace from him: for he saves souls *Ikos (continued):*
...from passions and tribulations, as a martyr and as a faithful hierarch, and champion of those who pray unto him from the soul. Therefore we cry aloud unto him: From perils ever deliver us by thy supplications, O our father, [Name].

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^320]  
The three youths did not heed the godless command to serve the golden image in the plain of Dura; but cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed, they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!  
*Troparion 1:*  
A cup of wisdom filled with virtue wast thou shown to be; and having gathered thy flock by the proclamation of thy high life, thou didst guide it, O God-wise one; and with them, O father, thou criest aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!  
*Troparion 2:*  
Unto the mansions that are there in the honorable dwelling hast thou arrived, taking up abode with the angels, and with them, O father, thou dancest in chorus: therefore honoring thee, O all-blessed one, with boldness we cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!  
*Troparion 3:*  
Blessed art thou, and well is it with thee, having now taken up abode where the hosts of the righteous dwell, where the habitations of the martyrs are, where the choirs of the most wise Apostles cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!  
*Theotokion:*  
Thou alone wast known as the unwedded Mother of God Who created all things by wisdom, O all-pure Virgin: therefore piously crying aloud unto thee, we say: Blessed is she that bare God in the flesh!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^321]  
Sing unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew, as Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!  
*Troparion 1:*  
Having piously officiated at the mystical ministry of Christ, thou didst offer thyself unto Him as a spiritual sheep, an acceptable and well-pleasing sacrifice, perfected by blood, O all-blessed one.  
*Troparion 2:*  
Having adorned the garment of the priesthood with thy deeds, O most wise one, thou wast shown to be the key-bearer of chastity, true food of abstinence, teacher of prayer, guide of repentance, and law of life.  
*Troparion 3:*  
A time of proposal, we ever offer the fruit of repentance; and the barren fig tree threatens with cutting down; fear the curse, O my soul, and receive Christ, the virtuous fruitfulness!  
*Theotokion:*  
Disdain not, O awesome protectress, the voice of thy servants, and from every distress and cruel threat, O holy Virgin, snatch all away: for thy maternal prayer inclines God.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^322]  
God the Word of God, Who with ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had fallen grievously into corruption through eating, and for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin, we the faithful magnify with one mind in songs.  
*Troparion 1:*  
Thou didst quench the various darts of the enemy with the fire of abstinence, O father; and having burned up fleshly mire with the coals of purity, thou wast gloriously vouchsafed the true splendors that are there.  
*Troparion 2:*  
Thy light-bearing memory having shone forth, O father, enlightens the souls of those who reverently celebrate it, O right wonderworker, and manifestly makes them partakers of divine light; which we worthily magnify in songs, O God-wise one.  
*Troparion 3:*  
Beholding thee bearing a crown, O father, and having received the prize of victory from the right hand of God, and having finished thy struggles, and filled with divine light, believing, we the faithful present thee unto Christ as a fervent petitioner.  
*Theotokion:*  
As the Mother of God, O Virgin, and Mother of the Word, who art our mediatress of eternal divine life and of the Sun of righteousness Who hath shone forth upon us, we the faithful magnify thee with one mind in songs.

*Exapostilarion (Photogogikon).*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "O Thou Who hast adorned heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*[^323]  
As a hieromartyr having authority to bind and loose, loose the bond of my evils, and number me with the love of God, and make me a partaker of the kingdom, O blessed one, by thy prayers.  
*Theotokion:*  
Enlighten my soul darkened by sins, O pure one, and deliver me from eternal flame and darkness through thy mediation, that in gladness I may praise thy greatness.

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^324]  
*Sticheron 1:*  
Spiritual grace, having shone forth upon thee, O father [Name], brightly enlightened thee: thenceforth it diminished the passionate night, and the day of dispassion arrived, purely joining thee unto the purest Light. In Which dwelling, O divinely speaking hieromartyr, forget not those who sing praise unto thy memory in faith. *(Twice)*  
*Sticheron 2:*  
Bearing desirable heavenly grace, O hieromartyr [Name], thou didst disregard earthly things: therefore, as one bodiless, thou didst choose the life of suffering, desiring to enjoy the eternal torrent of pleasure, O God-receiver; and with tears thou didst dry up the turbid spring of passions, and watered the soul-nourishing ears of corn.  
*Sticheron 3:*  
Thou didst disregard the body wounded by blows and brought near unto the fire: for thou didst wing thy mind with unconquerable desire for the most comely beauty of the divine Lover, wounded by Whose sweet longing, O adornment of martyrs, embellishment of hierarchs, sufferer [Name], companion of angels...

*Companion of angels.*  
*Glory... Tone 4:*  
As a new planting, like an olive tree, wast thou placed at the table of God, as a son of those who walk in the way of the Lord: for the sake of thy martyrdom the Lord blessed thee, and thou shalt see the good things of Zion on high, enjoying divine rejoicing with all the saints, O hieromartyr [Name], worthy of song: make us to be partakers thereof by thy supplications.  
*Both now... Theotokion:*  
O Mother of God, Queen of all, praise of the Orthodox, tear down the insolence of heretics, and put to shame the faces of those who do not bow down before, nor honor, O all-pure one, thy precious icon.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Ode 3 and Ode 6. Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*[^325]  
The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds (Ps. 149:5).  
*Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song, His praise in the church of the venerable ones (Ps. 149:1).*  
*Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 334 (Heb. 13:7–16).*[^326]  
*Alleluia, Tone 2:*  
*Verse: Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness (Ps. 131:9).*  
*Gospel from Luke, Pericope 67 (Luke 12:32–40).*[^327]  
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be (Ps. 111:6b).*[^328]

## Common Service to Two and Many Hieromartyrs[^329]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льніи мꙋ́нцы):*[^330]  
*Sticheron 1:*  
With the blood of martyrdom ye truly and brightly adorned the hierarchal vesture, and brightly adorned on both sides, ye stand before the Creator with the angels, O glorious servants: pray Him to grant unto our souls peace and great mercy.

*Sticheron 1 (continued):*  
...peace and great mercy.  
*Sticheron 2:*  
Ye were truly shown unto us as divinely inspired instruments, proclaiming the ineffable mysteries of God of the Divine Scripture. Refuting idol folly, ye brought all unto Christ: pray Him to grant unto our souls peace and great mercy.  
*Sticheron 3:*  
By the word of divine knowledge, O most wise ones, ye completely delivered the people from ignorance, and brought them saved unto the Word Who shone forth and was born of the Father, Christ our God: pray Him to grant unto our souls peace and great mercy.  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
O all-hymned Virgin, Moses with prophetic eyes beheld the mystery that is in thee, as the bush burning and unconsumed: for the fire of the Godhead burned not thy pure womb; therefore we pray thee as the Mother of our God to ask peace for the world, and great mercy.  
*Cross-Theotokion:*[^331]  
Standing by Thy Cross, O Word of God, Thy unblemished Ewe and Mother wept, crying aloud: Woe is me, my Son! How dost Thou die upon the Cross? Woe is me, my sweet Light! Whither hath the vision of Thy beauty now set, Who art more comely than all the sons of men?

*If thou celebratest with Polyeleos: On "Lord, I have cried", Stichera on 6.*  
*Glory... Tone 8:*  
Passing beyond the cycle of temporal things, O divinely called ones, beaten sevenfold and passing through rackings, and all crucified for Christ: neither fire, nor wounds, nor the sword weakened the strength of your souls; and ye trampled down the demonic strength, O fellow hieromartyrs: therefore pray Christ God to save our souls.  
*Both now... Theotokion:*  
*The King of heaven out of love for mankind:*[^332]

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the 3 Readings.*[^333]  
*Look in the Service to Martyrs (see fol. 51v).*[^334]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бла):*[^335]  
*Sticheron 1:*  
As many-lighted stars, having spiritually shone forth upon the firmament of the Church, ye enlighten the whole creation, driving away the darkness by the struggle of light, and by the splendors of wonders: therefore rejoicing today, we celebrate your light-bearing and holy memory, O glorious hieromartyrs.  
*Verse: Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice (Ps. 131:9).*  
*Sticheron 2:*  
Equal in honor with the Apostles, having become sacred leaders, having received a mind together zealous, ye feared not the bestial cruelty of the tormentor's godlessness; but manfully and firmly ye proclaimed the Savior, and endured manifold evils: for the sake of this, ye now receive future rewards, O all-wise ones.  
*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, he shall delight exceedingly in His commandments (Ps. 111:1).*  
*Sticheron 3:*  
Offering honorable services, O all-powerful ones, and an unblemished sacrifice unto the Master Christ, ye brought yourselves as perfect whole-burnt offerings in suffering, O blessed sacred leaders: pray Him to deliver from corruption and perils those who celebrate your honorable memory in faith.  
*Glory... Tone 4:*  
Unconquerable martyrs, all-praised hierarchs, and universal luminaries, unshakable pillars of the divine Church, and step of teachings, and guides of the pious: ye were destroyers of heresies, O God-minded fathers, radiant *Glory... Tone 4 (continued):*
...guides of our souls, companions of angels, and champions of the Trinity.  
*Both now... Theotokion:*  
*Look upon the entreaties of thy servants:*  
*If thou celebratest not, speak this Theotokion:*[^336]  
Wash away the defilement of my passionate heart, O all-hymned Mother of God, and all its wounds and sores that come from sin, cleanse, O pure one; and stay the instability of my mind, that I, thy wretched and unprofitable servant, may magnify thy power and great protection.  
*Cross-Theotokion:*[^337]  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бла):*[^338]  
Beholding Christ the Lover of mankind crucified and pierced in the ribs with a spear, the all-pure one wept, crying aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee, instead of the good things Thou didst do unto them, and strivest to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O Compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!

*Troparion in the Typikon.*[^339]  
*If there is no Typikon, speak this Troparion, Tone 4:*[^340]  
O God of our fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy gentleness, withdraw not Thy mercy from us, but by their prayers guide our life in peace.  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": the same Troparion.*  
*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^341]
