# PART II: THEOTOKION CANONS AND THEOTOKIA OF THE EIGHT TONES

## Theotokion Canons Sung According to the Typikon, on Memories of Celebrated Saints, Before Their Canons
*First Canon*
*Ode 1, Tone 2. Heirmos:*
Traversing the untrodden, unfamiliar, unwetted pathway of the sea, chosen Israel cried aloud: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is greatly glorified![^677]
*Refrain: Most holy Mother of God, save us!*
*Troparion 1:*
The immaterial ladder of old, and the strangely congealed pathway of the sea, revealed thy childbearing, O pure one, which we all sing: for He is greatly glorified!
*Troparion 2:*
The power of the Most High, the perfect Hypostasis, the Wisdom of God *Theotokion Canons*
having become incarnate of thee, O all-pure one, conversed with men: for He is greatly glorified!
*Troparion 3:*
The Sun of Righteousness passed through the impassable, sealed door of thy womb, O pure one, and shone forth upon the world: for He is greatly glorified!
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The bow of the mighty is broken by Thy dominion, O Christ, and the weak are girded with strength.[^678]
*Troparion 1:*
He Who is higher than every time, as the Creator of the times, was voluntarily formed as an infant from thee, O Virgin.
*Troparion 2:*
Let us hymn the womb more spacious than the heavens, through which Adam, rejoicing, lives in the heavens.
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard, O Lord, Thy glorious dispensation, and I glorified Thine incomprehensible power, O Lover of mankind.[^679]
*Troparion 1:*
Behold, the divine mountain of the house of the Lord is exalted higher than the powers, most manifestly as the Mother of God.
*Troparion 2:*
Apart from the laws of nature, having alone given birth unto the Master of creation, O Virgin, thou wast accounted worthy of the divine calling.
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
The coal revealed to Isaiah, the Sun, shone forth from the virginal womb, granting the enlightenment of the knowledge of God unto those who had wandered in darkness.[^680]
*Troparion 1:*
Ye clouds, drop down the sweetness of gladness upon those on earth: for a young Child is given, Who exists before the ages, our God incarnate of the Virgin.
*Troparion 2:*
A light shone forth upon my life and flesh, and destroyed the gloom of sin, the Most High having become incarnate in the last times of the Virgin without seed.
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Having heard the voice of supplicatory words from a suffering soul, O Master, deliver me from grievous things: for Thou alone art the Author of our salvation.[^681]

*Theotokion Canons*
*Troparion 1:*
Human nature, in bondage to sin, O pure Sovereign Lady, obtained freedom through thee: for thy Son, as a lamb, is slain for all.
*Troparion 2:*
We all cry out unto thee, true Mother of God: deliver thy servants who have provoked anger, for thou alone hast boldness before thy Son.
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Luminaries were shown forth of old in the most philosophic youths, for from a God-receiving soul, theologizing with their lips, they sang: O supremely divine God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou![^682]
*Troparion 1:*
Jacob indeed in the night beheld God incarnate as in an enigma; but from thee He appeared visibly unto us who sing: The supremely divine God of our fathers, and supremely glorified!
*Troparion 2:*
Manifesting the signs of the ineffable condescension in thee, He wrestled with Jacob, by which He was voluntarily united unto men, O pure one: the supremely divine God of our fathers, and supremely glorified!
*Troparion 3:*
Abominable is he who proclaims not Thee, the only Son of the Virgin, of the all-hymned Trinity, with unhesitating faith and with his tongue crying aloud: The supremely divine God of our fathers, and supremely glorified!
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Disregarding the golden likeness, the thrice-blessed youths, seeing the immutable and living image of God, sang in the midst of the fire: Let all united creation sing the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^683]
*Troparion 1:*
He was seen on earth through thee, and lived with men, Who is incomparable in goodness and in power, unto Whom singing we all the faithful cry: Let all united creation sing the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!
*Troparion 2:*
Truly proclaiming thee as pure, we glorify thee as the Mother of God: for thou didst bear One of the Trinity incarnate, unto Whom with the Father and the Spirit we all sing: Let all creation sing the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion Canons*
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thou art all desire, Thou art all sweetness, O Word of God, Son of the Virgin, God of gods, O Lord of the holy, ever-existing: therefore we all magnify Thee together with her who gave Thee birth.[^684]
*Troparion 1:*
A rod of strength was given unto corrupt nature, the Word of God in thy womb, O pure one, and raised it up, which had slipped down unto hades. Therefore we magnify thee, O all-pure one, as the Mother of God.
*Troparion 2:*
Whom Thou didst choose, O Master, accept graciously as an intercessor, Thy Mother on our behalf, and let all things be filled with Thy goodness: that we all may magnify Thee as Benefactor.
*Second Canon*
*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
Having traversed the water as dry land, and escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God![^685]
*Refrain: Most holy Mother of God, save us!*
*Troparion 1:*
Beset by many temptations, unto thee do I flee, seeking salvation: O Mother of the Word and Virgin, save me from grievous and dire calamities!
*Troparion 2:*
The assaults of passions trouble me, filling my soul with much despondency: calm them, O Maiden, with the tranquil peace of thy Son and God, O all-immaculate one!
*Troparion 3:*
Unto thee who didst bear the Savior and God, I pray, O Virgin, that I may be delivered from dire afflictions: for unto thee now fleeing, I stretch forth both soul and understanding.
*Troparion 4:*
Diseased in body and in soul, vouchsafe me divine visitation and providence from thee, O only Mother of God, as thou art good and the parent of the Good.
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Supreme Creator of the heavenly vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou confirm me in Thy love, O Summit of desire, firm foundation of the faithful, *Theotokion Canons*
O only Lover of mankind.[^686]
*Troparion 1:*
The protection and shelter of my life do I set thee, O Virgin Birthgiver of God: pilot me unto thy harbor, O cause of good things, firm foundation of the faithful, only all-hymned one!
*Troparion 2:*
I pray thee, O Virgin, dispel the turmoil of my soul and the storm of my grief: for thou, O Bride of God, hast given birth unto Christ the Prince of peace, O only all-pure one!
*Troparion 3:*
Having given birth unto the Benefactor, the Author of good things, pour forth the wealth of beneficence unto all: for thou canst do all things, as having given birth unto Christ Who is mighty in strength, O divinely blessed one!
*Troparion 4:*
Tested by grievous ailments and painful passions, O Virgin, do thou help me: for I know thee to be an inexhaustible and unfailing treasury of healings, O all-immaculate one!
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I considered Thy works, and I glorified Thy Divinity.[^687]
*Troparion 1:*
Having given birth unto the Lord the Pilot, calm the turmoil of my passions and the tempest of my transgressions, O Bride of God!
*Troparion 2:*
Grant unto me who call upon the abyss of thy tender compassion, O thou that didst bear the Compassionate One and Savior of all who hymn Thee!
*Troparion 3:*
Enjoying thy gifts, O all-pure one, we sing a thanksgiving hymn, knowing thee to be the Mother of God.
*Troparion 4:*
As I lie upon the bed of my illness and infirmity, help me as a lover of goodness, O Mother of God, only Ever-Virgin!
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Enlighten us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thine arm on high grant us Thy peace, O Lover of mankind.[^688]
*Troparion 1:*
Fill my heart with gladness, O pure one, giving thine incorrupt joy, O thou that didst give birth unto the Author of gladness!
*Troparion 2:*
Deliver us from dangers, O pure Mother of God, who didst bear the eternal deliverance *Theotokion Canons*
and peace, which surpasses all understanding.
*Troparion 3:*
Dispel the mist of my transgressions, O Bride of God, by the enlightenment of thy radiance, O thou that didst bear the divine and pre-eternal Light!
*Troparion 4:*
Heal, O pure one, the infirmity of my soul, having accounted me worthy of thy visitation, and grant me health by thy prayers!
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my sorrows, for my soul is full of evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades; and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God![^689]
*Troparion 1:*
As He hath saved from death and corruption, having given Himself over unto death, my nature that had been held by corruption and death: O Virgin, pray thy Lord and Son to deliver me from the malice of the enemies!
*Troparion 2:*
I know thee, O Virgin, as the protectress of life and a firm guardian, dispelling the tumult of temptations and driving away the assaults of demons: and I ever pray that I may be delivered from the corruption of my passions!
*Troparion 3:*
We have acquired thee as a wall of refuge, and the all-perfect salvation of our souls, and spacious relief in afflictions, O Maiden, and in thy light we ever rejoice: O Sovereign Lady, do thou even now save us from passions and dangers!
*Troparion 4:*
Now I lie diseased upon my bed, and there is no healing for my flesh: but unto thee who didst bear God and the Savior of the world, and the Deliverer from diseases, I pray, O good one: Raise me up from the corruption of illnesses!
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The youths who came from Judea in Babylon of old, through faith in the Trinity, trod down the flame of the furnace, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^690]
*Troparion 1:*
Having willed to accomplish our salvation, O Savior, Thou didst dwell in the womb *Theotokion Canons*
of the Virgin, whom Thou didst reveal unto the world as protectress: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!
*Troparion 2:*
Entreat the Lover of mercy, Whom thou didst bear, O pure Mother, that those who cry in faith may be delivered from transgressions and defilements of soul: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!
*Troparion 3:*
Thou didst show forth her that bare Thee as a treasury of salvation, a fountain of incorruption, a pillar of strength, and a door of repentance unto those who cry aloud: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!
*Troparion 4:*
Vouchsafe to heal the bodily weaknesses and soul-afflicting ailments of those who with love draw near unto thy shelter, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, who didst bear Christ the Savior for us!
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Praise the King of heaven, Whom the angelic hosts hymn, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^691]
*Troparion 1:*
Despise not those who require the help that comes from thee, O Virgin, who hymn and supremely exalt thee unto ages!
*Troparion 2:*
Thou healest the infirmity of my soul and bodily pains, O Virgin, that I may glorify thee, O pure one, unto ages!
*Troparion 3:*
Thou pourest forth the wealth of healings unto those who faithfully hymn thee, O Virgin, and supremely exalt thine ineffable childbearing!
*Troparion 4:*
Thou drivest away the assaults of temptations and the onslaughts of passions, O Virgin: therefore we hymn thee unto all ages!
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Truly we confess thee as the Mother of God, who through thee have been saved, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.[^692]
*Troparion 1:*
Turn not away from the stream of my tears, O Virgin, who didst bear Christ, Who hath taken away every tear from every face!
*Troparion 2:*
Fill my heart with joy, O Virgin, who didst receive the fullness of joy, consuming the sorrow of sin!

*Theotokion Canons*
*Troparion 3:*
With the rays of thy light enlighten, O Virgin, driving away the gloom of ignorance, those who piously confess thee as the Mother of God!
*Troparion 4:*
In the place of the affliction of infirmity, heal him who hath been brought low, O Virgin, transforming him from illness into health!

## Resurrectional Theotokia of the Eight Tones, in the Evening at "Lord, I Have Cried" and at the Aposticha
*Tone 1. At "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Let us hymn the Virgin Mary, the glory of all the world, who budded forth from men and bare the Master, the gate of heaven, the song of the bodiless hosts, and the adornment of the faithful! For she was shown forth as heaven and the temple of the Godhead: she, having broken down the barrier of enmity, ushered in peace and opened the Kingdom! Having her therefore as the firm foundation of faith, we have the Lord Who was born of her as our Defender! Take courage therefore, take courage, O people of God, for He will conquer the enemies, as He is all-powerful![^693]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Behold, the prophecy of Isaiah is fulfilled: for as a Virgin thou didst give birth, and after giving birth thou didst remain as thou wast before giving birth; for God was the One born, therefore He renewed the course of nature. But O Mother of God, despise not the supplications of thy servants offered unto thee in thy temple: but as carrying the Compassionate One in thine arms, have mercy upon thy servants, and pray that our souls may be saved![^694]
*Tone 2. Theotokion:*
The shadow of the Law passed away when grace arrived: for as the bush was not consumed when wrapped in flame, so as a Virgin thou didst give birth, and a Virgin thou didst remain. Instead of a pillar of fire, the righteous Sun shone forth; instead of Moses, Christ the salvation of our souls![^695]

*Resurrectional Theotokia of the Eight Tones*
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
O new wonder of all ancient wonders! For who hath known a Mother who gave birth without a husband, and carried in her arms Him Who upholds all creation? The One born is the counsel of God! Whom as an infant having carried in thine arms, O all-pure one, and possessing maternal boldness toward Him, cease not to pray for those who honor thee, that He may have compassion and save our souls![^696]
*Tone 3. Theotokion:*
How shall we not marvel at thy God-man childbearing, O most honorable one? For having received no temptation of a man, O all-immaculate one, thou didst give birth in the flesh without a father unto the Son, Who was born of the Father without a mother before the ages, Who suffered no change, nor mingling, nor division, but preserved entire the property of both natures. Therefore, O Mother, Virgin, Sovereign Lady, pray Him to save the souls of those who in orthodox manner confess thee as the Mother of God![^697]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Without seed from the Holy Spirit, but by the will of the Father, thou didst conceive the Son of God, Who exists before the ages from the Father without a mother, and Who for our sake became of thee without a father; thou didst give birth unto Him in the flesh and didst nurse Him as an infant with milk. Therefore cease not to pray that our souls may be delivered from dangers![^698]
*Tone 4. Theotokion:*
David the Prophet, the forefather of God, melodiously proclaimed beforehand concerning thee unto Him Who hath done great things for thee: "The Queen stood at Thy right hand." For God, Who was pleased to become man of thee without a father, revealed thee as the Mother and mediatress of life, that He might renew His own image corrupted by passions; and having found the lost sheep wandered upon the mountains, taking it upon His shoulder, He might bring it unto the Father, and according to His own will *Resurrectional Theotokia of the Eight Tones*
unite it with the heavenly hosts, and Christ, Who hath great and rich mercy, may save the world, O Mother of God![^699]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Look down upon the entreaties of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, calming the grievous uprisings against us, freeing us from every sorrow: for thee alone do we have as a firm and certain foundation, and we have acquired thy protection, that we who call upon thee may not be put to shame, O Sovereign Lady! Hasten unto the supplication of those who faithfully cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady, help of all, joy, protection, and salvation of our souls![^700]
*Tone 5. Theotokion:*
In the Red Sea the image of the unwedded Bride was inscribed of old: there Moses was the divider of the water; but here Gabriel was the minister of the wonder. Then Israel traversed the deep without being wetted; but now the Virgin hath given birth unto Christ without seed. The sea remained impassable after the passage of Israel; the immaculate one remained incorrupt after the birth of Emmanuel. He Who is and Who was before, having been revealed as man, O God, have mercy on us![^701]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Thou art the temple and the gate, the palace and the throne of the King, O all-honorable Virgin: through whom my Deliverer, Christ the Lord, shone forth upon those that slept in darkness, being the Sun of Righteousness, wishing to enlighten those whom He created according to His own image with His own hand. Therefore, O all-hymned one, as having acquired maternal boldness toward Him, unceasingly pray that our souls may be saved![^702]
*Tone 6. Theotokion:*
Who will not bless thee, O most holy Virgin? Who will not hymn thy most pure childbearing? For the only-begotten Son, Who shone timelessly from the Father, *Resurrectional Theotokia of the Eight Tones*
the Same came forth from thee, O pure one, having become ineffably incarnate: being God by nature, and having become man by nature for our sake, not divided into two persons, but known in two natures without confusion. Pray Him, O pure and all-blessed one, that our souls may be shown mercy![^703]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
My Maker and Redeemer, Christ the Lord, having come forth from thy womb, O all-pure one, and clothed Himself in me, delivered Adam from the former curse. Therefore unto thee, O all-pure one, as truly the Mother of God and Virgin, we cry unceasingly with the angel: Rejoice! Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady, protection, shelter, and salvation of our souls![^704]
*Tone 7. Theotokion:*
Thou wast known as a Mother above nature, O Mother of God, yet didst remain a Virgin, above word and understanding; and the wonder of thy childbearing no tongue can declare. For the conception being most glorious, O pure one, the manner of the birth is incomprehensible: for where God so wills, the order of nature is overcome. Therefore knowing thee to be the Mother of God, we earnestly pray thee: Entreat that our souls may be saved![^705]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
All us earthborn fleeing under thy protection, O Sovereign Lady, cry aloud unto thee: O Mother of God, our hope, deliver us from immeasurable transgressions, and save our souls![^706]
*Tone 8. Theotokion:*
The King of heaven, through love for mankind, was revealed upon the earth and lived with men: for having taken flesh from the pure Virgin, and having come forth from her with the assumption, He is one Son, twofold in nature, but not in hypostasis. Therefore, truly proclaiming Him as perfect *Resurrectional Theotokia of the Eight Tones*
God and perfect man, we confess Christ our God. Whom entreat, O unwedded Mother, that our souls may be shown mercy![^707]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
O unwedded Virgin, who didst ineffably conceive God in the flesh, Mother of the Most High God: accept the supplications of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, granting unto all purification of transgressions! Receiving now our entreaties, pray that we all may be saved![^708]

## Theotokia of the Eight Tones, Sung When There Is "Glory..." to the Saint in the Menaion, in the Evening at "Lord, I Have Cried" and at the Aposticha, and in the Morning at the Aposticha: "Both now...", According to the Tone[^709]
*Tone 1*
*On Sunday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
O Mary all-immaculate, who surpassest every understanding, purest chariot: direct me, who am held fast and overwhelmed by many sins, unto the breadth of repentance through thy most powerful protection! For thou canst do all things, as the Mother of the Almighty![^710]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Joy of the heavenly hosts, mighty protection of men upon earth, O all-pure Virgin: save us who flee unto thee, for in thee next to God have we placed our hope, O Mother of God![^711]
*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
Holier than all the holy powers, more honorable than all creation, O Mother of God, Sovereign Lady of the world: save us, who didst bear the Savior, from myriad transgressions and calamities, as thou art good, by *Theotokia of the Eight Tones*
thy prayers![^712]
*On Monday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Together with the Lamb that takes away the sins of the whole world, Whom the most great Forerunner preached unto all, pray Him, O Virgin, to show me a stranger to the portion of the goats in the hour of judgment, and to number me, unworthy though I be, with His sheep at the right hand![^713]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
O all-hymned Virgin, the mystery in thee did Moses see with prophetic eyes: the bush burning and unconsumed; for the fire of the Godhead did not consume thy womb, O pure one! Therefore we pray thee, as the Mother of our God, to entreat peace for the world and great mercy![^714]
*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
I have surpassed the harlot, the prodigal, and the thief, and in transgressions have outdone the Publican and the Ninevites: woe is me, what shall I become? How shall I escape torment, wretch that I am? O pure one, I fall down before thee: show me compassion by thy mercy, even as Thy Son saved them![^715]
*On Wednesday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Tempest-tossed by the deep billow of transgressions, I have fled unto the tranquil harbor of thy most pure prayer, O Birthgiver of God, and I cry aloud unto thee: Save me, stretching forth thy mighty right hand unto thy servant, O all-immaculate one![^716]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, joy of the righteous, gladness of the Apostles and Martyrs, and protection of us thy servants, O Virgin![^717]
*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mother of God, Virgin; rejoice, boast of the whole inhabited earth; rejoice, all-pure Mother of God, blessed one![^718]
*On Friday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: Dogmatikon of the concurring tone in the Octoechos.*[^719]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*

*Theotokia of the Eight Tones*
Truly marvelous above understanding are the majesties of thy childbearing, O Bride of God, which all the prophets proclaimed, all most glorious: conception and childbearing, O all-hymned one, incomprehensible and ineffable, by which He hath saved the world, as He is compassionate![^720]
*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
Rejoice from us, holy Mother of God, Virgin, pure vessel, unquenchable lamp of the whole inhabited earth, tabernacle of the Uncontainable, indestructible temple; rejoice, of whom was born the Lamb of God, Who takes away the sins of the whole world![^721]
*Tone 2*
*On Sunday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
The multitude of the angels of thy Son, O all-pure one, hymn thee with unceasing voices as His fiery throne, animate palace, and divine bridge that ever leads the earthborn unto Him, crying aloud unto thee in harmony with the Archangel Gabriel: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace, as having given birth unto the Fountain of joy![^722]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, Mary Mother of God, indestructible temple, and exceedingly holy, as the Prophet cries aloud: "Holy is Thy temple, wonderful in righteousness!"[^723]
*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
In thee have we placed our hope, O Mother of God, let us not fall away from expectation: save us from calamities, O Helper of the perplexed, and scatter the counsels of adversaries, for thou art our salvation, O blessed one![^724]
*On Monday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Sorrows and assaults of grievous things, and diverse passions weigh down my humble soul: O unwedded Maiden, Mother of Christ God, appear unto me as a pilot upon the sea *Theotokia of the Eight Tones*
of this life, and calm the impending storm, guiding me unto the tranquil harbor of repentance and refreshment, as I flee unto thy divine protection![^725]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Impassable gate, mystically sealed, blessed Mother of God, Virgin: accept our supplications, and offer them unto thy Son and God, that He may save our souls through thee![^726]
*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, Mary Mother of God, indestructible temple, and exceedingly holy, as the Prophet cries aloud: "Holy is Thy temple, wonderful in righteousness!"[^727]
*On Wednesday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
The Word Who is equally honored with the Father and the Holy Spirit, like a great Sun, having shone forth upon earth from the Virgin Maiden of God in the last times, sent forth the glorious Apostles as rays, enlightening all men in the darkness of delusion with the light of faith, and leading them unto Him through divine teachings.[^728]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
As a fruitful olive tree, O Virgin, didst thou sprout forth for thee the Fruit of life, to bring forth great and rich mercy for the world.[^729]
*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
All my hope I place upon thee, O Mother of God: preserve me under thy protection![^730]
*On Friday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: Dogmatikon of the concurring tone in the Octoechos.*
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Save from dangers thy servants, O Mother of God, Virgin: for next to God we all flee unto thee, as unto an unbreakable wall and *Theotokia of the Eight Tones*
protection![^731]
*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
Come, calling upon the Mother of Light with unceasing hymns, all ye people, let us glorify her, for she gave birth unto our salvation; and let us offer "Rejoice!", as the only one who bare the primordial God before the ages! Rejoice, thou who didst restore fallen Eve! Rejoice, all-pure Virgin unwedded![^732]
*Tone 3*
*On Sunday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
O all-pure one who rulest over all creatures: free my mind, which is sorely held fast by passions, through thy fervent protection and maternal prayer, and make me a servant unto thy Son and God![^733]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, protectress of all that pray to thee: in thee do we have boldness, and in thee do we boast, and in thee is all our hope: pray Him Who was born of thee on behalf of thine unprofitable servants![^734]
*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
Being the most primordial pure praise of the heavenly hosts, the song of the Apostles, and the fulfillment of the Prophets, O Sovereign Lady, accept our prayers![^735]
*On Monday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
O cloud of the unwaning Light, divinely rejoiced Maiden, blessed Mary: shine forth upon me the light of repentance, who am foolishly held fast in the darkness of sin; and deliver me by thy prayers from the fire of Gehenna and the dark without light, and show me forth, O Virgin, a partaker of the day that knows no evening, as I flee beneath thy protection, O all-pure one![^736]

*Theotokia of the Eight Tones*
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Among women, holy Mother of God, unwedded Mother: pray Him Whom thou didst bear, the King and God, that He may save us, as He is the Lover of mankind![^737]
*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
Without seed thou didst conceive of the Holy Spirit, and glorifying, we hymn thee: Rejoice, O most holy Virgin![^738]
*On Wednesday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Great is the power of thy wonders, O pure one: for thou deliverest from dangers, and savest from death, and snatchest away from unlooked-for misfortunes, and dissolvest griefs, and takest away the transgressions of men![^739]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Snatch me, who have greatly sinned, O Maiden, by thy great prayer from the violent flame; and guide me, O pure one, by thy supplications, directing me unto saving paths through thy maternal prayers![^740]
*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
Without seed thou didst conceive of the Holy Spirit, and glorifying, we hymn thee: Rejoice, O most holy Virgin!
*On Friday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: Dogmatikon of the concurring tone in the Octoechos.*
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Among women, holy Mother of God, unwedded Mother: pray Him Whom thou didst bear, the King and God, that He may save us, as He is the Lover of mankind!
*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
Without seed thou didst conceive of the Holy Spirit, and glorifying, we hymn thee: Rejoice, O most holy Virgin![^741]

*Theotokia of the Eight Tones*
*Tone 4*
*On Sunday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Surpassing the angelic hosts, O all-immaculate one, with the angels ever pray Him Who holds dominion over angels and all creation, to grant us remission of transgressions and deliverance from passions, and to make us then worthy to be singers of His glory and inheritors of incorruptible food![^742]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, cloud of light; rejoice, radiant lampstand; rejoice, jar wherein is the manna; rejoice, rod of Aaron; rejoice, bush unconsumed; rejoice, bridal chamber; rejoice, throne; rejoice, holy mountain; rejoice, refuge; rejoice, divine table; rejoice, mystic door; rejoice, joy of all![^743]
*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, Queen of all, boast of the Orthodox: tear down the pride of heretics, and put to shame their faces who neither bow down before nor honor thine honorable icon, O all-pure one![^744]
*On Monday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Grant me tears from the heart, O Maiden, and sighing from the depths, and contrition of soul, and confession of the transgressions committed by me in this life: that by thy help, O all-pure one, I may pass my life in repentance and obtain relief![^745]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Deliver us from our necessities, O Mother of Christ God, who didst bear the Creator of all, that we all may cry unto thee: Rejoice, only protection of our souls![^746]
*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
Preserve thy servants from all dangers, O blessed Mother of God, *Theotokia of the Eight Tones*
that we may glorify thee, the hope of our souls![^747]
*On Wednesday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
O thou that didst contain in thy womb the uncontainable God, Who through love for mankind became man, and took our mixture from thee and manifestly deified it: despise me not now who am in affliction, O all-pure one, but quickly show compassion, and free me from diverse enmities and the harm of the evil one![^748]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Having thee, O Mother of God, as our hope and protection, we shall not fear the plots of the enemy, for thou savest our souls![^749]
*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
We have acquired thee as a wall, O all-pure Mother of God, and a calm harbor, and a firm foundation. Therefore I pray, who am tempest-tossed in this life: Pilot me, and save me![^750]
*On Friday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: Dogmatikon of the concurring tone in the Octoechos.*
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Unquenchable lamp, righteous throne, O all-pure Sovereign Lady: pray that our souls may be saved![^751]
*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
O only pure and all-immaculate Virgin, who didst bear God without seed: pray that our souls may be saved![^752]
*Tone 5*
*On Sunday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Truly wast thou the cherubic throne, as being higher than the angels: for in thee, O pure one, the divine Word made His dwelling, wishing to renew our image. Having come forth from thee bearing flesh as the Compassionate One, He accepted the Cross and death for our sake, and granted resurrection as God. Therefore, as thou didst exchange us from condemned nature, with faith thanking the Creator, we pray: That through thy prayers we may obtain[^753] pardon of transgressions, and great mercy.[^754]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
A fearful, and most glorious, and great mystery: the Uncontainable was contained within the womb, and the Mother, after giving birth, again remained a Virgin; for she gave birth unto God incarnate from her. Unto Him let us cry aloud, unto Him let us say a song, hymning with the angels: Holy art Thou, O Christ God, Who for our sake didst become man, glory to Thee![^755]
*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
O thou who art full of grace, intercede by thy prayers, and ask for our souls a multitude of compassions, and cleansing of many transgressions, we pray.[^756]
*On Monday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Entirely from infancy have I shown myself ceaselessly working sins: for I have been sorely wounded in mind, and by long habit I have abided in them through love. And now mourning, I weep for my grievous deception, and my evil custom and folly, and the perdition of my soul: O Sovereign Lady, despise me not who am perishing evilly, but showing compassion, deliver me by thy protection from every assault and from passions, that at least in old age I may repent unto God.[^757]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Assuage the pains of my much-sighing soul, O thou that didst wipe away every tear from the face of the earth: for thou drivest away the diseases of men, and dost scatter the sorrows of sinners. Thee have we all acquired as hope and confirmation, O most holy Virgin Mother.[^758]
*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
O thou who art full of grace, intercede by thy prayers, and ask for our souls a multitude of compassions, and cleansing of many transgressions, we pray.[^759]
*On Wednesday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Rejoice, prophetic seal, and preaching of the God-proclaiming Apostles: for truly existing God, incarnate above mind and word, hast thou born, O pure one; through Whom having received our original nobility, and enjoying paradise's food, we honor thee in hymns as the mediatress of such radiance and acceptable intercessor. Having been enriched through thee, O all-pure one, we are accounted worthy of the everlasting life of thy Son, Who richly grants great mercy.[^760]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
We call thee blessed, O Virgin Mother of God, and we the faithful glorify thee as is due: the unshakable city, the impregnable wall, the firm protectress, and the refuge of our souls.[^761]
*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
We call thee blessed, O Virgin Mother of God, for from thee hath shone forth the Sun of Righteousness, Christ, Who possesses great mercy.[^762]
*On Friday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: Dogmatikon of the concurring tone in the Octoechos.*
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Thee we entreat as the Mother of God: O blessed one, pray for the salvation of our souls![^763]
*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
O wretched soul, what answer wilt thou give unto the Judge in that hour, when the thrones shall be set for judgment, and the Judge shall come from heaven, having descended with myriads of angels, and shall sit upon the judgment seat to enter into controversy with unprofitable servants like unto me? What wilt thou answer? What wilt thou bring forward then? Truly nothing, having defiled thy mind and body! Therefore fall down before the Virgin and cry unceasingly, that He may richly grant thee forgiveness of sins![^764]

*Tone 6*
*On Sunday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Having conceived in thy womb, O Mother of God, at the Archangel's voice, the Word co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, thou wast shown forth higher than the Cherubim, and the Seraphim, and the Thrones.[^765]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
In archangelic manner let us the faithful hymn the heavenly bridal chamber and the door truly sealed: Rejoice, for whose sake Christ, the Savior of all, the Giver of life and God, budded forth for us! Cast down, O Sovereign Lady, the tyrants, our godless enemies, by thy hand, O all-pure one, the hope of Christians![^766]
*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
Thou didst receive the archangelic word, and wast shown forth as the cherubic throne: for in thine arms, O Mother of God, thou didst bear the hope of our souls.[^767]
*On Monday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
The abyss of thy love for mankind, and the depth of thy tender mercy, and the unreckonable compassions of thy goodness, show forth, O all-pure Virgin, upon me the wretched one: assuage the pasturage of sin, granting chastity, and preserve undefiled both my soul and my body, O thou that didst bear the Savior![^768]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
No one who flees unto thee goes forth from thee put to shame, O all-pure Virgin Mother of God, but asks for grace, and receives a gift according unto beneficial petition.[^769]
*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
Of great gifts, O pure Virgin Mother of God, wast thou accounted worthy: for thou didst bear in the flesh One of the Trinity, Christ the Giver of life, unto the salvation of our souls.[^770]
*On Wednesday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Envy-stricken at thy flock, O all-pure one, the evil adversary ever wages war, wishing to make it food for himself: but do thou, O Mother of God, deliver us from his harm![^771]

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
The eye of my heart I direct unto thee, O Sovereign Lady: despise not my faint sighing; in the hour when thy Son shall judge the world, be thou my shelter and helper![^772]
*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
Transformation of the sorrowful, relief of the ailing, O all-hymned Mother of God: save the city and the people, O pacification of the embattled, stillness of the tempest-tossed, only protectress of the faithful![^773]
*On Friday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: Dogmatikon of the concurring tone in the Octoechos.*
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
By the prayers of her that bare Thee, O Christ, of Thy Martyrs, and Apostles, and Prophets, and Hierarchs, of the Venerable, and Righteous, and all the Saints, give rest unto Thy departed servants![^774]
*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
God incarnate of thee have we known, O Virgin Mother of God: pray Him for the salvation of our souls![^775]
*Tone 7*
*On Sunday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
With these hymns, together with the angels, let us cry aloud unto the Mother of God: for thou didst bear the Savior of the world, and after giving birth again remained a Virgin, and delivered the world from delusion by thy childbearing, and gave us inexhaustible food, nursing with milk the Liberator of our souls![^776]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
"Rejoice!" we cry unto thee with the angel, O Bride of God, calling thee bridal chamber, and door, and fiery throne, and uncut mountain, and bush unconsumed.[^777]
*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
Pacify our life by the prayers of the Mother of God, as we cry unto Thee: O merciful Lord, glory to Thee![^778]
*On Monday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Pilot me who am tossed by the tumults of sloth, O Sovereign Lady, into the calm haven of salvation![^779]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, cloud of the noetic and ineffable Sun, O Sovereign Lady; rejoice, all-radiant lamp; rejoice, all-golden lampstand! Through thee, O most holy one, Eve was delivered from the curse. But as having boldness toward thy most gracious Son and God, fail not in praying by thy maternal intercession, O all-pure one![^780]
*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
As Light, O Christ, Thou didst sprout forth from the Virgin, and didst enlighten the human race: O Lord, glory to Thee![^781]
*On Wednesday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Offer the entreaty of thy servants unto thy Son, O all-pure one, that He may save all whom He created![^782]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Let us hymn the only Virgin after giving birth, as the Mother of God the Word, saying: Glory to thee![^783]
*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
Thou wast born ineffably of the Virgin, O Christ, and didst enlighten those in darkness who cry: O Lord, glory to Thee![^784]
*On Friday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: Dogmatikon of the concurring tone in the Octoechos.*
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
O thou who alone didst receive the Uncontainable, and didst bear God the Word incarnate: pray that our souls may be saved![^785]

*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
Pray, O Virgin, with the Apostles and Martyrs, that the departed may find great mercy at the judgment![^786]
*Tone 8*
*On Sunday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Ever do I, the creature, grieve and provoke the Creator: grant me correction, O Maiden, before that departure; and by thy help raise me up unto pleasing works, that I may obtain salvation and forgiveness![^787]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Having received the voice of the Archangel Gabriel, let us say: Rejoice, Mother of God, who didst bear Christ the Giver of life unto the world![^788]
*On Monday Morning, Theotokion:*
The heavenly hosts hymn thee, O thou who art full of grace, unwedded Mother, and we glorify thine unsearchable childbearing, O Mother of God: pray that our souls may be saved![^789]
*On Monday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
With faith I flee unto thy protection, O pure Birthgiver of God: from trials, and perils, and passionate confusion, and from demonic malice save me, as possessing the abyss of mercy, and shown forth as mediatress of salvation, O Sovereign Lady, who didst bear the merciful God, the only all-compassionate and plenteously merciful One![^790]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, boast of the universe! Rejoice, temple of the Lord! Rejoice, overshadowing mountain! Rejoice, refuge of all! Rejoice, golden lampstand! Rejoice, honorable glory of the Orthodox! Rejoice, Mary Mother of Christ God! Rejoice, paradise! Rejoice, divine table! Rejoice, tabernacle! Rejoice, all-golden jar! Rejoice, hope of all![^791]

*On Tuesday Morning, Theotokion:*
Thy shelter, O Virgin Mother of God, is spiritual medicine: for fleeing under it, we are delivered from the diseases of the soul.[^792]
*On Wednesday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion:*
Look down upon the sighing of my contrite heart, O Bride of God; receive, O Virgin Mary, pure one, the lifting up of my hands, as thou lovest mankind, and reject me not, O all-immaculate one, that I may hymn and magnify thee who hast magnified our race![^793]
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
I, O holy Virgin Mother of God, fleeing unto thy protection, know that through thee I shall find salvation: for thou canst help me, O pure one![^794]
*On Thursday Morning, Theotokion:*
Snatch me away, O Sovereign Lady, from the hand of the man-slaying serpent who wishes to swallow me up utterly by cunning: crush his jaws, I pray thee, and destroy his snares, that having escaped from his claws, I may magnify thy protection![^795]
*On Friday Evening, at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: Dogmatikon of the concurring tone in the Octoechos.*
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*
O pure Virgin, gate of the Word, Mother of our God: pray that we may be saved![^796]
*On Saturday Morning, Theotokion:*
O Sun, receive the prayers of thy servants, and deliver us from every necessity and sorrow![^797]

## Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokia of the Eight Tones[^798]
*Tone 1. Theotokion:*
When Gabriel proclaimed unto thee, O Virgin: "Rejoice!", with that voice the Master of all was taking flesh in thee the holy ark, as the righteous David said: thou wast shown forth more spacious than the heavens, having borne thy Creator. Glory to Him Who dwelt within thee!

Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who freed us through thy childbearing![^799]
*Tone 2. Theotokion:*
All above understanding, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Mother of God: with purity sealed and virginity preserved, thou wast known as a true Mother, having given birth unto the true God; pray Him that our souls may be saved![^800]
*Tone 3. Theotokion:*
Thee, who didst intercede for the salvation of our race, we hymn, O Virgin Mother of God: for having through the flesh received from thee accepted the Passion upon the Cross, thy Son and our God delivered us from corruption, as He is the Lover of mankind.[^801]
*Tone 4. Theotokion:*
The mystery hidden from the ages and unknown unto angels was through thee, O Mother of God, revealed unto those upon earth: God incarnate in unconfused union, and willingly accepting the Cross for our sake, whereby having resurrected the first-formed, He saved our souls from death.[^802]
*Tone 5. Theotokion:*
Rejoice, impassable door of the Lord! Rejoice, wall and protection of those who flee unto thee! Rejoice, untempest-tossed haven, and thou that knewest not wedlock, who didst bear in the flesh thy Creator and God: fail not in praying for those who hymn and worship thy childbearing![^803]
*Tone 6. Theotokion:*
Calling Thy Mother blessed, Thou didst come unto the Passion by voluntary counsel, shining forth upon the Cross, wishing to seek out Adam, saying unto the angels: Rejoice with Me, for the lost drachma is found! O our God Who hast wisely ordered all things, glory to Thee![^804]
*Tone 7. Theotokion:*
As the treasury of our resurrection, lead up from the pit and depth of transgressions those who hope in thee, O all-hymned one: for thou didst save those guilty of sin, having given birth unto our Salvation; who before childbearing wast a Virgin, and in childbearing a Virgin, and after childbearing again dost remain a Virgin.[^805]
*Tone 8. Theotokion:*
Thou Who for our sake wast born of a Virgin, and didst endure crucifixion, O Good One, Who didst overthrow death by death, and reveal resurrection as God: despise not those whom Thou didst create with Thine own hand; show forth Thy love for mankind, O merciful One; receive the Mother of God who bare Thee, who intercedes for us, and save, O our Savior, a despairing people![^806]

## Dismissal Theotokia, Sung After the Troparia of the Saints Throughout the Whole Year, at Vespers, and at "God is the Lord" at Matins, and Again at the End of Matins[^807]
*Tone 1*
*On Sunday Evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
Beholding the wonder of wonders in thee, O full of grace, creation rejoices: for thou didst conceive without seed, and didst bear ineffably Him Whom the ranks of angels cannot behold; pray Him for our souls![^808]
*At the End of Matins:*
O all-pure Mother of God, blessed in the heavens, and glorified upon earth: Rejoice, unwedded Bride![^809]
*On Monday Evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
O thou that didst unconsumed conceive the divine Fire, and without seed didst bear the Fountain of life, the Lord: O Mother of God full of grace, save those who magnify thee![^810]
*At the End of Matins:*
Him Who is motherless in heaven above thought and hearing, didst thou bear upon earth without a Father: pray Him, O Mother of God, for our souls![^811]
*On Tuesday Evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
Having thy protection, O all-pure one, and by thy prayers being delivered from grievous things, preserved everywhere by the Cross of thy Son, as is due we all piously magnify thee.[^812]
*At the End of Matins:*
O all-pure Mother of God, blessed in the heavens: (*see above*).[^813]
*On Wednesday Evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
O thou that didst unconsumed conceive the divine Fire: (*see above*).[^814]
*At the End of Matins:*
Him Who is motherless in heaven: (*see above*).[^815]
*On Thursday Evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
Having thy protection, O all-pure one: (*see above*).[^816]
*At the End of Matins:*
O all-pure Mother of God, blessed in the heavens: (*see above*).[^817]
*On Friday Evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
When Gabriel proclaimed unto thee, O Virgin: "Rejoice!", with that voice the Master of all was taking flesh in thee the holy ark, as the righteous David said: thou wast shown forth more spacious than the heavens, having borne thy Creator. Glory to Him Who dwelt within thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who freed us through thy childbearing![^818]
*At the End of Matins:*
Him Who is motherless in heaven: (*see above*).[^819]

*Tone 2*
*On Sunday Evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
As thou art a fountain of tender mercy, account us worthy of compassion, O Mother of God; look down upon a sinning people; show forth thy power as ever: for hoping in thee, we cry unto thee: Rejoice! as once did Gabriel, commander of the bodiless hosts.[^820]
*At the End of Matins:*
Holy Mother of ineffable Light, honoring thee with angelic hymns, we piously magnify thee.[^821]
*On Monday Evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
Partakers of the divine nature have we become through thee, O Mother of God, Ever-Virgin: for thou didst bear unto us God incarnate. Therefore as is due we all piously magnify thee.[^822]
*At the End of Matins:*
Thee we magnify, O Mother of God, crying aloud: Rejoice, cloud of the unwaning Light, who didst carry Him in thy bosom, the Lord of Glory![^823]
*On Tuesday Evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
O most glorified Virgin Mother of God, we hymn thee: for by the Cross of thy Son hades was overthrown, and death was put to death; we who were slain are arisen, and have been accounted worthy of life, and have received paradise, the ancient enjoyment. Therefore giving thanks we glorify Christ our God as mighty, and the only plenteously merciful One.[^824]
*At the End of Matins:*
Thee we magnify, O Mother of God, crying aloud: Rejoice, rod from which without seed God budded forth, destroying death upon the Tree![^825]
*On Wednesday Evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
Partakers of the divine nature: (*see above*).[^826]
*At the End of Matins:*
Thee we magnify, O Mother of God, crying aloud: (*see above*).[^827]

*On Thursday Evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
O most glorified Virgin Mother of God: (*see above*).[^828]
*At the End of Matins:*
Thee we magnify, O Mother of God, crying aloud: Rejoice, rod: (*see above*).[^829]
*On Friday Evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
All above understanding, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Mother of God: with purity sealed and virginity preserved, thou wast known as a true Mother, having given birth unto the true God; pray Him that our souls may be saved![^830]
*At the End of Matins:*
Holy Mother of ineffable Light: (*see above*).[^831]
*If "Alleluia" occurs in any tone whatsoever, these are said:*
Apostles, Martyrs, and Prophets, Hierarchs, Venerable, and Righteous, who have well finished the course and kept the faith: having boldness toward the Savior, entreat Him as good on our behalf, we pray, that our souls may be saved![^832]
Remember, O Lord, as Thou art good, the souls of Thy servants, and whatever they have sinned in life forgive: for none is sinless save Thee, Who art able to give rest even unto the departed.[^833]
*Theotokion:* Holy Mother of ineffable Light: (*see above*).[^834]
*Tone 3*
*On Sunday Evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
Marveling at the beauty of thy virginity and the exceeding brilliance of thy purity, Gabriel cried aloud unto thee, O Mother of God: What worthy praise shall I bring unto thee? What shall I name thee? I am in doubt and marvel! Therefore, as I was commanded, I cry unto thee: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace![^835]

*At the End of Matins:*
Whithersoever anyone is saved, thither justly does he also flee: and what other such refuge is there as thou, O Mother of God, sheltering our souls?[^836]
*On Monday Evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
Our refuge and strength, O Mother of God, mighty help of the world: by thy prayers shelter thy servants from every necessity, O only blessed one![^837]
*At the End of Matins:*
Whithersoever anyone is saved: (*see above*).[^838]
*On Tuesday Evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
Having acquired the Cross of thy Son as a rod of strength, O Mother of God, thereby we cast down the haughtiness of enemies, unceasingly magnifying thee with love.[^839]
*At the End of Matins:*
Our refuge and strength, O Mother of God: (*see above*).[^840]
*On Wednesday Evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
The Prophets proclaimed, the Apostles taught, the Martyrs confessed, and we have believed thee to be truly the Mother of God: therefore we also magnify thine ineffable childbearing.[^841]
*At the End of Matins:*
Whithersoever anyone is saved: (*see above*).[^842]
*On Thursday Evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
Having acquired the Cross of thy Son as a rod of strength: (*see above*).[^843]
*At the End of Matins:*
Our refuge and strength, O Mother of God: (*see above*).[^844]
*On Friday Evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
Thee, who didst intercede for the salvation of our race, we hymn, O Virgin Mother of God: for having through the flesh received from thee accepted the Passion upon the Cross, thy Son and our God delivered us from corruption, as He is the Lover of mankind.[^845]

*At the End of Matins:*
The Prophets proclaimed, the Apostles taught: (*see above*).[^846]
*Tone 4*
*On Sunday Evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
Unto thee who wast reared in the temple in the Holy of Holies, clothed with faith, and wisdom, and boundless virginity, the arch-commander Gabriel brought the salutation from heaven, and the "Rejoice!": Rejoice, blessed one; rejoice, most glorified one: the Lord is with thee![^847]
*At the End of Matins:*
As thou art higher than all creation, being in doubt how to hymn thee worthily, O Mother of God, we entreat thee: Freely have mercy on us![^848]
*On Monday Evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
Unto the Mother of God let us sinners and humble ones now earnestly flee, and let us fall down, crying in repentance from the depths of the soul: O Sovereign Lady, help, having shown mercy upon us; hasten, for we are perishing from the multitude of transgressions! Turn not thy servants away empty, for thee alone do we have as hope![^849]
*At the End of Matins:*
Thee we magnify, O Mother of God, crying aloud: Thou art the bush wherein Moses unconsumed beheld, as a flame, the fire of the Godhead.[^850]
*On Tuesday Evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
O all-immaculate Virgin, Mother of Christ God, a sword pierced thy most holy soul when thou didst behold thy Son and God willingly crucified: Whom cease not to entreat, O blessed one, that He may grant us forgiveness of transgressions![^851]
*At the End of Matins:*
Thee we magnify, O Mother of God, crying aloud: Thou art the mountain from which ineffably a stone was cut without hands, and crushed the gates of hades.[^852]

*On Wednesday Evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
The Word of the Father, Christ our God, Who was incarnate of thee, have we known, O Virgin Mother of God, only pure one, only blessed one: therefore unceasingly hymning thee, we magnify thee.[^853]
*At the End of Matins:*
As thou art higher than all creation: (*see above*).[^854]
*On Thursday Evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
O all-immaculate Virgin, Mother of Christ God: (*see above*).[^855]
*At the End of Matins:*
Thee we magnify, O Mother of God, crying aloud: Thou art the mountain: (*see above*).[^856]
*On Friday Evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
The mystery hidden from the ages and unknown unto angels was through thee, O Mother of God, revealed unto those upon earth: God incarnate in unconfused union, and willingly accepting the Cross for our sake, whereby having resurrected the first-formed, He saved our souls from death.[^857]
*At the End of Matins:*
Thee we magnify, O Mother of God, crying aloud: Thou art the bush: (*see above*).[^858]
*Tone 5*
*On Sunday Evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
With the angels the heavenly ones, with men the earthly ones, with the voice of gladness, O Mother of God, we cry unto thee: Rejoice, door more spacious than the heavens! Rejoice, only salvation of the earthborn! Rejoice, pure one full of grace, who didst bear God incarnate![^859]
*At the End of Matins:*
O Christ God, Who didst shine forth upon the world from the Virgin, and through her didst show forth sons of light: Have mercy on us![^860]
*On Monday Evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
Thy speedy protection, and help, and mercy show forth upon thy servants, and calm the waves of vain thoughts, O pure one, and raise up my fallen soul, O Mother of God: for I know, yea I know, O Virgin, that thou canst do whatsoever thou desirest.[^861]
*At the End of Matins:*
All-pure Mother of God, wall of Christians: deliver thy people as is thy wont, who earnestly cry unto thee; oppose shameful and obstinate thoughts, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, Ever-Virgin![^862]
*On Tuesday Evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
By the Cross of thy Son, O Mother of God full of grace, all idolatrous delusion was abolished, and the demonic strength was trampled down: therefore as is due we the faithful ever hymn and bless thee, and confessing thee to be truly the Mother of God, we magnify thee.[^863]
*At the End of Matins:*
O Christ God, Who didst shine forth upon the world from the Virgin: (*see above*).[^864]
*On Wednesday Evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
The strange mystery of the Virgin was shown forth unto the world as salvific: for of her wast Thou born without seed, and in the flesh wast Thou revealed without corruption, O Joy of all: Lord, glory to Thee![^865]
*At the End of Matins:*
All-pure Mother of God, wall: (*see above*).[^866]
*On Thursday Evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
By the Cross of thy Son, O Mother of God full of grace: (*see above*).[^867]
*At the End of Matins:*
O Christ God, Who didst shine forth upon the world from the Virgin: (*see above*).[^868]
*On Friday Evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
Rejoice, impassable door of the Lord! Rejoice, wall and protection of those who flee unto thee! Rejoice, untempest-tossed haven, and thou that knewest not wedlock, who didst bear in the flesh thy Creator and God: fail not in praying for those who hymn and worship thy childbearing![^869]

*At the End of Matins:*
O Christ God, Who didst shine forth upon the world from the Virgin: (*see above*).[^870]
*Tone 6*
*On Sunday Evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
The beginning of salvation was Gabriel's utterance unto the Virgin: for she heard the "Rejoice!", and did not shun the greeting, nor did she doubt as Sarah in the tent, but thus she spoke: Behold the handmaid of the Lord; be it unto me according to thy word![^871]
*At the End of Matins:*
Thou didst receive the archangelic word, and wast shown forth as the cherubic throne, and in thine arms, O Mother of God, thou didst bear the hope of our souls.[^872]
*On Monday Evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
O good Virgin Mother of God, the hope of the world: we ask thy sole and sovereign protection; have mercy upon a helpless people; pray the merciful God, that our souls may be delivered from every threatening, O only blessed one![^873]
*At the End of Matins:*
No one who flees unto thee goes forth from thee put to shame, O all-pure Virgin Mother of God: but asks for grace, and receives a gift according unto beneficial petition.[^874]
*On Tuesday Evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
O Virgin Mother of God, pray thy Son, Who was willingly nailed upon the Cross, and freed the world from delusion, Christ our God, to have mercy on our souls![^875]
*At the End of Matins:*
The Son and Word of God, begotten before the ages of the Father without a mother, didst thou bear in the latter times, incarnate of thy pure blood without a husband, O Birthgiver of God:

pray Him that forgiveness of sins may be granted us before the end![^876]
*On Wednesday Evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
Holy pure Mother of God, Mother of our God, who didst ineffably bear the Creator of all: with the sacred Apostles ever entreat His goodness to deliver us from passions, and to grant us remission of sins![^877]
*At the End of Matins:*
Of great gifts, O pure Virgin Mother of God, wast thou accounted worthy: for thou didst bear in the flesh One of the Trinity, Christ the Giver of life, unto the salvation of our souls.[^878]
*On Thursday Evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
O Virgin Mother of God, pray thy Son: (*see above*).[^879]
*At the End of Matins:*
The Son and Word of God, begotten before the ages of the Father without a mother: (*see above*).[^880]
*On Friday Evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
Gideon foretells the conception, and David declares thy childbearing, O Mother of God: for as rain upon the fleece the Word came down into thy womb, and thou didst sprout forth without seed, O holy land, the Salvation of the world, Christ our God, O full of grace![^881]
*At the End of Matins:*
The Son and Word of God, begotten before the ages of the Father without a mother: (*see above*).[^882]
*Tone 7*
*On Sunday Evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
O undefiled Virgin Mother of God, pray thy Son with the hosts on high, that forgiveness of transgressions may be granted us before the end, who faithfully glorify thee.[^883]
*At the End of Matins:*
Thou hast surpassed the heavenly powers, for thou wast shown forth as a divine temple, O blessed Mother of God, having given birth unto Christ, the Savior of our souls.[^884]

*On Monday Evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
"Rejoice!" we bring unto thee, O Mother of God, for thou wast shown forth higher than the angels, having given birth unto God.[^885]
*At the End of Matins:*
Pacify our life by the prayers of the Mother of God, as we cry unto Thee: O merciful Lord, glory to Thee![^886]
*On Tuesday Evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
Unceasingly pray Christ God, Who was crucified for us and destroyed the power of death, O Virgin Mother of God, that He may save our souls![^887]
*At the End of Matins:*
Deliver us, O Mother of God, from the sins that hold us fast, for we the faithful have no other hope save thee and God Who was born of thee.[^888]
*On Wednesday Evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
The fruit of thy womb, O all-pure one, is the fulfillment of the Prophets and the Law: therefore glorifying thee with understanding as the Mother of God, we piously magnify thee.[^889]
*At the End of Matins:*
The fruit of thy womb, O Bride of God, was shown forth as the mediatress of salvation unto men: therefore we the faithful, glorifying thee with mind and tongue as the Mother of God, magnify thee.[^890]
*On Thursday Evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
Christ God, Who was crucified for us: (*see above*).[^891]
*At the End of Matins:*
Deliver us, O Mother of God, from the sins that hold us fast: (*see above*).[^892]
*On Friday Evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
As the treasury of our resurrection, lead up from the pit and depth of transgressions those who hope in thee, O all-hymned one: for thou didst save those guilty of sin, having given birth unto our Salvation; who before childbearing wast a Virgin, and in childbearing a Virgin, and after childbearing again dost remain a Virgin.[^893]
*At the End of Matins:*
Rejoice, thou that didst contain within thy womb Him Who cannot be contained in the heavens! Rejoice, O Virgin, preaching of the Prophets, through whom Emmanuel shone forth! Rejoice, Mother of Christ God![^894]
*Tone 8*
*On Sunday Evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*
Rejoice, thou that through the angel didst receive the joy of the world! Rejoice, thou that didst bear thy Creator and Lord! Rejoice, thou that wast accounted worthy to be the Mother of God![^895]
*At the End of Matins:*
Rejoice, gate of the King of Glory, which the Most High alone passed through, and kept sealed alone, unto the salvation of our souls![^896]
*On Monday Evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*
The immovable confirmation of the faith, and the honorable gift of our souls, the Mother of God, let us the faithful magnify in hymns: Rejoice, thou that didst contain within thy womb the Rock of life! Rejoice, hope of the ends of the earth, protection of the sorrowing! Rejoice, unwedded Bride![^897]
*At the End of Matins:*
O all-pure Virgin, save us by thy prayers, stirring maternal compassion toward thy Son and our God.[^898]
*On Tuesday Evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*
Beholding upon the Cross the Lamb, and Shepherd, and Savior of the world, she that bare Thee said weeping: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance, but my maternal heart burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for all, my Son and my God![^899]
*At the End of Matins:*
Rejoice, gate of the King of Glory, which the Most High alone... (*see above*).[^900]

...passed through, and kept sealed alone, unto the salvation of our souls.[^901]

*On Wednesday Evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*  
O noetic gate of life, all-pure Mother of God: deliver from perils those who flee unto thee with faith, that we may glorify thy most holy childbearing, unto the salvation of our souls.[^902]

*At the End of Matins:*  
O all-pure Virgin, save us: (*see above*).[^903]

*On Thursday Evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*  
Beholding upon the Cross the Lamb, and Shepherd, and Savior of the world: (*see above*).[^904]

*At the End of Matins:*  
Rejoice, gate of the King of Glory: (*see above*).[^905]

*On Friday Evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*  
Who for our sake wast born of the Virgin, and didst endure crucifixion, O Good One, Who by death didst overthrow death, and reveal resurrection as God: despise not those whom Thou didst create with Thine own hand; show forth Thy love for mankind, O Merciful One; receive the Mother of God who bare Thee, who intercedes for us, and save, O our Savior, a despairing people![^906]

*At the End of Matins:*  
O all-pure Virgin, save us by thy prayers, stirring maternal compassion toward thy Son and our God.[^907]

*(Tailpiece — Decorative typographic lozenge ornament concluding the liturgical text).*

*(Flyleaf — Archival binding sheet)*[^908] *(Binding — Lower outer rear cover: Worn brown calfskin binding over pasteboard with mottled leather and scuffed edges; no printed or manuscript text present).*

# FOOTNOTES AND SCHOLARLY COMMENTARY



[^1]: The paste-down on the inside of the front board contains assorted nineteenth-century archival inscriptions, inventory numbers, and a typographical imprint of M. Dzikowski in Przemyśl (1870), indicating provenance in the library of the Greek Catholic Eparchy of Przemyśl.

[^2]: Sylvester Lubieniecki-Rudnytsky (Sylwester Lubieniecki-Rudnicki, c. 1708–1777), Basilian monk and Greek Catholic Bishop of Lutsk and Ostroh (1752–1777), was appointed Exarch of all Rus' by the Holy See.

[^3]: The initials "Ч. С. В. В." stand for the Order of Saint Basil the Great (*Чина Святаго Василїя Великаго*, Latin *Ordo Sancti Basilii Magni*, OSBM). The Pochaiv printing press was operated by the Basilian order from 1730 until 1831.

[^4]: The year of creation 7281 according to the Byzantine-Greek calculation (5508 BC) corresponds precisely to AD 1773.

[^5]: In accordance with Ruthenian printing tradition, the Table of Contents (*Оглавленїе*) provides the folio references for each category of the Common Services, followed by the appendices of Theotokia.

[^6]: The melodic model (*Podoben*) heading designates the common Tone 4 prosomoion *Зва́нный свы́ше* (*Called from on high*, Greek *Ὁ ἐξ ὑψίστου κληθείς*).

[^7]: The abbreviation *(И҆́мⷶ)* represents *И҆́мярекъ* (*Name*), the liturgical variable placeholder where the celebrant or reader inserts the specific name of the prophet being commemorated.

[^8]: The Cross-Theotokion (*Крестобогородиченъ*, Greek *Staurotheotokion*) is appointed for Wednesday and Friday services when a polyeleos or higher festal rank is not celebrated, recalling the lamentation of the Mother of God at the Foot of the Cross.

[^9]: Wisdom of Solomon 3:1–9, the classical First Reading (*Paremia*) common to martyrs, prophets, and monastic saints, setting forth the eternal preservation and glorification of the righteous in the hand of God.

[^10]: Wisdom of Solomon 5:15–6:3, the Second Reading (*Paremia*), depicting the final vindication of the righteous and the divine armor of righteousness against oppressors.

[^11]: Wisdom of Solomon 4:7–15, the Third Reading (*Paremia*), setting forth true wisdom and holiness as maturity of soul rather than mere number of years.

[^12]: The melodic model (*Podoben*) heading *Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва* (*When from the Tree*, Greek *Ὅτε ἐκ τοῦ ξύλου*) is the standard Tone 2 model used for both festal and common commemorations.

[^13]: Tone 3 model *Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства* (*The beauty of virginity*, Greek *Τὴν ὡραιότητα τῆς παρθενίας*).

[^14]: Tone 3 model *Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры* (*Of the divine faith*, Greek *Θείας πίστεως*).

[^15]: The Megalynarion (*Величанїе*) or Refrain (*Припѣвъ*) sung at the Polyeleos on feast days of a single prophet.

[^16]: Tone 6 model *Милосе́рдїя две́ри* (*The gates of compassion*, Greek *Τῆς εὐσπλαγχνίας τὴν πύλην*).

[^17]: Ode 1 Heirmos of Tone 6, *Я́кѡ по сꙋ́хꙋ пѣшеше́ствовавъ* (*When Israel traversed the deep on foot*, Greek *Ὡς ἐν ἠπείρῳ πεζεύσας*).

[^18]: Ode 3 Heirmos of Tone 6, *Нѣ́сть ст҃ъ, я́коже ты́* (*There is none holy like Thee*, Greek *Οὐκ ἔστιν ἅγιος ὡς σύ*).

[^19]: Ode 4 Heirmos of Tone 6, *Хрїсто́съ моя̀ си́ла* (*Christ is my power*, Greek *Χριστός μου δύναμις*).

[^20]: Ode 5 Heirmos of Tone 6, *Бо́жїимъ свѣ́томъ твои́мъ* (*With Thy divine light*, Greek *Τῷ θείῳ φέγγει σου*).

[^21]: Ode 6 Heirmos of Tone 6, *Жите́йское мо́ре* (*Beholding the sea of life*, Greek *Τοῦ βίου τὴν θάλασσαν*).

[^22]: Tone 4 model *Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь* (*Thou hast appeared today*, Greek *Ἐπεφάνης σήμερον*).

[^23]: Ode 7 Heirmos of Tone 6, *Росода́тельнꙋ ᲂу҆́бѡ пе́щь* (*An angel made the furnace shed dew*, Greek *Δροσοβόλον μὲν τὴν κάμινον*).

[^24]: Ode 8 Heirmos of Tone 6, *И҆з̾ пла́мене преподо́бнымъ ро́сꙋ* (*From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew*, Greek *Ἐκ φλογὸς τοῖς ὁσίοις*).

[^25]: Ode 9 Heirmos of Tone 6, *Бо́га человѣ́кѡмъ невозмо́жно ви́дѣти* (*It is not possible for men to behold God*, Greek *Θεὸν ἀνθρώποις ἰδεῖν ἀδύνατον*).

[^26]: Tone 8 model *Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ* (*O marvelous wonder*, Greek *Ὢ τοῦ παραδόξου θαύματος*).

[^27]: The liturgical readings for the Divine Liturgy of a single prophet provide pericopes from the Epistles (1 Corinthians 12:7–11 on spiritual gifts, Hebrews 11:33–40 on the heroic faith of the prophets, or James 5:10–20 on prophetic patience and prayer) and Gospels (Matthew 23:34–39 or Luke 11:47–12:1 on the righteous prophets).

[^28]: Tone 8 model *Что́ вы нарече́мъ* (*What shall we call you*, Greek *Τί ὑμᾶς καλέσωμεν*).

[^29]: Cross-Theotokion (*Крестобогородиченъ*), sung on Wednesday and Friday when no Polyeleos is appointed.

[^30]: 1 John 3:21–4:6, First Reading (*Paremia*) for an Apostle, instructing the faithful to test the spirits and discern the Spirit of Truth from the spirit of error.

[^31]: 1 John 4:11–16, Second Reading (*Paremia*) for an Apostle, setting forth mutual love as the dwelling of God within the Church.

[^32]: 1 John 4:20–5:5, the Third Reading (*Paremia*) for an Apostle, on fraternal love as the essential proof of love for God, and overcoming the world through faith in Christ Jesus.

[^33]: The melodic model (*Podoben*) heading designates the common Tone 4 prosomoion *Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе* (*Thou hast given a sign*, Greek *Ἔδωκας σημείωσιν*).

[^34]: The rubric directs the reader to the Resurrectional Theotokion of Tone 2 (*Ѡ҆ чꙋдесѐ но́вагѡ* / *O new wonder*), which is printed in the rear appendix of Resurrectional and Common Theotokia of the Eight Tones at folio 233 verso.

[^35]: The Cross-Theotokion (*Крестобогородиченъ*, Greek *Staurotheotokion*) is appointed for Wednesday and Friday services when a Polyeleos is not celebrated, recalling the maternal grief of the Mother of God at the Foot of the Cross.

[^36]: The common dismissal troparion for an Apostle, Tone 3 (*А҆́пⷭ҇ле ст҃ы́й* / *Holy Apostle*), appointed when no distinct proper troparion for the commemorated apostle is appointed in the Typikon.

[^37]: The Megalynarion (*Величанїе*) or Refrain (*Припѣвъ*) sung at the Polyeleos on the feast day of an Apostle, honoring his apostolic labors in preaching the Gospel.

[^38]: Tone 8 melodic model (*Podoben*) *Премꙋ́дрости и҆ ра́зꙋма* (*Of Wisdom and Understanding*, Greek *Τὴν σοφίαν καὶ λόγον*).

[^39]: Matthew pericope 34 (Matthew 9:36–10:8), the appointed Matins Gospel for an Apostle, recounting Christ's compassion for the multitude and His commissioning of the Twelve Apostles with power over unclean spirits and infirmities.

[^40]: The rubrical distribution for the Matins Canons: four troparia are sung from the canon of the Theotokos (drawn from the Oktoechos or the Theotokion appendix), and eight troparia are sung from the canon of the Apostle.

[^41]: Ode 1 Heirmos of Tone 8, *Пѣ́снь возсле́мъ лю́дїе* (*Let us send up a song, O ye peoples*, Greek *ᾎσμα ἀναπέμψωμεν λαοί*).

[^42]: Ode 3 Heirmos of Tone 8, *Нѣ́сть ст҃ъ ꙗ҆́кѡ Гдⷭ҇ь* (*There is none holy like the Lord*, Greek *Οὐκ ἔστιν ἅγιος ὡς ὁ Κύριος*).

[^43]: Tone 3 melodic model (*Podoben*) *Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры* (*Of the divine faith*, Greek *Θείας πίστεως*).

[^44]: The Cross-Theotokion (*Staurotheotokion*) appointed following the Kathisma/Sessional Hymn after Ode 3 when the service is sung without a Polyeleos on Wednesday or Friday.

[^45]: Ode 4 Heirmos of Tone 8, *И҆з̾ горы̀ приосѣне́нныя Сло́ве* (*From the mountain overshadowed, O Word*, Greek *Ἐξ ὄρους κατασκίου, Λόγε*).

[^46]: Ode 5 Heirmos of Tone 8, *Ѡ҆ но́щи невѣ́дѣнїя* (*Out of the night of ignorance*, Greek *Ἐκ νυκτὸς ἀγνοίας*).

[^47]: Ode 6 Heirmos of Tone 8, *Ри́зꙋ мнѣ̀ пода́ждь свѣ́тлꙋ* (*Grant me a robe of light*, Greek *Χιτῶνά μοι παράσχου φωτεινόν*).

[^48]: The proper kontakion of the commemorated apostle is taken from the Menaion when appointed in the Typikon; otherwise the general kontakion of Tone 4 (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ ѕвѣздꙋ̀ пресвѣ́тлꙋю* / *As a most radiant star*) is sung.

[^49]: Tone 4 melodic model (*Podoben*) *Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь* (*Thou hast appeared today*, Greek *Ἐπεφάνης σήμερον*).

[^50]: Ode 7 Heirmos of Tone 8, *Ѻ҆́троцы є҆вре́йстїи въ пещѝ* (*The Hebrew youths in the furnace*, Greek *Παῖδες Ἑβραίων ἐν καμίνῳ*).

[^51]: Ode 8 Heirmos of Tone 8, *Мꙋсікі́йскимъ ѻ҆рга́нѡмъ согласꙋ́ющимъ* (*While the musical instruments sounded in harmony*, Greek *Μουσικῶν ὀργάνων συμφωνούντων*).

[^52]: Ode 9 Heirmos of Tone 8, *Вои́стиннꙋ Бцⷢ҇у тя̀ и҆сповѣ́дꙋемъ* (*Truly we confess thee to be the Birthgiver of God*, Greek *Κυρίως Θεοτόκον*).

[^53]: Melodic model (*Podoben*) of the Exapostilarion, *Дх҃омъ прозря̀* (*Having seen in spirit*).

[^54]: Tone 4 melodic model (*Podoben*) *Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ въ мꙋ́ченицѣхъ* (*As one noble among the martyrs*, Greek *Ὡς γενναῖον ἐν μάρτυσιν*).

[^55]: 1 Corinthians pericope 131 (1 Corinthians 4:9–16), the appointed Divine Liturgy Epistle for an Apostle, depicting the apostolic calling as "fools for Christ's sake" and "the offscouring of all things."

[^56]: Tone 4 melodic model (*Podoben*) *Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ* (*As one noble*).

[^57]: The Cross-Theotokion (*Staurotheotokion*) for Wednesday and Friday at Vespers.

[^58]: Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone 8 (*Цр҃ь нбⷭ҇ный за человѣколю́бїе* / *The King of heaven, for His love of mankind*), found in the rear appendix at folio 235 recto.

[^59]: 1 Peter 1:1–9, the First Reading (*Paremia*) for Two and Many Apostles, on the incorruptible inheritance reserved in heaven and the trial of faith more precious than gold.

[^60]: 1 Peter 1:13–19, the Second Reading (*Paremia*) for Two and Many Apostles, exhorting the faithful to holiness of life and contemplation of their redemption by the precious blood of the unblemished Lamb of God.

[^61]: 1 Peter 2:11–24, the Third Reading (*Paremia*) for Two and Many Apostles, instructing Christians in civil order, patient endurance of suffering, and conformity to Christ's voluntary passion on the Cross.

[^62]: Tone 6 melodic model (*Podoben*) *Трїдне́внѡ воскре́слъ є҆сѝ Хрїстѐ* (*On the third day Thou didst rise, O Christ*, Greek *Τριήμερος ἀνέστης Χριστέ*).

[^63]: The Resurrectional Theotokion of Tone 4 (*При́зри на моле́нїа твои́хъ ра̑бъ* / *Look down upon the entreaties of thy servants*), found in the rear appendix of Resurrectional Theotokia at folio 234 recto.

[^64]: Cross-Theotokion (*Staurotheotokion*) appointed at the Aposticha for Wednesday and Friday.

[^67]: The Megalynarion (*Величанїе*) or Refrain (*Припѣвъ*) sung at the Polyeleos for Two and Many Apostles, extolling their global illumination of the nations.

[^68]: Tone 3 melodic model (*Podoben*) *Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры* (*Of the divine faith*, Greek *Θείας πίστεως*).

[^69]: Luke pericope 50 (Luke 10:1–15), the appointed Matins Gospel for the Apostles, recounting Christ's commissioning of the Seventy Disciples to preach the Kingdom of God.

[^70]: The rubrical distribution for the Matins Canons: four troparia are sung from the canon of the Theotokos and eight from the canon of the Apostles.

[^71]: Ode 1 Heirmos of Tone 4, *Мо́ря чермнꙋ́ю пꙋчи́нꙋ* (*Having traversed the deep of the Red Sea*, Greek *Ἄισομαί σοι Κύριε* / *Θαλάσσης τὸ ἐρυθραῖον πέλαγος*).

[^72]: Ode 3 Heirmos of Tone 4, *Весели́тся ѡ҆ тебѣ̀ цр҃ковь твоѧ̀ Хрⷭ҇тѐ* (*Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ*, Greek *Εὐφραίνεται ἐπὶ σοὶ ἡ Ἐκκλησία σου*).

[^73]: Tone 8 melodic model (*Podoben*) *Премꙋ́дрости и҆ ра́зꙋма* (*Of Wisdom and Understanding*, Greek *Τὴν σοφίαν καὶ λόγον*).

[^75]: Ode 4 Heirmos of Tone 4, *Вознесе́на тя̀ ви́дѣвши цр҃ковь* (*Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross*, Greek *Ἐπαρθέντα σε ἰδοῦσα ἡ Ἐκκλησία*).

[^76]: Ode 5 Heirmos of Tone 4, *Ты̀ Гдⷭ҇и мо́й свѣ́тъ въ мі́ръ прише́лъ є҆сѝ* (*Thou, O my Lord, didst come into the world as a light*, Greek *Σὺ Κύριέ μου φῶς εἰς τὸν κόσμον ἐλήλυθας*).

[^77]: Ode 6 Heirmos of Tone 4, *Пожрꙋ́ ти со гла́сомъ хвале́нїя Гдⷭ҇и* (*I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord*, Greek *Θύσω σοι, μετὰ φωνῆς αἰνέσεως Κύριε*).

[^78]: When the Menaion appoints a proper kontakion for the commemorated apostles, it is sung; otherwise the general kontakion of Tone 4 (*Ло́зїе ꙗ҆ви́стеся* / *Ye appeared as branches*) is used.

[^79]: The common dismissal kontakion for Two and Many Apostles, Tone 4 (*Ло́зїе ꙗ҆ви́стеся Хрⷭ҇то́ва вїногра́да мꙋ́дрїи* / *Ye appeared as branches of the vine of Christ, O wise ones*).

[^82]: Ode 9 Heirmos of Tone 4, *Ка́мень нерꙋкосѣ́чный* (*A cornerstone was cut out without hands*, Greek *Λίθος ἀχειρότμητος*).

[^84]: Tone 8 melodic model (*Podoben*) *Гдⷭ҇и а҆́ще и҆ на сꙋди́щи* (*O Lord, though Thou didst stand before the tribunal*, Greek *Κύριε, εἰ καὶ κριτηρίῳ παρέστης*).

[^85]: Epistle reading at the Divine Liturgy for Two and Many Apostles: 1 Corinthians, pericope 131 (1 Cor. 4:9–16).

[^86]: Gospel reading at the Divine Liturgy for Apostles: Luke, pericope 50 (Luke 10:1–15) or pericope 51 (Luke 10:16–21).

[^87]: Primary woodcut headpiece opening the Common Service to a Single Hierarch (*Слꙋ́жба ѻ́бща, Ст҃и́телю є҆ди́номꙋ*).

[^90]: Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone 8, *Цр҃ь нбⷭ҇ный за человѣколю́бїе на землѝ ꙗ҆ви́ся* (*The King of heaven, through His love for mankind, appeared upon earth*, Greek *Ὁ Βασιλεὺς τῶν οὐρανῶν*); cross-referenced to Tone 8 Theotokia on fol. 235r.

[^93]: Second Old Testament Paremia at Great Vespers for a Hierarch: a liturgical cento drawn from Proverbs 10:31–32, 11:7 and the Wisdom of Solomon 6:12–15, 7:30, 8:2–8.

[^96]: Cross-Theotokion (*Staurotheotokion*) for Wednesday/Friday Vespers, *Тѧ̀ ви́дѧщи пригвожда́ема на Крⷭ҇тѣ̀ І҆и҃се* (*Beholding Thee nailed to the Cross, O Jesus*).

[^736]: Tone 3 Monday evening at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: "O cloud of the unwaning Light..." (Свѣ́та незаходи́магѡ ѻ҆́блаче...).

[^737]: Tone 3 Monday evening at the Aposticha, Theotokion: "Among women, holy Mother of God, unwedded Mother..." (Въ жена́хъ, ст҃а́ѧ Бцⷣе...).

[^738]: Tone 3 Tuesday morning Theotokion: "Without seed thou didst conceive of the Holy Spirit..." (Без̾ сѣ́мене зачала̀ є҆сѝ...).

[^739]: Tone 3 Wednesday evening at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: "Great is the power of thy wonders, O pure one..." (Ве́лїа чꙋде́съ твои́хъ...).

[^740]: Tone 3 Wednesday evening at the Aposticha, Theotokion: "Snatch me, who have greatly sinned, O Maiden..." (Зѣльмѝ согрѣша́юща мѧ̀...).

[^741]: Tone 3 Saturday morning Theotokion: Identical formula as the Tuesday and Thursday morning Theotokia (Без̾ сѣ́мене зачала̀ є҆сѝ...).

[^742]: Tone 4 Sunday evening at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: "Surpassing the angelic hosts, O all-immaculate one..." (Превозше́дши чи́ны А҆́гг҃лскїѧ...).

[^743]: Tone 4 Sunday evening at the Aposticha, Theotokion: "Rejoice, cloud of light; rejoice, radiant lampstand..." (Ра́дꙋйся свѣ́та ѻ҆́блаче...), compiling Old Testament types of the Mother of God (the cloud of Exodus, the golden lampstand, the urn of manna, Aaron's rod, the burning bush).

[^744]: Tone 4 Monday morning Theotokion: "O Mother of God, Queen of all, boast of the Orthodox..." (Бцⷣе всѣ́хъ цр҃и́це...), an explicit anti-iconoclast troparion defending the veneration of icons.

[^745]: Tone 4 Monday evening at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: "Grant me tears from the heart, O Maiden..." (Сле́зы мѝ да́ждь ѿ се́рдца...).

[^746]: Tone 4 Monday evening at the Aposticha, Theotokion: "Deliver us from our necessities, O Mother of Christ God..." (И҆зба́ви на́съ ѿ нꙋ́ждъ на́шихъ...).

[^747]: Tone 4 Tuesday morning Theotokion: "Preserve thy servants from all dangers, O blessed Mother of God..." (Ѿ всѣ́хъ бѣ́дъ рабы̑ твоѧ̑ сохранѧ́й...).

[^748]: Tone 4 Wednesday evening at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: "O thou that didst contain in thy womb the uncontainable God..." (Ꙗ҆́же Бг҃а невмѣсти́маго...).

[^749]: Tone 4 Wednesday evening at the Aposticha, Theotokion: "Having thee, O Mother of God, as our hope and protection..." (И҆мꙋ́ще тѧ̀ Бцⷣе ᲂу҆пова́нїе...).

[^750]: Tone 4 Thursday morning Theotokion: "We have acquired thee as a wall, O all-pure Mother of God..." (Тѧ̀ стѣ́нꙋ стѧжа́хомъ...).

[^751]: Tone 4 Friday evening at the Aposticha, Theotokion: "Unquenchable lamp, righteous throne, O all-pure Sovereign Lady..." (Свѣщѐ негаси́маѧ...).

[^752]: Tone 4 Saturday morning Theotokion: "O only pure and all-immaculate Virgin, who didst bear God without seed..." (Є҆ди́на чⷭ҇таѧ...).

[^753]: Tone 5 Sunday evening at "Lord, I Have Cried", Theotokion: "Truly wast thou the cherubic throne, as being higher than the angels..." (Прⷭ҇то́лъ херꙋві́мскїй вои́стиннꙋ была̀ є҆сѝ...).

[^754]: Concluding line of the Sunday evening sticheron at "Lord, I Have Cried" in Tone 5, carried over across the quire boundary from Fol. 130r.
